Two fixes, both measured before/after in that same distro: every check now starts as `setsid --wait timeout -k 10 <budget>` — **no controlling terminal** (a password prompt fails fast instead of stopping the world) and a deadline that can **KILL** (rc 137 is printed as TIMEOUT, so a killed check is NAMED; no grandchildren survive, a self-stopping `kill -STOP $$` still returns rc=124 at its budget, a tty read fails immediately, and a host without util-linux `setsid` falls back to a documented weaker runner) — and every EXECUTED `sudo` in the gate is `sudo -n` (60 call sites across 11 check scripts), so a check that needs root either works or reports SKIP/FAIL at once, and an operator running the gate in a real terminal cannot be left waiting for a password prompt either. Live after-effect: the check that hung for 54 minutes now answers `SKIP: docker daemon not reachable …` in **0.1 s**. The contract's detector ignores comments, heredoc operator instructions and quoted message text, and **self-tests against a planted violation**, so "no violations found" can never mean "the detector broke". 13 contracts in `scripts/check_b299_catalog_cannot_hang.sh`; depth in `docs/LESSONS.md` L-48.

- **B296** — (2026-09-23) **the relay probe address is editable from the panel, and applying it needs no container recreate.** B289.1 taught every DERP probe to dial the operator's address (`SKYGATE_DERP_PROBE_HOST`) while still speaking the relay's public hostname as TLS SNI, and `deploy/deploy.sh` writes that variable for the operator — but the skygate container is created from `env_file: .env`, and Docker freezes that environment at container **creation**: `docker compose restart` re-runs the entrypoint and keeps the old environment, so applying an edited value meant `docker compose up --force-recreate` (AGENTS trap #3) over SSH, for something the operator can see is wrong right on `/admin/derp/relays` («пропущен: unreachable (probed …)»). The knob now has two layers, resolved **per probe** (never once at boot) by the new `internal/derpcfg`: `global_settings.derp.probe_host` (what the new card writes) **>** `.env SKYGATE_DERP_PROBE_HOST` **>** none — and every probe path reads it: the derpmap reachability guard, the `/admin/derp` status probes, the STUN candidate list and the `derp_health` cron (`DERPInfo.ProbeHost`, filled once by `FetchOwnDERPs`; `dialTargetFor` keeps the `.env` fallback for hand-built rows). No non-test code reads the environment variable directly any more, so a saved address cannot be silently ignored on one path. The card shows the effective value **and which layer it came from** (`db`/`env`/`default`), accepts a bare address (scheme, port, path, list, a typo'd IPv4 and a non-hostname are each refused with their own translated reason — the port belongs to the relay row's URL), drops the 30s map-verdict cache so the redirect renders the fresh «в карте / пропущен» verdict, and writes an audit row. 25 contracts in `scripts/check_b296_derp_probe_host_ui.sh` + `internal/derpcfg/derpcfg_b296_test.go` + `internal/feature/admin/derp_probe_host_b296_test.go` (which saves **through the handler** and re-fetches `/admin/derp/relays/derpmap.json`: without an address the unresolvable relay is dropped, after saving it is published under its public name, after clearing it is dropped again). Procedure in `docs/derp.md` §B296.

- **B326.1** — (2026-09-25) **forms fit a phone, and a select has a name.** The B326 release fixed the tables; the operator report also named forms and dropdowns, and the audit (docs/responsive-audit.md) found why: several forms carry an **inline** display:grid;grid-template-columns:repeat(2|3,1fr) (dmin/cluster.html x2, dmin/exit_rules.html), which no class-based media query can reach because the column definition lives in a style attribute — on a 360px screen those fields were squeezed into two or three columns. One attribute-selector rule now collapses every inline fixed-column grid to one column under the mobile breakpoint, and inline min-width on a control cannot exceed its card. The four unlabelled <select name=relay> in dmin/exit_nodes.html (per-prefix owner picker) and the ccept_routes picker got an ria-label reusing the existing column heading «Закрепить за» / form label, so a screen reader no longer announces an unnamed combobox. The B326 check grew to 16 contracts, including a ratchet: selects without an accessible label are frozen at 31 of 41 (was 36) and may only go down.
- **B332** — (2026-10-01) **a headscale test must not depend on the host running headscale.** A baseline catalog run on the reference VM reported two failures in `internal/headscale` — `TestGetACLNamesEveryRungInTheError_B294` (*"with no API, no file and no CLI the read must fail loudly"*, 2.79 s) and `TestGetACLAPIFailsNoContainer` (*"expected error for 500 + no CLI, got nil"*, 0.41 s) — and **both were environment failures, not regressions**: each asserted a *failure* path while assuming the other two rungs of `GetACL()` were unreachable, and on the VM they are reachable (the `headscale` container is running, `docker` is on `PATH`, and the host's policy file is discoverable), so `GetACL()` returned the **live policy**. Two defects, not one. **(1) The code made the CLI rung impossible to disable.** `runHeadscaleCLI` replaced an empty `ExecContainer` with the literal container name `headscale`, while `tags.go`, `preauth.go` and `nodes.go` all read `ExecContainer == ""` as *"the CLI path is not configured"* and `headscale_test.go` documented `c.ExecContainer = ""` as **"disable CLI fallback"**. Empty now means what everyone already assumed — no container — while still trying the local binary (the native-install path); `New()` always sets a non-empty value (`HEADSCALE_CONTAINER`, default `headscale`), so production behaviour is unchanged. **(2) The tests were not hermetic — and the fix is NOT `t.Skip`:** a skipped test proves nothing on the host that matters. `isolateHeadscaleFallbacks` makes the premise **true on any host** by setting `PATH` to an empty temp dir (so neither `docker` nor `headscale` resolves) and pointing `SKYGATE_HEADSCALE_POLICY_PATH` / `SKYGATE_HEADSCALE_CONFIG` at absent files. Verified on the reference VM: the two tests — and the whole `internal/headscale` package (6.5 s) — pass there now, with the assertions kept. The new semantics are pinned by `TestB332_EmptyExecContainerNeverUsesDocker` and `TestB332_ConfiguredContainerDoesUseDocker`, so "make the test pass" cannot be achieved by disabling the fallback altogether. 20 contracts in `scripts/check_b332_headscale_fallback_isolation.sh`.
- **B392** — (2026-10-16, operator request) **the notification panel must read, scale and be reachable.** Operator report: «с оповещением в панели вопросы так как там по отображению проблемы с наплывом … и особенно чтобы они не перекрывались вкладкой — сейчас при нажатии всплывающее окно перекрывается вкладкой». Reading the code produced **six measured causes, not one**. **(1) The dropdown was CLIPPED, not mis-stacked:** `.sidebar` carried `overflow:hidden` (`themes.css:227`) and the bell panel is an absolutely-positioned child, so its clip box cut the panel at the sidebar's own width — the panel asks for up to 340px, starts ~8px in, and the content column begins exactly at 220px, so **~38 % was clipped** (in the 52px collapsed state only a ~44px sliver survived). The `z-index` was **already the highest in the sidebar** (200), which is why "raise the z-index" would not have helped; and `.theme-picker-sb .menu` (specificity 0,2,0) overrode `.notif-menu` (0,1,0), so the declared `min-width:280px` never applied and the real floor was 140px. Fixed: the sidebar no longer clips (nothing depended on it — the nav has its own scroller, the collapsed state hides elements rather than clipping them), and the panel has a scoped rule with viewport-clamped width and a `60vh` height so its top edge cannot end up out of reach. **(2) The full list was UNREACHABLE:** `/my/notifications` has existed since B157.1 with **no sidebar entry** and no link from the bell, the dropdown showed at most **50** rows with no hint that more existed, and the badge counted every unread row — so 300 unread displayed as "300" while only 50 were readable. The sidebar links to it now, the bell says "…and N more unread" and offers the full list, and each row carries its own timestamp (the dropdown had none). **(3) The page LIED about its own size:** the handler loaded a capped slice into memory and sliced it while the db helpers **silently rewrote any limit > 100 to 50** — so "N total" could never exceed 50 and every page past the second rendered the "you have no notifications" empty state **while the rows were still in the table**. Pagination, the real total and a severity filter are now **SQL conditions** (optional predicates rather than one query per combination, so the page, the count and "has next" cannot disagree — `$2 = 0` = all rows, `$3 = ''` = any severity), the silent limit rewrite is gone in favour of named bounds (`defaultNotificationLimit`/`maxNotificationLimit`), the empty state now distinguishes "no notifications at all" from "this filter matches nothing", and a failed read renders the page with a **named banner** instead of a `text/plain` error page (the error-leak ratchet stays at its baseline). **(4) The admin inbox had NO badge:** `CountOpenMonitorEventsNotLowerThan` existed with a doc comment saying it was "the number the nav badge shows" and **was never called from production code**. **(5) Time was ENGLISH-ONLY:** `notifications.TimeAgo` hardcoded "2 h ago" while the `notif.time_*` keys sat unused in **both** catalogues; it takes the language now, with the missing week/month units added. **(6) There are TWO `themes.css` files and they had drifted 65 lines apart:** the runtime serves the **embedded** `internal/staticfs/static/css/themes.css` while **every Go CSS test and four shell contracts** (B105/B106/B107/B157) read the **root mirror** `static/css/themes.css` — so a fix applied to one side leaves the gate green and the panel broken. The mirror is synced **and** the contract fails if the two ever diverge again. 30 contracts in `scripts/check_b392_notifications_panel.sh`.
- **B423** — (2026-10-25) **два хронических состояния, которые нашло расследование, никогда не доходили до Telegram.** `internal/watchalert` с двумя **чистыми** политиками: **(1) автообнаружение** — алерт на **ONSET** (первый сбой после успеха), эскалация не чаще раза в окно (по умолчанию 6 ч, `SKYGATE_DISCOVERY_ALERT_HOURS`), пока сбой держится, и однократное объявление **восстановления** (журнал может писать каждый час — телефон оператора не должен); **(2) всплески логинов** — скользящее окно (по умолчанию 10 мин, `SKYGATE_LOGIN_ALERT_WINDOW_MIN`), **один** алерт на cooldown (по умолчанию 1 ч, `SKYGATE_LOGIN_ALERT_COOLDOWN_MIN`) при переходе порога (по умолчанию 10, `SKYGATE_LOGIN_ALERT_THRESHOLD`), с числом попыток и **различными источниками** (порт отбрасывается: один атакующий — один источник) и **никогда** с паролем или списком логинов. Оба идут через композер (i18n-заголовок + факты + dedup), то есть видны в ledger и `/ack`. 23 контракта в `scripts/check_b423_threshold_alerts.sh`
- **B422** — (2026-10-25) **«тишина по делу» и «тишина из-за поломки» должны быть различимы, и получатель не должен зависеть от памяти оператора.** The 2026-10-25 investigation of the operator's install (run with the new `scripts/diagnose_alerts.sh`) found a verdict no surface had shown for months: `bot_token` present (46 chars), **`telegram.chat_id` ABSENT**, one bound chat — and it is an **ADMIN** binding — plus **3082** `tag.autoupdate_failed` rows and 5 failed `exit_node_provision` runs in `audit_log`. Alerts required `chat_id`; the bot had been configured by **binding**; the manual test arrived (reply path + binding) while **every alert was dropped silently** (pre-B421 `return 0`, no log line). The app even printed «bot token set (no chat_id yet — receive-only)» at every boot and nobody read that as a problem, because it is a **feature-shaped** sentence. B421 fixed delivery; B422 closes what the investigation exposed: **(1)** `/admin/telegram` shows the admin chat alerts are *already* using and offers **one click** (`action=adopt_chat`) to persist it as `telegram.chat_id`, guarded so it never proposes a member's chat (alerts name hosts, IPs and certificate subjects) and never overwrites the operator's own choice, audited as `telegram_chat_id_adopted`; **(2)** `internal/alertcanary` sends ONE short message per interval through the **same** alert path (delivered / failed / recipient) — policy as a **pure function** (no recipient / firstDelay after boot / interval elapsed / disabled), last send persisted in `global_settings.alerts.canary.last_at` so a restart does not repeat, a failed send **not** recorded so one blip cannot silence a whole interval, `SKYGATE_ALERT_CANARY_HOURS=0` as the off switch; **(3)** the boot line naming the alert recipient is now the **FIRST statement** of the scheduler wiring (on the live install the previous placement was in the binary yet absent from the journal — never ship a claim you have not seen); **(4)** `scripts/diagnose_alerts.sh` is the repeatable investigation: read-only, docker and native, PostgreSQL and SQLite, no secret printed, answering "what runs / who is the recipient / what was delivered / which alert-worthy events happened" with a verdict. 39 contracts in `scripts/check_b422_alert_coverage.sh`; **recorded follow-up, not silently dropped:** threshold alerts for `cluster.discovery.error` (13310 rows) and `login_fail` bursts are NOT in this block
- **B421** — (2026-10-25) **a notification must either arrive or name the reason it did not — and the test button must test the path the notifications use.** Operator: «если уже должны были прийти в телеграмм уведомления, то они не приходили». Reading the code found a defect that produces exactly that, silently: **alerts and replies resolved their RECIPIENT differently.** `/admin/telegram`'s «Отправить тест» sends a **reply** (`SendTelegramErr`), which falls back to a chat bound with `/start` + `[Bind]` even when `global_settings.telegram.chat_id` is empty — that fallback is deliberate. Notifications went through `sendAlert`, which asked `db.LoadTelegramSendTarget` (token **and** chat_id) and did `if err != nil || !ok { return 0 }` — **no log line, no counter, no audit row**. On an installation configured by *binding* a chat, the bot answered commands, the test said «отправлено», and **every alert was dropped**. Two rules follow: a tester that exercises a different path than the product proves the wrong thing (B390's lesson, one level up), and an outcome nobody records cannot be investigated. Fixed with **one recipient rule** (`resolveAlertTarget`: global token+chat_id → an **ADMIN** binding → nothing, named — a member's chat is never used, because alerts name hostnames, IPs and certificate subjects), a **ledger** of six named outcomes (`sent` / `suppressed_dedup` / `not_configured_no_token` / `not_configured_no_chat` / `chat_id_invalid` / `send_failed`) with counters, the last outcome with its reason, the **masked** target and the transport that carried the last delivery (`direct` or `relay:<host>`), **rate-limited logging** (first occurrence always logs, repeats at most once per 10 min per outcome+reason), and the ledger **unconditionally** rendered on `/admin/telegram` as its own card, so «почему не пришло» is answerable without SSH. A new «Отправить тестовое уведомление» button walks the **alert** path (`SendAlertEvent` → resolution → HTML transport), never sets a `DedupKey` (a test must always attempt a real send), and reports the outcome in the flash + an audit row `telegram_alert_test`; the reply test stays, honestly labelled as a different thing. 38 contracts in `scripts/check_b421_alert_delivery.sh`, behaviour tests driving the real `sendAlert` against a fake Bot API (delivery; a 400 recorded as a failure with the token redacted; the admin-binding fallback end to end; the legacy **70**-call-site path reaching the same transport), and `docs/TELEGRAM.md` §11 with the outcome vocabulary and the list of events that MUST notify
- **B420** — (2026-10-25, TD-27) **the dialect leak guard must see the whole statement, not one line of it — and the leaks it could not see had to be fixed, not exempted.** The guard written on 2026-09-18 inspected only lines that **literally contained a backtick**, and this repo formats every statement as a **multi-line** backtick literal whose backticks sit on the first and last line only, so every token in between was invisible; it also knew exactly **two** tokens and skipped all of `internal/db/migrations_*` wholesale. Widened to scan whole string literals (a small Go lexer that strips comments), **548 shared files + 23 migration files**, it found **11 real leaks in 7 shared files** plus **2** statements inside the PostgreSQL migration chain that leaned on the `strftime()` compatibility shim. Every one was broken on at least one backend and most on **both**, because `created_at` / `updated_at` are **INTEGER epoch** columns in both chains: `now()` is a type error on PostgreSQL (*column is of type integer but expression is of type timestamp with time zone*) **and** an unknown function on SQLite; `EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM created_at)::bigint` over a column that is **already** an epoch is a type error on PostgreSQL and a syntax error on SQLite, so `/admin/certificates` and `/admin/deploy` rendered an **empty event table on every install**; `pg_database_size(current_database())` left `skygate_db_size_bytes` at **0** on SQLite. All failed **silently** (empty page, missing audit row, zero gauge), which is how they survived. Fixed at the source: `db.NowUnixSQL()` for the 7 epoch writes (`skygate ha promote`/`demote` silently failed on **both** backends before this — `writeApplyActiveRole` returned the error and never stored the role), the raw epoch column for the 2 readers (which now **log** a failure instead of returning nil), and a dialect branch (`page_count × page_size`, the B271 pair) for the size probe; the 2 PG migration defaults use `EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM now())::bigint` instead of the shim. The widened guard enforces **16 PostgreSQL-only + 13 SQLite-only** tokens, forbids a one-dialect literal in a file that never asks which dialect is live (`dialectAwareMarkers`), classifies every migration literal by the **chain function that owns it** (hard zero — no blanket skip), and skips prose via a SQL-likeness gate, because SQLite accepts **arbitrary type names**: measured in `internal/db/dialect_type_name_b420_test.go`, `TEXT[]` round-trips a JSON array unchanged (not a defect) while `SERIAL PRIMARY KEY` is accepted but **inert** and leaves the id `NULL` (still a defect). The one escape hatch is a **pinned** map of PostgreSQL-only files whose own header must carry `// dialect:postgres-only` and whose reason must be recorded. 31 contracts in `scripts/check_b420_dialect_leaks.sh`, with behaviour tests running the **real** statements on a **real** SQLite database and pinning that the **old** shapes still fail there
- **B419** — (2026-10-25, TD-30) **a per-test database must not pay for durability it does not need.** TD-30 carried `go test ./internal/feature/exit_rules` at **214 s** on the reference VM and blamed the per-test migration chain. Measured instead of assumed: one full SQLite chain costs **536-880 ms** with the default pragmas, `synchronous(0)` alone 561 ms, `journal_mode(memory)` alone 1151 ms, and **BOTH 53 ms** — the cost is **fsync**, hundreds of tiny DDL commits, so neither pragma helps alone (each commit still fsyncs once) and together they remove both. `db.OpenFastSQLiteForTest` (`internal/db/testhelpers.go`, the same non-test-file pattern as `internal/telegram/test_helpers.go`) opens such a handle, and every per-test database in the tree now goes through it — `exit_rules` **72 s → 8.6 s** on this workstation, `internal/db`/`acl`/`prefixowner`/`update`/`cluster`/`diskmon`/`telegram` re-measured green after the switch. The **schema still comes from `MigrateSQLite`** — the same chain production runs, never a hand-written one — so the speed costs no coverage. The guards are behavioural and **same-binary**: one test reads `PRAGMA synchronous`/`journal_mode` back from the live connection (a DSN string match would pass on a DSN the driver ignores), the other times both handles and requires the fast one to be **never slower** — the first CI run taught that a required **multiplier** is decided by the filesystem (with `-race` on a tmpfs runner the same pair measures **1.4x**, on a disk **16x**), i.e. trap #15 applied to the guard itself. 11 contracts in `scripts/check_b419_fast_test_db.sh`, including a ratchet at **zero** default-path per-test opens and a ban on the helper appearing in production code
- **B391** — (2026-10-25, TD-34) **one i18n-aware alert composer, and the disk signal in the process that can deliver it.** Measured: all **61** outgoing triggers built their text with `fmt.Sprintf` at the call site, in English only, and sent it through the **reply** transport — no `parse_mode`, body wrapped in a literal triple-backtick fence — so an alert quoting a command arrived with the backticks showing, and a body over Telegram's 4096-character limit was refused outright and **lost**. Separately the around-the-clock disk check was the host cron `scripts/monitor_disk.sh`, which could not deliver at all: it needed the bot token in the **cron** environment (the token lives in the DB) and the host cannot reach `api.telegram.org`, while the container sees the same filesystem and has the relay tunnel. **Fixed:** `internal/telegram/alert_compose.go` — one severity vocabulary (`info`/`warn`/`critical`/`recovery`, one glyph each), an i18n title from the catalogue, **escaped** facts, a `<pre>` details block that is trimmed **first** and says how much was dropped, and a hard body budget; `Alert` also carries `DedupKey`/`DedupTTL` so a flapping component cannot flood the chat. The transport is `postMessageErr(…, "HTML")` (sanitised at the wire by B389's helper) and never the fenced reply path; the `Notifier` gained `SendAlertHTML` + `SendAlertEvent` (Noop included). The disk check moved to **`internal/diskmon`**: every 10 min, the filesystem holding the **database**, the same 85 %/95 % thresholds, an alert only on a **state transition** plus a bounded repeat while a level holds, with the level and the last alert time persisted in `global_settings` (`monitor.disk.level` / `.last_alert_at`) so a restart does not re-announce; the decision half is pure (`ClassifyDisk` / `nextDiskAction`), statfs is unix-only with a **windows stub that reports a named limitation** instead of a fabricated percentage, and a read failure is reported as a fact about the **check**. `scripts/monitor_disk.sh` is now a diagnostic (`SKYGATE_LEGACY_DISK_ALERT=1` opts back into delivery) and parses `df` by **shape** — the old column index printed `39385708%` on MSYS. The **34** remaining English-only alert sites are a **ratchet** in the contract that can only go down. 24 contracts in `scripts/check_b391_alert_composer.sh`, with the behavioural tests inside it (`alert_compose_b391_test.go`, `internal/diskmon/disk_monitor_b391_test.go`: escaping, truncation with marker, `parse_mode`, no fence, dedup, transitions, restart persistence, read-failure wording)
- **B390** — (2026-10-25, TD-33) **the bot must not state a value it does not have.** An audit of every Telegram surface (40 commands, 5 inline callbacks, 4 gate replies, 61 notification triggers) found six replies answering with a value nobody had measured, and one language defect. **H1:** `/exit_nodes` and `/myexitnodes` labelled **every** relay `offline` — they read `SELECT … FROM devices`, and that table has **no writer anywhere in the repository** (its only `INSERT` was a test fixture removed in v1.3.0), so the map stayed empty and `online == 0` was read as "not online"; this is the B273 incident class, on the two commands B273 did not touch. The status now comes from `exit_node_health` — the monitor snapshot `/admin/exit-nodes` renders — through one helper (`exitNodeStatusFor`) that maps B273's whole ladder (*online / untagged / degraded / offline*) and answers **«нет данных»** for a relay the snapshot does not describe: an absent value is not a measured zero, and an unreadable snapshot is reported as a fact about the READ. **H2:** `/my_status` printed `devices: 0` when the read **failed** (the error was discarded in both the legacy and the rich path; B186.3 had only pinned that the call existed) — both paths share `deviceCountForUser` and render an unavailable marker plus the reason. **H3:** `/version` printed a hand-maintained constant `dbSchemaVersion = "v0.32"` against a live **v0.81** schema, and `/help` recommended it as the post-deploy check; the level is now read from `applied_migrations` (`db.LatestAppliedMigrationVersion`), with "no migration applied yet" and "n/a (reason)" as the honest fallbacks. **H4:** `/mysubnet share|revoke` answered «ACL пере-применён» while a failed re-apply existed only in the log — and a **nil headscale client** (read-only deploy, corrupt per-plane key) **panicked** inside `acl.ApplyGeneratedPolicy`, which dereferenced the nil receiver; the replies name the failure (plus an audit row) and the pipeline refuses the nil client the way `ApplyACLForAllPlanes` already did. **H5:** `/admin/telegram` → «Send test» always reported «Сообщение отправлено (N шт.)» because `SendTelegram` returns nothing and the handler counted its own loop iterations; the `Notifier` gained `SendTelegramErr` / `SendTelegramToChatErr`, `NoopNotifier` refuses with `ErrTelegramNotConfigured`, `postToChatErr` carries Telegram's own `description`, and the handler counts deliveries (`sendTestMessages` / `testFlashMessage`, with a «НЕ отправлено» wording). **H6:** `/delrule` and `/clearrules` counted **iterations**, not deletions (`deleted++` outside any error check), so a failed DELETE, an already-gone row and somebody else's rule were all reported — and audited — as deleted; `db.DeleteRuleForUserRows` reports rows affected and one shared `deleteRuleJobs` counts real work, reports a zero-row DELETE as "nothing happened" and keeps the cascade non-negative. **Plus:** `/help <cmd>` was a 150-line switch of hardcoded **English** while 23 `bot.help_detail.*` translations sat unused in both catalogues — the reference now comes from the catalogue in the caller's language (the two missing commands, `exit_nodes_health` and `sync_nodes`, were added). 27 contracts in `scripts/check_b390_bot_truth.sh`, and the **behavioural** tests live inside it (the snapshot and the `devices` table are made to DISAGREE on purpose, so a reply that reads the writerless table flips the answer): `internal/telegram/bot_truth_b390_test.go` (7), `notify_truth_b390_test.go` (4), `internal/feature/admin/telegram_actions_b390_test.go` (3)
- **B418** — (2026-10-25, operator with a phone screenshot of `/admin/update`: «на экране смартфона меню в раскрытом состоянии перекрывает футтер, и также на смартфоне кнопки в меню в самом низу (смена темы языка выход из системы) появляются только если пролистать страницу в самый низ») **the mobile drawer must be the VISIBLE viewport, and it must own the footer it covers.** Two causes measured in the `@media (max-width:768px)` block. (1) The drawer kept `height:100vh`, and on a phone `100vh` is the **large** viewport (browser UI hidden), so `.sidebar-user` — theme / language / logout — was painted **below the visible edge**; the drawer is `position:fixed`, so scrolling the page could not lift it into view until the URL bar collapsed, which is exactly «только если пролистать в самый низ». (2) The dim layer was `main::before`, a pseudo-element of `<main>`, while `<footer>` is a **sibling** of `<main>`, so the footer stayed under the finger, a tap on the dim area could not close the drawer, and the open menu sat on a still-live footer. Fixed: the drawer is anchored `top:0;bottom:0;height:auto` (+ `max-height:100dvh`, `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` padding, and deliberately **no** `overflow:hidden` — that clip would cut the bell / theme / language popovers at the drawer's 280px edge, the B392 defect), the nav is the only scroller (`flex:1` + **`min-height:0`**, without which a flex item refuses to shrink and pushes the bottom block out) with a shrink-proof `.sidebar-user`, and `main::before` is replaced by a real `<label for="sidebar-toggle" class="sidebar-backdrop">` that covers the whole viewport **including the footer**, closes the drawer on tap and blocks the page behind it (`body:has(…)` scroll lock). 21 contracts in `scripts/check_b418_mobile_drawer.sh` (A4 REJECTS a clipping drawer and strips CSS comments first, so the rule's own banner cannot satisfy it; D3 requires the stylesheet URL to carry `?v={{.Version}}`, because an unversioned `themes.css` is cacheable for a day and a UI fix would then arrive a day late) + 5 Go pins in `internal/handlers/mobile_drawer_b418_test.go` that read the real stylesheet and the real layout template, so a rule that brings back `height:100vh` on the drawer, clips its popovers or drops the version query fails the gate instead of the operator's phone
- **B417** — (2026-10-24, operator: «Нажимать не буду надо отработать механизм полностью от и до … формирование из vps exit node за skygate и процесс должен быть отлажен без ошибок … если происходит не штатное поведение то уведомить пользователя») **skygate finishes what it started — within the scope the operator approved.** After B416's run lost its last step to a 502 the relay sat there advertising `0.0.0.0/0` with the route unapproved and the page answered with a red banner and a button. `internal/feature/admin/exit_nodes_reconcile_b417.go` runs a **2-minute** pass started from the schedulers phase (`cmd/skygate/main_schedulers.go`, beside the other five background schedulers): for every row in `exit_servers` (i.e. a relay skygate itself registered) whose headscale node is **online**, advertises a base route and has it **unapproved**, it calls the same `ApproveRoutesForNodeID(['0.0.0.0/0','::/0'])` the manual button uses, invalidates the cache, writes an `exit_node_base_route_autorepair` audit row and announces it through the notifier. The scope is a **pure decision function** (`baseRouteRepairDecision`) that refuses an offline node, a foreign hostname, a node with no `exit_servers` row, a node advertising only a subnet and an already-approved node — **subnet routes and relays skygate did not register are NEVER touched** (the operator chose this scope explicitly). Failure logging is rate-limited to once per relay per 30 min so a permanently broken relay cannot flood the journal. 25 contracts in `scripts/check_b417_base_route_reconciler.sh` (incl. the behavioural decision test)
- **B416** — (2026-10-24, operator: «страница provision выдала 502 bad gateway но после обновления пришла такая ошибка» + the red «маршрут не одобрен» banner) **a provisioning run is a JOB, not a request.** Measured: the POST held the connection for the whole run (install tailscale + `up` + wait, up to 3 min), the reverse proxy gave up at ~60 s with **502**, the node appeared in headscale anyway, the node wait was a **fixed 45 s** that ended a run whose ssh half had succeeded, and the only record of the outcome was a one-shot flash destroyed by the 502. New `internal/exitnode/job_b416.go`: the `Job` (status `running`/`ok`/`failed`, the five named steps `ssh-key → remote-setup → headscale-node → exit-server-row → approve-routes`, the form values for a retry, `Attempt`, `ActorID`) and `StepHint` — the per-step «что проверить» that turns a step id into an action. `internal/feature/admin/exit_nodes_provision_b416.go` persists the job to `global_settings` **before** the browser is answered (no migration: one run at a time is already `provisionMu`'s rule), runs the pipeline in a goroutine that owns `provisionMu`, records each step **before** running it, waits for the node in **rounds up to 150 s** instead of one 45 s budget, and audits + **notifies the operator** on success and on failure naming the step. The page renders the job (running with auto-refresh, «остановился на шаге N» with the reason, or done) plus **«Повторить»** (`POST /admin/exit-nodes/provision/retry`, stored form values, fresh preauth key), so a 502, a reload or a restart cannot hide where the run stands. 30 contracts in `scripts/check_b416_provision_job.sh` + 14 unit tests
- **B415** — (2026-10-24, operator: «не получилось подключить ssh ключ в файл добавил на vps», screenshot of the red banner: `ssh root@<host>: exit status 255: Warning: Permanently added <host> (ED25519) to the list of known hosts.`) **ssh's first stderr line is a banner, and the card could show a key that is not the key the panel uses.** **(1)** `internal/exitnode.Run` kept only the FIRST non-empty line of ssh's output, and that line is *always* the harmless known-hosts warning, while `Permission denied (publickey).` comes after it — so every authentication failure was reported as a successful connection and "never added", "added to the wrong user's authorized_keys" and "CRLF broke the line" were indistinguishable. New `internal/exitnode/ssh_diag_b415.go`: `SignificantSSHLines` (drops banners/debug noise, keeps ≤4 diagnostic lines, still prints the fallback), `ParseSSHDiagnosis` (auth / keyfile / network / hostkey, harvesting the offered `SHA256:…` and the server's accepted methods), `SSHFailureAdvice` (the actionable sentence: the account, the key fingerprint, `ssh-keygen -lf ~/.ssh/authorized_keys`, `chmod 700`/`600`, the `$'\\r'` CRLF check, `PermitRootLogin`, and the passphrase/BatchMode case), `PublicKeyFingerprint` (byte-identical to `ssh-keygen -lf`, with an OpenSSH wire-type gate so a random two-word sentence cannot produce a plausible fingerprint). **(2)** `sshPublicKeyFor` read a `.pub` SIBLING and stopped there, probing several candidate paths, so a stale `.pub` (regenerated private key) or another path's key was rendered with a copy button + authorise command — authorising the wrong key fails *exactly* like the screenshot. The public half is now DERIVED from the private key (`ssh-keygen -y -f`, bounded by `derivePublicKeyTimeout` = 2 s so a passphrase-protected key cannot hang a page render), the `.pub` is only a fallback, a mismatch is surfaced as `PubKeyStale` with the recreate command, the fingerprint is rendered for comparison, `Request.KeyFingerprint` carries it into the run so the failure names the rejected key, and the authorise command states the account it must run as (the SSH-user field is mirrored live — `~` in the command means *that* home). 26 contracts in `scripts/check_b415_ssh_key_truth.sh` + 14 unit tests / 11 sub-cases (incl. the real stderr shape from the screenshot and ssh-keygen's own fingerprint for the fixture); troubleshooting: `docs/troubleshooting.md` §4 "SSH is refused when adding a new exit node"
- **B414** — (2026-10-24, operator: «Проблема с цветом отображения текста в новых добавленных полях и в целом проверить что нет смешения стилей тем и на всех страницах по всем темам видно все элементы страницы - что они не сливаются с фоном») **a page may not paint its own colours, and a missing token must not hide behind a fallback.** Root cause measured on the B412 exit-node card: the new SSH-key blocks used `background:var(--bg-subtle,#111)` and `--bg-subtle` **never existed in `themes.css`**, so the fallback turned them into dark slabs while the inherited `--text` stayed dark in the two LIGHT themes (`vercel`, `mint`) — the operator's «цвет текста в новых добавленных полях» was an unreadable key block, and the fallback is what converted a typo into an invisible panel instead of a visible error. The sweep found 28 style rules + 23 `<style>` blocks with the same class (hardcoded `#dc3545`/`#198754`/`#6c757d`, `#fff`/`#000` on token fills, surfaces `#f5f5f5`/`#e0f2fe` that only read on a light page). Fixed: a semantic alias block in `:root` (`--bg-subtle`, `--bg-muted`, `--bg-input`, `--card-bg`, `--fg`, `--card-elevated`, `--error`, `--warning-fg`, `--accent-success|danger|warning` …) so older markup resolves; `--text-subtle` raised to a legible value in every theme; `mint` accent/semantic hues darkened to clear the floor; every literal replaced by the token it was imitating. Guard `scripts/check_b414_theme_visibility.sh`: **A** every token the panel uses is defined by `themes.css`, the aliases resolve in all five themes, and no template carries a hardcoded `var()` fallback — that is the shape that turned a missing token into an invisible page; **B** WCAG floors **computed** from the stylesheet for **75** (foreground, surface) pairs across `linear`/`vercel`/`sentry`/`nvidia`/`mint` (text ≥7:1, muted/subtle/semantic ≥4.5:1 on both `--bg` and `--bg-card`, `--accent-fg` on `--accent` ≥4.5:1), plus a planted unreadable palette proving the checker can fail, so no floor is satisfied by an empty scan; **A3/A4** no template and no stylesheet `var()` may pass a hardcoded colour as a FALLBACK — that is the shape that turned a missing token into an invisible page (20 template fallbacks and the `--bg-subtle,#111` block were removed; `--text-subtle` raised to clear 4.5:1 on the CARD surface, not just the page: `linear` `#767b87`→`#8a8f9f`, `sentry` `#8a8198`→`#948b9d`); **C/D** frozen literals in `scripts/b414_hardcoded_bg_allowlist.txt` (**12** colour-swatch rules that paint the colour they offer) and `scripts/b414_hardcoded_fg_allowlist.txt` (**3** keys), both may only shrink — C2 fails a fixed page that stays in the list; **E** the two `themes.css` copies stay byte-identical (B392/B411 rule). The sweep also renegotiated **B250**: its three `/admin/acls` info-card contracts were INERT (a `check` whose grep was piped into a second grep swallowed its own PASS row and reported 5 pass / 0 fail while all three assertions were false), and the card they pinned was itself the theme-mixing defect — hand-picked light text over a fixed dark palette. 22 contracts
- **B413** — (2026-10-23, operator: «очень долго осуществляется прогон гейт есть ли способ распараллелить выполнение его и тем самым ускорить?») **the parallel pool was built, measured and removed; the gate got faster a different way.** Measured on the reference 8-core Windows workstation: sequential runs took **93, 115 and 138 minutes** on three different runs (459/0), while **6 workers over a file queue took 119 minutes AND turned a check red on a healthy tree** (a go-test contract that normally takes a couple of minutes blew its budget under load and reported a product failure that was a scheduling artefact — trap #15). The per-check timings explain it: 182 checks cost ≥10 s and spend 88 % of the catalog's CPU, 19 are multi-minute (708 s, 350 s, 208 s…), and they are `check_b*.sh` contracts that themselves run `go test` — concurrency oversubscribes the box and makes a go-test check blow its budget, which then reads as a product failure (trap #15). Parallelism does not create CPU, it only decides who waits. Shipped instead: (1) **per-check timings** — the summary prints the eight slowest checks and `SKYGATE_GATE_TIMINGS_OUT=<file>` dumps the TSV, so optimization targets what actually costs; (2) **`SKYGATE_GATE_ONLY=<id,…>`** in the catalog (selected run, the rest print `SKIP`, a SKIP never affects the verdict and the summary names the scope) and **`scripts/gate_affected.sh`**, which maps a diff to the catalog entries whose registration text names a changed path or its basename, always adds the meta set (B1 B2 B3 B4 B7 B10 B325 B330 B337 B340 B413, plus B281 B299 B322 B335 when the catalog moved), reports changed paths **no check owns**, and refuses a bad `--base` with exit 2 instead of selecting nothing. The full catalog stays the release gate and what CI runs. 31 contracts in `scripts/check_b413_fast_gate.sh` (behavioural filtered run, TSV dump, bad-base refusal, no resurrected pool without a new measurement, and — found on 2026-10-24 while releasing v1.5.158, when a 117-check diff-scoped run reported `GATE PASSED: 0 check(s) green` — the filter deleted SPACES instead of treating them as separators, so `gate_affected.sh`'s space-separated list matched NOTHING: **either separator is now honoured, a scope that matches no catalog entry FAILS and names itself, an unknown id is listed, and the selector prints its scope in the parseable form**)
- **B412** — (2026-10-23, operator: «просто указав необходимые параметры нового vps и нажать кнопку зарегистрировать новый exit node … сделать удобную форму … подсказки на главной странице … учитывать, нативно или в докере») **one button, whole onboarding, and the page stops offering two look-alike "registrations".** B266 only PRINTED a command and the «Добавить exit node» form required a `node_id` for a node that must already exist, so a fresh VPS could not be added from the panel; the key block also rendered BELOW the form that needs it. New `internal/exitnode/provision_b412.go` builds ONE idempotent bash script (root gate → ip_forward → tailscale install → `tailscale up --authkey … --advertise-exit-node` → MASQUERADE/FORWARD with `-C` before `-A`) run over the hardened ssh argv with the key on **stdin only**; `PostAdminExitNodeProvision` (POST `/admin/exit-nodes/provision`) validates, preflights the key (B292), refuses a taken node name, serialises runs, mints the tagged key as `infra`, waits for the node, writes the `exit_servers` row and finishes through the same `SyncRoutesForNode` as «Пере-синхронизировать»; dry-run only probes. Page leads with the card (`#new-exit-node`) and states the detected install kind + key path; manual B266 half kept below as «Ручной режим»; admin dashboard carries the hint. 67 contracts in `scripts/check_b412_exit_node_provision.sh` + `internal/exitnode/provision_b412_test.go` (incl. a real `bash -n` of the generated script)
- **B411** — (2026-10-22, operator: «большинство страниц на широких экранах не оставляет справа много свободного места … надо делать глобальный рефактор и проверку инструментами по каждой странице») **two fixes: the shell is now centred in the space that exists, and a rotating CDN is no longer reported as a broken mechanism.** **(1) Layout.** `.shell` carries `max-width:1180px;margin:0 auto`, and a later rule overrode **only** its left margin to 220px, so `margin-right:auto` absorbed every spare pixel and the content stayed pinned to the sidebar: measured 0px empty at 1366, 40 at 1440, **520 at 1920, 1160 at 2560, 2040 at 3440**. The shell was never centred — it was left-aligned inside a centred 1180px box. The reservation moved to `main` (220px / 52px collapsed / 0 on mobile), so `.shell` centres inside the real content column, and two media steps widen it on large displays (1400px at ≥1600px, 1600px at ≥2200px). The breadcrumb is a sibling **inside** `main`, so it inherits the offset: B120's mirrored 220/52px margins are **gone** (a second offset would double the shift) while B120's intent — never under the sidebar — is re-pinned in `scripts/check_b120.sh` and `TestB411_*`. The per-page half is a ratchet, not a promise: no template may force an inline `min-width` ≥ 300px (the thing that overflows a 360px phone; 0 today) and inline container widths ≥ 600px are frozen at **5** in `scripts/b411_inline_width_allowlist.txt` (`admin/cluster.html` ×3 forms, `admin/derp.html`, `admin/user_subnet.html`) and may **only fall**. **(2) `exit_rules.domain_prefix` was red on a healthy installation.** Measured minutes apart: 6 of 13 addresses "stale" — then the next pass left the 5 published Akamai ranges untouched, removed 8 obsolete `/32` rows and wrote the new answer as a gap. The mechanism was right both times; the assertion compared today's DNS with the stored rows, which a rotating provider (Akamai `62.115.x`, AWS registry edges) can never satisfy while B308 re-resolves every 6h. The verdict is now about the mechanism — pure `exit_rules.DomainRowsVerdictFor` fed by the SAME per-domain resolve stamp the auto-updater writes: **covered** / **pending-next-pass** (the answers moved after the last resolve, the interval covers them → PASS, and it says so) / **stale** (a pass was due and did not cover them, the rule was never resolved, or auto-update is OFF so nothing will ever run → FAIL). The pre-B409 defect is the only failing case. 21 contracts in `scripts/check_b411_layout.sh` + 8 pure verdict tests + 5 layout tests.
- **B410** — (2026-10-22, operator: «на странице /admin/system_tests?tab=history содержимое выходит за границы таблицы … большинство страниц на широких экранах не оставляет справа много свободного места») **a table must scroll inside its card, not leave it.** Measured with the audit method of the `skygate-web-ui-audit` skill: the History tab renders a **nine-column** table (six of them with fixed widths, plus `th{white-space:nowrap}` in `themes.css`) and it was **not** inside `.table-wrap` — the only element carrying `overflow-x:auto` (`themes.css:485`) — so a wide row pushed the table past the card's padding and out of the page column. The same class was measured on **19 other admin templates** (a repeatable one-liner: count `<table` vs `table-wrap` per file). Fixed for the named page (all three of its tables wrapped), and the remainder is a **frozen budget** (`scripts/b410_unwrapped_tables_allowlist.txt`, the B337 ratchet pattern): `scripts/check_b410_table_overflow.sh` fails when an unwrapped table appears in a NEW file **and** when a file listed in the budget has been fixed but not removed from it, so the list can only shrink. The contract also asserts the **CSS prerequisite** (`.table-wrap` sets `overflow-x:auto`), because wrapping a table in an element that does not scroll would read like a fix and change nothing.

- **B409** — (2026-10-22, live run as the operator) **a CDN marker must not end the conversation.** `exit_rules.domain_prefix` answered «5 of 12 address(es) are stale or missing», naming `agent.minimax.io → 62.115.253.59` and `cdn-registry-1.docker.io → 54.157.162.28 / 100.60.245.70`. The rows were **fresh** (5 Akamai ranges, 6 rows) and the resolver had run that day — yet today's answers were covered by **nothing**. Root cause, straight out of `sync_domain.go`: `existingMarker := … parent_domain LIKE 'cdn:%:<domain>' …; if isCDNMarker(existingMarker) { continue }` — a bare `continue` **before any DNS lookup**, so a domain with a CDN marker was never resolved again and the marker was treated as the whole answer. A CDN's published set grows and a domain's answers move to other edges, so an **enabled** rule sat in the panel with rows in the database and no traffic routed. Fixed by resolving on the marked path and writing a **`/32` for every answer no stored range contains** (`b409ReconcileGaps`), deleting gap rows whose address is no longer an answer, keeping the published **range** rows untouched (no churn; the gap query filters `/32`, so a range can never be mistaken for a gap) and writing gap rows **under the same marker** so the B298 dedup partition keeps them. A resolver failure is **not** a verdict: the rows are left alone, the domain is not marked resolved, the next tick retries. Pinned by `internal/feature/exit_rules/sync_domain_cdn_gap_b409_test.go` (covered stays covered; uncovered becomes a gap; no ranges means every answer is a gap; IPv6/garbage never become `/32`; the gap list is stably sorted) + 21 contracts in `scripts/check_b409_cdn_gap_coverage.sh`

- **B408.1** — (2026-10-21, from the B408 debug session) **«прогнать этот» must leave a trace.** Measured on the operator's installation with v1.5.151 live: the audit shows **two** starts of the same single test (`20:41:35` and `20:42:17`, `system_tests_run started test exit_rules.domain_prefix`), while `system_tests_runs` gained **nothing** — `runTestsAsync` persisted only whole-catalogue runs (`persisted := false; if only == "" { s.PersistRun(...) }`). After the page reloaded, the row fell back to the last FULL run, **three days old**, so the only reasonable conclusion was that the re-run does not work. **The button worked; its result was discarded.** V081 adds `system_tests_runs.only_test` (**both** migration chains) and `PersistRunScoped` writes it, so a run remembers its **scope**; the runner persists every run, single or full. Because the newest row may now hold exactly one test, the page does **not** read the newest row: `ListLastResultsPerTest` returns the newest verdict **per test** across the last 50 runs (scanned in Go so the same code runs on SQLite and PostgreSQL — a window function would fork the dialect), which is what the page always meant by «last result». History **rates** stay comparable because `ListRecentRuns` excludes `only_test <> ''`, and the B394 contract that demanded the opposite («a single-test run must not write a history row») was **renegotiated** rather than left contradicting the code. 16 contracts in `scripts/check_b408_1_single_run_persisted.sh`

- **B408** — (2026-10-21, operator: «подключись инструментом для отладки») **a test must not blame a host for its own deadline.** Measured on the operator's installation with the build that ALREADY contains B403: `tailnet.all_nodes_reachability` ran for exactly **5.000534369 s** and `tailnet.split_suspected` for **5.000250288 s**; `emilia`, `karolina` and `sharlotta` answered in ~100 ms each, and **every** remaining host then reported `dial tcp …:18022: i/o timeout`, which the page rendered as LIKELY TAILNET SPLIT. The five seconds were the **harness** ceiling — one `context.WithTimeout` per test — not a network timeout: the probes were cancelled mid-sweep by the per-test context, so the verdict was a fact about the **runner** reported as a fact about the tailnet. That is the same class B403 removed from the probe itself, one layer up, and the probe could never fix it because the deadline arrives from **outside** it. Fixed in three mechanical steps: `SystemTestDef` gains a `Timeout` field and `systemTestBudget` sizes the budget **by category** (an in-process check keeps **5 s**; the network / headscale / derp sweeps get **75 s**, because a fleet sweep cannot finish in five seconds); the runner checks `budgetExhausted(testCtx)` after the test returns and, when the harness stopped it, **appends** a sentence naming the budget and stating that any host without an answer was **NOT measured** («a host nobody asked is not a host that refused»); and a cut-off run can never be reported as PASS. Pinned by `internal/feature/admin/system_tests_budget_b408_test.go` (the fleet categories no longer keep the 5 s ceiling, ordinary tests still do, a declared `Timeout` wins, an exhausted budget is visible and a live one is not) + 18 contracts in `scripts/check_b408_verdict_budget.sh`

- **B406** — (2026-10-21, from the B401-era audit) **a derived row is a prefix, not a /32.** `/admin/system_tests` answered `exit_rules.domain_prefix` `FAIL` «8 of 12 address(es) are stale or missing», while the **same tree** wrote **215** derived subnet rows that day and its `domain_resolve_at:*` keys had been refreshed minutes earlier. Both facts were true because the **writer** and the **reader** disagreed about what a derived row IS: the CDN path (B183/B274/B298) writes the provider's **RANGE** — `cdn:cloudflare:registry.npmjs.org` → `104.16.0.0/12`, **15 rows per domain** — while the plain path writes the resolved address as `/32`. The test asked for a row whose `target_value` is **exactly `ip/32`**, which is false for every range-covered domain, so healthy rules looked rotten and the real staleness (a domain whose row set no longer contains its address at all) was buried in the same sentence; the operator's only remedy would have been pressing Re-sync forever. Fixed by asking the question the ACL actually asks — **do any ENABLED rows of THIS device CONTAIN the address** — with containment computed on parsed `netip.Prefix` values in **one pure helper** (`b406Prefixes` masks host bits so a sloppy legacy row cannot widen the answer, `b406Covers` answers containment, `b406DevicePrefixes` loads only this device's enabled subnet rows). A **read failure** is now reported as a fact about the **check** («could not read the device's derived rows»), never as staleness for every address (trap #15), and a genuine gap names the address with «is in no enabled row». Pinned by `internal/feature/admin/system_tests_domain_rows_b406_test.go` against a real in-memory SQLite DB (a `/12` covers an address inside it and not one outside; a `/32` covers only itself; non-CIDR values are ignored; an invalid address is never covered; disabled rows and another device's rows never charge an address) + 18 contracts in `scripts/check_b406_domain_rows.sh`
- **B407** — (2026-10-21, operator: «посмотри анализ утечек») **a test fixture must not look like a leaked credential.** GitHub Secret scanning showed **four open Tailscale API Key alerts**, and all four were fixtures: `tskey-…` then `auth-test-placeholder` in `internal/module/tailscale/tailscale_test.go:155`, plus three more (`deadbeef`, `supersecret`, `auto-generated` words) in `internal/feature/admin/admin_tailscale_test.go`. None is a real key — a real one is `tskey-…` with an `auth` type and a `k` prefix + ~43 random characters (**57** total), while these are **27–35** characters containing dictionary words — but the scanner matches the **pattern**, scans the **whole history**, and the three old fixtures live in commits whose code was **deleted in v1.3.0**, so **no edit can ever clear those alerts; only a dismissal can**. That is the damage: a repository that keeps producing this class teaches everyone to ignore the scanner, exactly as a red system-test row for «not configured» taught operators to ignore red rows (B405). Fixed **at the source**: the one fixture still in the tree is now **composed at runtime** (`"tskey" + "-auth-test-placeholder"`) so no contiguous key text exists, while the module config and the `--authkey=` assertion both use the composed value, so the test proves exactly what it proved before. The contract forbids the **class**, not the instance: it scans every tracked file for a key-SHAPED credential (GitHub matched `tskey-…` + a word + more, 31 characters, so the detector models the shape rather than a length floor; plus the GitHub / AWS / Slack patterns), **proves the detector by planting a real-shaped key** in a temp file (the B340 lesson — a detector nobody has seen fire proves nothing), checks that the documented placeholders (`tskey-auth-...`) do **not** trip it so the check cannot be dismissed as noisy, and forbids private-key **material** in non-test code while allowing the header-only PEM fixtures the tree already uses. **Deliberately did NOT** add `**/*_test.go` to `.github/secret_scanning.yml` → `paths-ignore`: a real key pasted into a test is exactly the case that must be caught. The dismissal procedure — why three alerts can never be fixed by editing code, and the exact `gh api … -f state=resolved -f resolution=used_in_tests` call — is written into `docs/operations.md` §19, and the contract asserts it stays there. 17 contracts in `scripts/check_b407_no_key_literals.sh`
- **B405** — (2026-10-21, from the B401-era audit) **«not configured» is not «broken».** Measured in the catalogue: `/admin/system_tests` answered configuration questions with a FAILURE verdict — `integrations.configured` said «no integration keys configured (DERP / headplane / telegram all empty)» and `backup.recent` said «backup dir not configured» as red rows. An installation that uses neither Telegram nor Headplane nor S3 backups is a **correct** installation, and a red row for it is not cosmetic: the catalogue's whole value is that red means *this is broken*, so a red row that means *I never turned this on* teaches the operator to ignore red rows. A row about an **optional** subsystem has **three** answers, not two — `PASS` (configured, and it works), `FAIL` (configured, and it is broken), `SKIP` (never configured: **nothing was verified and nothing is wrong**) — and the third is now produced by one constructor, `systemTestNotConfigured`, which names the feature, says exactly what is missing and adds one line on how to configure it (a bare «not configured» leaves the operator as stuck as the false red row did). `FAIL` for «not configured» survives **only** for the dependencies without which the process cannot work at all (its database, its headscale client), and the contract scans every remaining site so an optional feature cannot drift back one call site at a time. The block also fills a hole: the catalogue had **no row for the OIDC provider**, so an operator who enabled it had nothing to diagnose — `integrations.oidc` reports `SKIP` when there is no `oidc_settings` row (or it is disabled) and `FAIL` naming the missing `client_id` / `redirect_uris` / `key_dir` when it IS enabled, because an enabled-but-incomplete provider means logins will fail with nothing on the page saying why. Pinned by `internal/feature/admin/system_tests_notconfigured_b405_test.go` against a real in-memory SQLite DB (the skip names the fix; no row skips; a disabled row skips; enabled-but-incomplete fails and names all three gaps; complete passes and reports the issuer) + 29 contracts in `scripts/check_b405_not_configured.sh`
- **B404** — (2026-10-21, from the B401-era audit) **a stored secret must not travel to the browser, and it must not sit in the database as plaintext.** Measured in the shipped template: `/admin/backup` rendered **both** backup credentials as ordinary input values — the SMB password and the S3 Secret Access Key — so **every page load shipped the decrypted secret to the browser**. `type=password` masks the glyphs and nothing else: the value sits in the DOM, so a screenshot, a shared screen, the browser's own form history or a password manager's «save this login?» prompt was enough to lift an SMB or S3 credential off the panel. The same two values sat verbatim in `global_settings` while the rest of the codebase already encrypts this class of value (`ha.dns.password_enc`, `portal_users.headscale_api_key_enc`, the OIDC client secret — all `db.EncryptForColumn`). **The echo could not simply be removed**: the form is the write path, so an unchanged save would then have *silently erased* both credentials — so the block is three contracts at once. **(1) Write-only rendering:** the value never appears in the HTML; the page shows only a masked fingerprint (`SecretFingerprint` — last four characters, full mask for anything short) plus an explicit «delete the saved secret» checkbox, and the detector scans the **whole template tree** so the pattern cannot come back with a copied form. **(2) Keep / replace / delete** in one decision point, `mergeSubmittedSecret`: an empty field **keeps** the stored value, a ticked box deletes it, a typed value replaces it — and `PostAdminBackupTest` merges the stored secret too, because taking the echo away would otherwise have turned every «Test» click into a false authentication failure. **(3) Encrypted at rest:** an `enc:v1:` marker plus AES-256-GCM from `internal/db` when `SKYGATE_SECRET_KEY` is set; a legacy plaintext row keeps working and is upgraded on the next save (**no migration, no re-entry**); the at-rest form is **reported** on the page, because plaintext storage must be a warning and not a silent default; a ciphertext row read without the key is **named and cleared** instead of being handed to `mount.cifs` as a password that would blame the NAS; and a save without the key **refuses to overwrite** ciphertext rather than downgrading it. Every panel and CLI path goes through `LoadWithKey`/`SaveWithKey` (`backup-run`, `show-config`, «Run now», the in-app toggle, the S3 download, the certsync S3 config), so a cron-driven backup can no longer disagree with a panel-driven one about the same credential. Pinned by `internal/backup/secrets_b404_test.go` (real SQLite round trip: ciphertext at rest, plaintext warning, unreadable-not-a-blob, refuse-to-downgrade, upgrade-on-save, mixed state) and `internal/feature/admin/backup_config_b404_test.go` + 42 contracts in `scripts/check_b404_secret_write_only.sh`
- **B403** — (2026-10-20, operator question) **a probe must say what it measured.** Measured on the operator's installation: `tailnet.all_nodes_reachability` answered «3/6 probable Tailscale nodes reachable (50%)» with `cyborg`, `skybars-secure` and `svyatoslava` all failing `dial tcp …:18022: i/o timeout`, and `tailnet.split_suspected` then declared «LIKELY TAILNET SPLIT». **None of it was about the tailnet.** Two semantic defects: **(1)** a TCP connect that is **REFUSED proves the path works** — the peer answered — while only a timeout or «no route» leaves it unproven; the probe treated every error as «unreachable», so a host with the port closed was indistinguishable from a host behind a broken tunnel; **(2)** SSH is skygate's own contract **only for the hosts it manages** (`tag:dev-infra-*`, whose advertised routes it sets over SSH) — a phone, a laptop or a Windows box never runs sshd, so probing one measures nothing. Two of the three failures were exactly such devices, and the third (`svyatoslava`) answers its cluster heartbeat every 30 s. Fixed by making the probe answer three questions and say which: `TailnetProbeVerdict` = `open` (a connect completed) | `closed` (answered and refused — the PATH IS PROVEN) | `filtered` (timeout/no-route/unreadable — the only blameable verdict), with `Reachable() = open|closed` as the single rule; a refusal on any candidate port is accepted as a proven path and printed as «path-ok (no sshd)» instead of inventing a `0s` latency; and `tailnetProbeTargets` splits online nodes into **managed hosts** (probed) and **devices** (counted, named, never failed), which both tailnet tests consult — so the split detector's denominator is what it can actually ask, and its output names the devices it did not ask, with the reason. Pinned by `internal/feature/admin/system_tests_tailnet_b403_test.go` (a refusal is a proven path; a timeout is the only blameable class; the three live devices produce **zero** probe targets; the skip list stays a third class) + 25 contracts in `scripts/check_b403_probe_truth.sh`.
- **B402** — (2026-10-20, operator question) **a heartbeat that arrives must beat a state that says it did not, and the panel must be able to settle it by hand.** Measured on the operator's installation: cluster_node held svyatoslava | roles={skygate-standby} | state=failed | skygate_version=v1.5.108 | joined 2026-10-09 09:45 | last_seen_at = 2026-10-20 07:45 — **the current minute** — while cluster_audit showed 2026-10-09 17:40 node_health {to:failed, from:ready, actor:elector}. Two independent freezes: **(1)** Heartbeat() moved only pending → ready, while the elector marks ailed when heartbeats stop, and **nothing moved it back** — RejoinNode had exactly **one** caller, the rolling-upgrade orchestrator — so a node that kept heartbeating every 30 s stayed «dead» for **eleven days** and only hand-written SQL could fix it; **(2)** HeartbeatRequest carried 
ode_id + 	oken only, so cluster_node.skygate_version could only ever hold what the JOIN wrote: a standby on v1.5.108 and one on the current build were indistinguishable. Fixed in four places that must stay in step: the heartbeat now reverses **failed → ready** (the failed verdict's premise is falsified by the heartbeat itself) and audits it as 
ode_health with ctor=heartbeat plus the reason and the reported build, while **draining is never touched**; HeartbeatRequest.Version + selfVersion() (the same string /healthz and the panel footer use, B366) with a CASE WHEN … <> '' guard so an older agent cannot erase the stored build; the page derives **HeartbeatAlive** from the elector's own 90 s window (OnlineThresholdSec = 30 s × 3) and names the contradiction with its own reason/action instead of letting «failed» mean both *dead* and *stale*; **VersionDrift** is one comparison whose contract is that the +commit suffix is part of the identity. **Mark Ready** is reachable by hand: POST /admin/cluster/node/rejoin → cluster.RejoinNode, rendered exactly where the state contradicts the heartbeat. Pinned by internal/cluster/heartbeat_b402_test.go (real invite token: failed → ready; draining survives; empty version does not erase; the reversal is audited) and internal/feature/admin/cluster_b402_test.go (reason pair, drift table incl. hex-case and empty sides, the collector deriving the flag from the row) + 28 contracts in scripts/check_b402_cluster_truth.sh.
- **B401** — (2026-10-20, operator question) **the relay list must say what each row is, and a loopback resolution must name the fix that needs no compose edit.** Operator, verbatim: «derp в форме пишет что не может skygate воспринимать его информацию нужен debug mode, также в релеи висят еще два не рабочих derp что это и к чему». Measured on his host: derp_relays held **three** rows and only **one** is a relay — https://derp.skynas.ru:443 (healthy, 5 ms), https://derp.skynas.ru:8443 (the LAN-direct HTTP port the derper-in-docker migration adds, B260.2) and https://controlplane.tailscale.com/derpmap/default (**a DERP map document** AutoMigrateDerpRelays copied from the legacy derp.external_urls, B265). The page called both non-relays «skipped» and said nothing else — and **three** places had their own opinion about what counts as a relay (the map publisher isDerpMapURL, the prober urlIsRelayNode, the page's reason string). Fixed with **one classifier** in internal/derphealth — ClassifyRelayURL (
ode | derpmap | other_path) and ClassifyAltPort for the same-host/other-port shape, decided from the **data** and never from the notes text — used by the relay page, the map status and a new system test. The table now renders a badge per shape (catalogue / non-DERP path / alternative port) with RU+EN help saying the catalogue is merged by headscale itself and the extra port is a LAN-only entry point; **neither is a broken relay**, and the add **and edit** forms now refuse a non-node URL with the reason. Separately derp.reachable failed because derp.skynas.ru resolved to 127.0.0.1 **inside the container** — the HOST's /etc/hosts maps its own relay name to loopback (line 10) and Docker's resolver forwards it (trap #2). The message used to stop at «fix extra_hosts»; it now names **derp.probe_host FIRST** (a panel field, B296 — the probes still speak the hostname as TLS SNI, so the certificate matches) and the extra_hosts path SECOND with an explicit docker compose up -d --force-recreate skygate (a plain restart keeps the frozen hosts file, trap #3). Guarded by the new system test derp.relay_rows (warning, st.fix.derp_relay_rows) and internal/derphealth/relaykind_b401_test.go.
- **B400** — (2026-10-20, operator question) **the ownership question must be asked where the answer lives.** Operator, verbatim: «сейчас все устройства принадлежат ему [`tagged-devices`] и различие идёт только строго по тегам … в начальных настройках указывается что 14 устройств не определены за пользователем … должен ли быть явный пользователь у устройств или они остаются за tagged device?» **Measured on his installation the same day: `node_owner_map` held 14 rows, 0 unattributed (skyadmin 6 · infra 5 · michail 3); headscale reported all 14 as owned by the built-in `tagged-devices`; the dashboard said «14 node(s) have no panel owner yet».** The wizard's rule was *"does headscale's `node.User` match a panel username?"* — and a tagged node is **always** `tagged-devices` there (that is what tagging does), so the comparison could never succeed and the counter could never reach zero: it measured headscale's bookkeeping, not the presence of an owner, while `node_owner_map` — the table the ACL, `/my/*`, «all my devices» and the preference reconciler read — knew every device. **Answer to the question: keep the tags (and therefore headscale's `tagged-devices`), and keep the explicit owner in `node_owner_map`** — the two layers are not interchangeable; dropping a tag to "get the user back" breaks every per-device ACL rule, every `via` pin and prefix ownership (B265/B272/B275). Fixed by asking in the order that reflects reality: **(1)** infrastructure — a node carrying `tag:dev-infra-*` or owned by the technical user, recognised from **the tags the node carries** (a relay tagged before `node_owner_map` caught up is still a relay); **(2)** `node_owner_map` by node id — the runtime's own answer, accepted only when the panel still has that user, otherwise surfaced as review; **(3)** headscale's user — **only** for a node that carries no tag (a pre-tagging install reads correctly); **(4)** else genuinely awaits review. New `NodeRow.OwnerSource` (`panel` | `headscale`) is **rendered** in the node table with a tooltip explaining that a tagged node's `User` column is bookkeeping, and the step text was corrected to «attached to neither a panel user nor a headscale user». The runtime now reports the SAME question: new system test `ownership.attribution` reads `node_owner_map` and names every unattributed device **plus** every attribution naming a user the panel does not have (warning, `st.fix.ownership_attribution`, RU+EN). Pinned by `internal/setupstate/setupstate_b400_test.go` (8 shapes incl. the nil-map fallback and the corrected count through `Detect`), a render test that asserts the badges reach the **page**, and 25 contracts in scripts/check_b400_attribution.sh.
- **B399** — (2026-10-19, operator blocker) **an operator's «no» must survive the next tick, and re-pointing a device must not silently restore a strict pin.** Operator report, verbatim: «теперь несмотря на то что для skyworker я отключаю strict mode по karolina он ставиться обратно». The live audit log made it a measurement, not a guess — twice in six hours: `19:08:56 skyadmin my_device_preferred_exit_set hostname=skyworker tag=` (the ✕ clear) followed by `18:17:12 system preferred_exit_reconciled CREATE pref hostname=skyworker tag=tag:dev-infra-karolina via=1 reason=missing-pref-unanimous rules=269`. **Clearing DELETED the row**, so the B229 reconciler saw *"a device with rules and no preference"* and did exactly what it exists to do — created one, with `via_enabled=1` — which (because `via` on an `autogroup:internet` grant is a FILTER, B398) **restored the restriction the operator had just removed**. The second half sat in the same pass: the reconciler's UPDATE wrote `via_enabled=true` unconditionally, so the first relay move switched a deliberate opt-out back on. Fixed in four places that must stay in step: **(1)** V080 adds `device_exit_node_prefs.opted_out` in **both** chains (additive, default 0 — an existing install is unchanged and a derived row stays the engine's); **(2)** clearing now **RECORDS the decision** — the row is KEPT with an empty `exit_node_tag`, `via_enabled=0`, `opted_out=1` and the human's id in `set_by_user_id` — because deleting is precisely what erased the person's intent; an explicit set clears the flag, so choosing a node always beats an older opt-out; **(3)** `PlanDevicePrefChange` refuses such a row **FIRST**, before every create branch, and reports it as the named skip `operator-opted-out` (visible, not silent); **(4)** every UPDATE carries the **existing** `via_enabled` forward (`ReconcilerChange.ViaEnabled`) instead of writing `true`. The state is visible where the decision is made: both device pages render «авто-подбор выключен» with the reason. **Same release, same page:** `/admin/exit-nodes` gained a collapsible explanation of the other half of the report — «если включен exit node то локальная сеть не видится, только локальная сеть tailnet, а обычная нет» — documented Tailscale behaviour (an exit node carries **all** of the device's traffic, its own LAN included) fixable only **on the client** with `--exit-node-allow-lan-access`, a flag skygate can neither read nor set; the block names that switch and the alternative (a **subnet router** for a LAN behind the relay). Behaviourally pinned by `internal/db/exit_node_prefs_b399_test.go` (clear records; set clears; a clear of a missing row still records; both list readers carry the flag) and `internal/feature/exit_rules/reconciler_b399_test.go` (never re-created through any of the three derivation paths; a derived row is still managed; an UPDATE preserves the flag in both positions) + 33 contracts in `scripts/check_b399_pref_optout.sh`. **One contract renegotiated, not weakened:** B68a pins the explicit column list of the device-preference INSERT (that literal is the v0.33.1.19 column-order fix); the new column is appended AFTER `via_enabled`, so the literal now asserts the ordered PREFIX — appending a column is allowed, reordering or dropping one is not.
- **B398** — (2026-10-18, operator blocker) **a DERIVED exit-node preference must not become a permission filter, and the device's own choice must be a CLAIM.** Operator report, measured on his own installation: «если выставить emilia, то нет доступа ни к чему; если выбрать karolina — всё грузится». `device_exit_node_prefs` held `cyborg → tag:dev-infra-karolina` with **`set_by_user_id=0`** — written by the auto-reconciler, **not by a human** — and the ACL generator turned every row into a grant `{src:[tag:dev-skyadmin-cyborg], dst:[autogroup:internet], ip:[*], via:[tag:dev-infra-karolina]}`. **`via` on an `autogroup:internet` grant is a SELECTOR, not a preference:** choosing emilia in the Tailscale client made the grant *unmatched*, headscale dropped **all** of that device's exit-node traffic, and no page said the device was restricted (the operator found it by clearing the row and watching access return). The mirror-image defect sat in the **assignment**: cyborg's **16 Google rules all carry an EMPTY `exit_node_id`** ("the engine decides"), so they voted for **no relay** and `prefixowner` spread those four `/32`s over emilia and sharlotta — relays the device never chose (B397 then had to teach the sync to advertise them). Fixed in two places that must stay in step: **(A)** the per-device pin is applied **only** for an explicit human choice (`if dp.SetByUserID <= 0 { derivedNotPinned++; continue }`, logged as `acl: N auto-derived device exit-node preference(s) left UNPINNED (B398) …`) — a derived row still feeds the assignment, it just no longer constrains the client; **(B)** `LoadClaims` now counts a device's **recorded preference as a claim** for the prefixes its own relay-less rules cover (`JOIN device_exit_node_prefs p … AND (r.exit_node_id IS NULL OR r.exit_node_id='')`), joined through **`node_owner_map`** to convert the pref's **tag** into the assignment table's **hostname** — the two units differ, and without the join the claim named a relay `Assign` could never match as healthy (measured: it picked emilia anyway until the join was added). The constraint is now **visible**: a new system test `exit_rules.exit_node_pin` names every pinned device and states the consequence (`via` is a FILTER — another exit node in the client blocks all of its traffic), annotated warning + `st.fix.exit_node_pin` (RU+EN). Behaviourally pinned by `internal/acl/acl_b398_test.go` (a derived preference ⇒ **zero** pinned internet grants and one loose grant; an explicit one ⇒ exactly one pinned, no loose) and `internal/prefixowner/prefixowner_b398_test.go` (the preference votes, the assignment follows it, a device without one stays engine-decided) + 18 contracts in `scripts/check_b398_exit_node_pin.sh`. **What the operator must still know (cannot be fixed in skygate):** `tailscale set --accept-routes` / `--exit-node` are **client-local** — skygate can neither read nor set them, only the relay side (`exit_servers.accept_routes`). The behaviour matrix: exit node + `--accept-routes=false` ⇒ full tunnel; exit node + accept-routes ⇒ accepted prefixes via their relays, everything else via the exit node; no exit node + accept-routes ⇒ per-prefix routing only. Full tunnel **and** per-prefix routing for the same destination is not expressible. **One test fixture renegotiated, not weakened:** `internal/acl/acl_b361_test.go` (C)/(D) seeded the pref with `set_by_user_id = 0` (a DERIVED row) while asserting B361's OWNERSHIP predicate — B398 decides provenance first, so those two would have passed for the wrong reason; they now seed `1`, which is what their prose describes.
- **B397** — (2026-10-18) **every relay the assignment table names must be visited with the UNION of what it owns, because `--advertise-routes` REPLACES the whole set.** Measured on the live installation: `prefix_owner` gave **emilia** `64.233.162.91/32` + `.136/32` and **sharlotta** `.93/32` + `.190/32` by **auto** assignment (claimed by cyborg's 16 Google rules, **all with an empty `exit_node_id`** — the rules claim the prefixes but name no relay), while the other **215** prefixes went to karolina from explicit claims. The sync visited emilia (a rule named her for something else) and applied her **BASES only**, because the per-rule loop can only advertise prefixes in that relay's own candidate list — and the second loop (B279's "relays the table names even when no rule mentions them") **skipped her precisely because a rule already named her** (`relaysFromAssignment`'s `already` guard). Four prefixes were served by **nobody**, every device pinned to them black-holed, and only the system test noticed. Fixed by **one pass per relay over the union**: `relaysToSync(owners, exitRoutes)` = relays the rules name ∪ relays the table hands prefixes to, each applied with `mergedOwnedPrefixes(node, ruleCandidates, owners)` = `OwnedPrefixes` (its own candidates that it owns) ∪ `OwnedPrefixesForRelay` (everything the table gives it), so a table-owned prefix can no longer be dropped and a not-yet-recorded rule candidate is not lost either. The base routes are still **prepended at the apply boundary** (`syncOneExitNode`), so a table-only set cannot unmake an exit node; the B352 prune loop is intact; and the **zero-claim release already existed** (`Prune`, which keeps `source='manual'` operator pins) — the missing half was the **advertisement**, not the table. Behaviourally pinned by `internal/feature/exit_rules/sync_routes_b397_test.go` (the union, the skipped relay, single-advertiser integrity, empty names) + 12 contracts in `scripts/check_b397_route_union.sh`. Live consequence: the four phantom prefixes are advertised by their recorded owners on the next sync pass, so `prefix_owner.consistency` stops failing.
- **B396** — (2026-10-18) **the diagnostic catalogue must not answer one question from two sources, and it must name the cause it has already measured.** Four of the failures the B394 catalogue produced on the operator's live installation were about the TESTS, not the product. **(1) Two backup tests, one question, two sources:** `backup.recent` FAILED on an 819 h old **local** archive while `backup.verify` PASSED with an S3 archive 20 h old — the destination had moved to S3 and the local directory kept a file from before the move, so the test that read the wrong source was the lie. The run records (`backup.last_run` / `backup.last_archive`, written by the backup itself whatever the destination) now win whenever they exist, and the local-directory scan stays only for a legacy installation that has archives but has never recorded a run. **(2) `derp.reachable` reported `dial tcp 127.0.0.1:443` as "unreachable — check the firewall/DNS"**, but the relay NAME resolved to the container's own loopback: a host that resolves its own public name to 127.0.0.1 leaks into containers through `/etc/hosts` or a compose `extra_hosts` entry (trap #2), and no firewall rule changes that.
