The test now resolves the name first, classifies an **all-loopback** answer (`b396AllLoopback`, unit-tested) and names the mechanism and the fix. **(3) `prefix_owner.consistency` merged two faults with two different buttons:** a prefix the owner does not **advertise** (→ Re-sync) and one it advertises but nobody **APPROVED** (→ Approve; clients ignore unapproved routes — B273's lesson). Each is now its own named class and the summary carries both counts. **(4) `exit_rules.domain_prefix` promised "the domain auto-updater rewrites them on its interval"** whether or not the auto-updater was switched on — exactly the case where the rows rot forever; it now reports the effective switch (`dns_autoupdate_enabled`) and the interval the updater itself parses, and says OFF means nothing will rewrite them until the operator acts. Behaviourally pinned by `internal/feature/admin/system_tests_b396_test.go` (a fresh run record with no local archive must PASS `backup.recent`; an 819 h old one must FAIL **both** backup tests) + 13 contracts in `scripts/check_b396_diagnostic_honesty.sh`. Lesson: two tests that answer the same question from different sources are a bug with a countdown — one of them will lie, and the operator cannot tell which.
- **B395.4** — (2026-10-18) **the users half of the import table, found by the third docker scenario.** The objective asks for *"a table of users AND nodes over an existing headscale, with the ambiguities"*; the nodes table shipped in v1.5.134 and `Report.Users` was collected from the first version and **rendered nowhere** — notice that nothing caught it: every installation the block had been looked at (the operator's tailnet, the live host) has **nodes**, so the node table covered the page, and the one shape that has users without nodes is a control plane just taken over, which is *exactly* the "transfer these people to the panel" case. The three local docker scenarios found it in one run: scenario 3 seeds control-plane **users** (node registration is not scriptable on headscale 0.29 without a real tailscale client) and the page said «Headscale: есть данные» with no table at all. Shipped `Report.PendingUsers Count` (users the panel has no row for, or whose username is ambiguous — `Known`, so an unmeasured number is «неизвестно», never 0) and a `wiz-users` table (headscale user · panel user or «не привязан» · state) with a counted summary line (`wizard.users_show` / `wizard.users_unmapped`, RU + EN); nodes keep their own independent table, so users-without-nodes now shows what to transfer. Contract `I11` (40 total in `scripts/check_b395_setup_wizard.sh`) + `TestDashboardWizard_UsersTableIsRendered` (mapped / unmapped / ambiguous + the counted line). All three scenarios re-run against this tree and PASS: absent (install block, no user card — no nodes can be listed without headscale) / empty (user card) / populated (USERS table + count + ACL ack form + AI link). **The harness itself was repaired first:** `scenario.sh` aborted the entire run when a scenario legitimately lacked one of its printed markers (`grep -o` exits 1 under `set -e` + `pipefail` — AGENTS trap #9 inside the harness), and `probe_state.sh` now asserts the EXPECTED state per scenario instead of printing marker counts. Lesson: a scenario that only prints is not a scenario that checks.
- **B395.3** — (2026-10-18) **the click must say something.** v1.5.136 made the ACL acknowledgement work on PostgreSQL and the live click answered `?wizard=acl_acked&snapshot=147738` — a correct result the page said **nothing** about: `POST /admin/setup/acl-ack` redirects with `?wizard=acl_acked&snapshot=N` / `?wizard=acl_ack_failed&why=…` and **no code read those parameters**, so a successful click looked like nothing happened until the 30 s wizard cache expired, while a REFUSED click (the very `invalid input syntax for type integer` sentence v1.5.136 fixed) lived only in the URL. The acknowledged id was also applied **inside** the cached report, so it could not appear before the TTL even when it was recorded. Fixed: `setupWizardReport` is a thin wrapper over `setupWizardReportCached` that applies the acknowledged id (`global_settings['setup.acl_ack_snapshot']`, one small read) on **every** call, so the button disappears and «снапшот №N сохранён» appears the moment the redirect lands; `wizardFlashFromQuery` turns the redirect into `WizardFlash` (success with the id, or the measured refusal reason) and the card renders one line (`wizard.ack_ok` / `wizard.ack_failed_label`, RU + EN) — a refusal is now a visible sentence carrying the raw DB/headscale error instead of a silent no-op. Contracts `I9`/`I10` (39 total in `scripts/check_b395_setup_wizard.sh`) + `TestDashboardWizard_AcknowledgementFlash` (success / refusal / no flash, and a plain visit must show none). **Two contracts renegotiated, not weakened:** `E4` asserts the CALL (`ackSnapshotIDForWizard(s)`) rather than the exact assignment line the wrapper moved, and `I2` names the step marker (`wiz-step-why">{{.Why}}`) because the flash renders its own `Why` — a raw diagnostic by design — so the generic `>{{.Why}}<` scan would have forbidden the fix it just shipped. Lesson: L-74
- **B395.2** — (2026-10-18) **press the button on the backend the operator runs.** v1.5.135 put the ACL-acknowledgement form on the dashboard and the deploy verification proved it was there (and that a WRONG CSRF token is refused with a named reason); the first real press on the host, which runs **PostgreSQL**, answered `snapshot: ERROR: invalid input syntax for type integer: "setup-wizard" (SQLSTATE 22P02)`. `SnapshotForACL` wrote the literal string `"setup-wizard"` into `acl_snapshots.version` — `INTEGER NOT NULL` in **both** migration chains — and **SQLite accepts a string in an INTEGER column while PostgreSQL rejects the statement**, so the action "worked" in every local docker scenario (all SQLite) and never once on the operator's backend. Same function, same class: it returned `Result.LastInsertId()`, which **pgx does not support**, so even a correct insert would have created the row and lost its id. Fixed by reading the version as `db.NextACLVersion` does (`SELECT COALESCE(MAX(version),0)`, written as `MAX(version)+1`) and taking the id from `RETURNING id` inside the INSERT (the `db.qInsertPortalUser` shape, which works on pgx and on `modernc.org/sqlite`); `applied_success` stays 0 because this is the PRE-apply copy. Tests per dialect on purpose: `internal/setupstate/snapshot_b395_2_pg_test.go` runs the PostgreSQL leg against `SKYGATE_TEST_PG_DSN` (SKIPs without it per rule 1 — CI's test-pg job sets it; verified locally against a throwaway `postgres:16-alpine` BEFORE the push) and the SQLite leg so the fix cannot trade one dialect's bug for the other's. Contracts I7/I8 pin the shape (no string version, no `LastInsertId`) — `scripts/check_b395_setup_wizard.sh` is at **37**. Lesson: L-74 rule 5
- **B395.1** — (2026-10-18) **a release is not verified until you have READ the page it renders.** v1.5.134 went live with three defects that 3 local docker scenarios, 14 tests and 29 contracts could not see, all in the gap between "the symbol exists" and "the operator can read or press it": the dashboard printed the literal i18n key `wizard.why_acl_will_be_rewritten` (Step.Why carries a KEY and the template rendered `{{.Why}}` RAW), a Russian card carried the English sentence `14 node(s) have no panel owner yet` (a `Sprintf` inside `stepsFor` — the catalogue parity test compares key SETS and B325 scans templates, so Go-built text is invisible to both), and the ACL warning's acknowledgement — a registered, audited, idempotent route since B395 — had **no form on the page**, so the "recoverable snapshot" promise had no button (`grep -c 'setup/acl-ack'` on the live page = 0). Fixed: the step reason renders through `t`/`tf` (a key translates, a raw diagnostic `Reason` passes through unchanged) with `Step.WhyCount` for counted text and the new key `wizard.why_pending_review`; the dashboard now renders the acknowledgement form until one exists (the saved id then replaces it) and mints a per-page CSRF token (`skygate_dashboard_csrf`, HttpOnly + SameSite=Lax + 10 min, path `/admin/setup` — the `/my/telegram` pattern), the handler compares it with `subtle.ConstantTimeCompare` and audits a rejection as `setup_acl_ack_csrf_fail`. The contract grew to **35** with section I, which asserts the RENDER path rather than the symbol: translated step text (`I1`/`I2`), a counted reason as key + `WhyCount` (`I3`), the form + CSRF field in the template (`I4`), the token minted on GET and compared on POST (`I5`), and the audited rejection (`I6`). The render tests' stub now WRAPS translated values (`[T:wizard.x]`) instead of echoing them, because a key-echoing stub made "translated" and "printed raw" produce identical strings — which is why the defect shipped; `TestDashboardWizard_StepWhyGoesThroughTheTranslator` and `TestDashboardWizard_AclAcknowledgementIsReachable` pin both. Same tag: v1.5.134's release-notes section lost its 68 duplicated copies (a bulk insert ran without `-First`, 2 345 unintended lines) and its 7/12 test counts became 9/14, and `B237.7 C.3` was repaired (trap #9 pipe + a 180 s budget against a 275 s suite) after it failed a healthy tree. Lesson: L-74
- **B395** — (2026-10-17) **the first-run wizard must NAME the state, never guess it, and must not touch what it does not own.** Operator ask: an interactive first-run wizard on the dashboard with separate admin and user branches, a clean `setupstate` with an honest unknown instead of zeros, the four scenarios («нет headscale / не отвечает / пуст / есть данные»), an import table for users and nodes over an existing headscale with the ambiguities surfaced, an ACL rewrite warning **with a snapshot** and a link to the tokenised AI helper, headscale install instructions for **docker and systemd as ready-made scripts without editing the operator's compose**, and a permission split. **The defect this prevents:** a naive wizard answers «0 devices, 0 exit nodes» for EVERY situation — headscale absent, unreachable and genuinely empty look identical — so an operator whose control plane is down is told the tailnet is empty and starts creating users that go nowhere. Shipped: `internal/setupstate` — `StateAbsent`/`StateUnreachable`/`StateEmpty`/`StatePopulated` (+`StateUnknown`), `Count{Value,Known}` whose `String()` renders «—» (the page then prints a localised «неизвестно»), `Report.StateName()` (**a template cannot `eq` a typed enum with a string — it fails the WHOLE render**, caught by the render test), infra-vs-review classification (`infra` = the technical user, the same literal `BackfillInfra` uses), an `Ambiguity` field so a duplicate headscale username is surfaced instead of silently picked, and `SnapshotForACL`. The dashboard (`GetDashboard`) computes the admin report **only under `if c.IsAdmin`** — the permission split lives in the data, not the template — and a separate `UserWizard` (4 steps: connect → exit node → rules → notifications) for everyone else; the report is cached 30 s keyed by the headscale client pointer (the dashboard is the busiest page; the wizard is a guide, not a monitor). The ACL warning is **recoverable**: `POST /admin/setup/acl-ack` snapshots the live policy into `acl_snapshots`, records `global_settings["setup.acl_ack_snapshot"]` (idempotent — the FIRST snapshot wins, it is the pre-apply one), audits `setup_acl_ack`, and the dashboard reads the id back on **every** visit (E4 asserts the CALL SITE, not the symbol: a helper that is only defined is dead code, which is what staticcheck's `U1000` caught here — the contract used to accept the definition). The install scripts are i18n TEXT (`wizard.install_docker_script`, `wizard.install_systemd_script`) and the wizard performs **no file write and no exec** — the operator's `docker-compose.yml` is theirs (rule 7). Tests: `internal/setupstate/model_b395_test.go` (9 — absent≠empty with unknown counts, unreachable keeps the raw error, empty vs populated, infra separated from review, duplicates flagged, steps per state, stable state strings) + `internal/handlers/dashboard_wizard_render_b395_test.go` (5 render tests — never prints an unknown zero, the admin branch never leaks to a user, the import table classifies, the ACL warning carries the snapshot, the absent state ships the install scripts). 29 contracts in `scripts/check_b395_setup_wizard.sh`; first-run section in `docs/INSTALL.md` (EN + RU).
- **B394** — (2026-10-17) **system tests must be a diagnostic tool, not a grid of pass/fail cells.** Operator ask: severity, «what to do», context, run progress, the false «ОТКЛЮЧЕНО» after POST, filters/anchors/copy-failed, eleven new tests and history retention. **The false state was measured in the code, not guessed:** GetAdminSystemTests built a page map carrying DNSAutoUpdateEnabled / PrefReconcileEnabled / DNSResolveInterval* / Tab / Window / History / LastRun*, while PostAdminSystemTestsRun built its **own, much smaller** map and rendered the same template with it — so right after every run {{if .DNSAutoUpdateEnabled}} read a MISSING key (nil → falsy) and the card claimed both background workers were disabled while they were enabled. One builder (systemTestsPageData) now feeds both handlers, and the contract fails if a second inline page map appears. The rest of the block: every test carries a **severity** (critical/warning/info), an **i18n remediation key** and a **doc link**, applied by ONE annotation table with **per-category floors** so a newly added test can never render unannotated (a Go test walks AllTests() and fails on a gap); the run is **asynchronous** (StartSystemRun + in-memory SystemRunState, polled through GET /admin/system_tests/run.json, 409 on a concurrent start) with a progress bar, and **one test can be re-run from its row** (?test=<name> — it reports to the monitoring inbox but writes no history row, or a history of one-test runs would bury the real ones); the history is **pruned** (PruneSystemTestRuns, 200 runs / 90 days, ANDed, audited) after every successful run — the table had **no pruning at all** before. **Eleven new tests** cover the failures this project has actually had: headscale.exit_routes_approved (an exit node advertising .0.0.0/0 without approval, B273), headscale.tags_drift (the tag 
ode_owner_map records is not on the node, B272), headscale.policy_current (live ≠ generated, B276), prefix_owner.consistency (a prefix served twice or not at all, B274/B275), db.writable_primary (a rolled-back write probe — catches a replica / default_transaction_read_only), db.schema_tables (16 required tables), disk.free_space (fail <5 %, warn <15 %; statfs split into _unix/_windows), derp.reachable (TLS handshake, distinguishing «port closed» from «TLS broken»), uild.ready (the process serves the real routes, not the provisional handler, B269), exit_rules.domain_prefix (stored /32 vs today's DNS) and ackup.verify (< 48 h, status ok, archive recorded). Every one returns **SKIP with a reason** when its live dependency is absent (rule 1). UI: severity badges, a «что делать» block per failing row, a browser-side «failed only» + category filter, per-row anchors (#t-<name>), «copy the failed» and the progress poller. Tests: internal/feature/admin/system_tests_b394_test.go (annotation coverage, category floor, explicit-wins, severity order) + system_tests_b394_run_test.go (async run, single-test run does not pollute history, unknown test refused, retention) + the render test's stub widened (a missing field is a render PANIC in html/template). 33 contracts in scripts/check_b394_system_tests_diagnostics.sh; procedure in docs/operations.md §18.
- **B393** — (2026-10-17, operator blocker) **a self-update must prove it swapped, and the swap helper must see the same `/data` the orchestrator wrote to.** `/admin/update` reported `phase=done, error=null` while the panel kept serving the previous build; v1.5.130 and v1.5.131 only became live because the container was recreated **by hand** (`docker compose up -d --force-recreate --no-deps skygate`). The operator-readable swap log carried, twice and verbatim, `/bin/sh: can't open '/data/skygate-swap-helper.sh': No such file or directory` — two independent defects in one sentence. **(R1)** BOTH swap scripts mounted the literal volume `skygate-data`; that volume is a legacy leftover (created 20:08:31Z, **empty**) while the app's `/data` is the checkout's `./data` **bind** — exactly where the script was written, so the helper mounted a different filesystem and never saw its own script (v1.5.130 "worked" only because a stale pre-v1.3.1 helper survived inside it). **(R2)** Nothing verified that a swap had happened: `pollHealthz` accepted any 200 with `status:ok` (its own doc comment promised an `expectedBuild` parameter that did not exist) and `confirmPendingSwap` in `internal/feature/admin/update.go` did the same from **every** `/admin/update` page render before calling `store.Complete()` — the OLD container answers that 200, so the job was promoted within one page view. Fixes: **one** resolver `resolveDataMount` (`internal/update/swapmount.go`) reads the live container's own `/data` mount (`volume` → volume name, `bind` → absolute host path, `SKYGATE_DATA_MOUNT` override, documented `${SKYGATE_HOST_REPO_PATH}/data` fallback) and renders it into **both** scripts via `renderSwapScript`, so the two can no longer drift; a **pre-flight** in both scripts proves the helper can read its script before it is spawned and writes a `FAILED:` marker naming the mount (a wrong mount is now a named failure, not a 127 buried in the log); both probes are **build-aware** (`BuildSatisfiesTarget`, one comparison for the whole release path) and also reject a still-booting process (`ready:false`/`phase`, B269 — it serves the NEW build string early); the **recreated container id** (`State.SwapFromContainerID`, persisted before the spawn) is the second witness that proves a swap for branch/HEAD targets whose label cannot be predicted; `ResolvePendingSwap` runs at **boot** (`cmd/skygate/main.go`) and on the page, resolves the job immediately when the swap log carries a failure marker, and after `SwapGracePeriod` (4 min) declares it `failed` with the swap log tail folded into the job log instead of leaving it at `build_done` forever. Tests: `internal/update/docker_b393_test.go` (mount parsing, rendered scripts, quoting, build comparison, verdict transitions, persistence, healthz parsing) + `internal/feature/admin/update_b393_test.go` (the missed half: a stale build in the unchanged container must fail, a matching one must complete, the grace window keeps the job open). 40 contracts in `scripts/check_b393_swap_helper_handoff.sh`, including a plant-the-bug detector proof and a live section that SKIPs without docker. Manual/break-glass apply of v1.5.131 (operator `docker-compose.yml` preserved by sha256, rollback path to v1.5.130) recorded in `docs/UPDATE.md`.
- **B389** — (2026-10-16, operator request — «разобраться с телеграмом и найти хороший скилл для оформления») **the rich message must reach the wire, and every reply must survive it.** Measured against the **live Bot API** with the bot's own token from `global_settings.telegram.bot_token` and a bound chat from `telegram_bindings`, POSTing from **inside the skygate container** — the only side with a working egress (B356.1); the **host cannot reach `api.telegram.org` at all** (`curl` → `000` timeout, and the relay names do not resolve either, so the host-side `scripts/monitor_disk.sh` can never deliver its own alert). Two defects, both masked because every failure path only logs. **(1) The envelope was wrong, so the rich path had never delivered a single message since B186.** `sendRichMessage` takes the message under `rich_message`; the code posted `blocks` at the **top level**, and unknown top-level fields are **silently ignored** — `{"chat_id":N,"blocks":[…]}` → `400 Bad Request: rich message must be non-empty`, after which `SendRich`'s own fallback re-sent the body as flat `parse_mode=HTML`. Nesting them then failed too (`type "section_heading" is unsupported`) because the IR's type names were invented from the HTML tag list instead of `InputRichBlock`: the live parser **accepts** `paragraph`, `heading` (numeric `size`), `list` (items are **objects**), `table` (**`cells`**, `PageBlockTableCell`), `details`, `footer`, `divider`, and **rejects** `section_heading`, `aside`, inline `text`, `mark/sub/sup`, and wants `url` rather than `href`. `rich_message.html` answered **200**, and the vocabulary was then enumerated in one shot — **all 31** of h1–h6, p, b, i, u, s, code, pre, a, br, hr, blockquote (plain **and** `expandable`), ul, ol, details/summary, footer, `table bordered striped` (caption, th), tg-spoiler, mark, sub, sup, tg-time, checkbox input and divider were accepted, and **`reply_markup` works alongside `rich_message`**, so a rich reply keeps its inline keyboard. **(2) Four commands could not be delivered in ANY form**: `/invite`, `/accept`, `/mesh create`, `/mesh join` call `markHTMLReply()` at the top of the builder, so their **usage** branch goes out as HTML while the catalogue value carries a literal `<placeholder>` — `400 can't parse entities: Unsupported start tag "username"` (and `"код"`), and `sendPlain` only logs it. The mirror-image defect was as bad: **141** catalogue values carried `&lt;`/`&gt;` while being sent **without** parse_mode, so the user read `&lt;username&gt;`. **Shipped:** escaping moved from the catalogue **value** to the **send boundary** — `internal/telegram/htmlsafe.go` runs at the last moment before the wire (after trimming and splitting, so a truncation can no longer leave a half tag): an accepted tag passes through with attributes, any other `<word>` is escaped, an **unbalanced** accepted tag is escaped (a truncated body is the realistic source), a bare `&` becomes an entity that renders identically, existing entities are untouched so the function is **idempotent**. The catalogue now carries logical text and an i18n test fails if escaping is ever baked back in. The same pass fixed what the reader sees: the rich `/status` table printed the raw `%d` (it used the *format-string* keys as labels), `/mesh create` printed `%!s(MISSING)` (four placeholders, three arguments), the key/value table header read `Field/Value` in every language, `trimForTelegram` counted **bytes** while its sibling heuristic counted runes (a Cyrillic reply could be cut mid-rune and dropped outright) and its marker was always English, `/my_nodes` had **no cap** so a large tailnet exceeded 4096 and the whole reply was lost, the exit-node status words / ago-suffixes / no-limit label / NODE-TAG headers were hardcoded English in a Russian chat, the `/add_device` platform instructions became real HTML code blocks instead of raw Markdown fences, and a `preformatted` block — which had **no case in either renderer** — now renders. Contracts are **behavioural**: they capture the payload the notifier **actually posts** to a stub server, which is precisely what the old suite never did (it marshalled its own map and asserted its own field names, and stayed green for two months while every rich send was rejected). **The gate itself then found the companion defect on B389's first run:** `scripts/check_b186.sh` contract **I** *asserted the rejected payload* (`check_ge "I-blocks" 1 … '"blocks":  blocks'`), so a green B186 was evidence **for** the bug — the contract is renegotiated (nested envelope + `html` + the **absence** of the old key, with the measured responses in its header) and B389 contract E9 fails if it is reverted. Companion deliverable: the `.dsh/skills/skygate-telegram-message-style/SKILL.md` skill + `docs/TELEGRAM.md` §9. 30 contracts in `scripts/check_b389_rich_message_wire.sh`.
- **B388** — (2026-10-15, closes TD-32) **the pipeline's verdict must be grep's, not the producer's — and the detector that was supposed to guard it had silently stopped reading.** TD-32 was carried as one item («`producer | grep -q` under `pipefail` in ~120 scripts»); measuring the **333** piped `grep -q` sites in `scripts/` separated it into **two hazards that need two remedies**. **H1 (SIGPIPE):** `grep -q` exits at the FIRST match and closes the pipe, a producer still writing dies with **141**, and `pipefail` reports the **pipeline** as failed — so a pattern that **is** present reads as absent. It needs a producer larger than the 64 KB pipe buffer **and** a pattern that can match early, and that is exactly how B387 failed its **own first CI run** (`code_only "$VP" | grep -qF …` over a ~110 KB file: MSYS passed, Linux did not). **H2 (POLARITY):** even with a small producer, `pipefail` makes a **failing** producer fail the pipeline — which **inverts** an output-presence test: `go build 2>&1 | grep -q .` asks «did the tool print anything?», a broken build is what prints, so the pipeline reported failure-of-the-wrong-kind, the «build is broken» branch was **skipped**, and **nine checks passed a broken tree**. Both are removed by neutralising pipefail for that one pipeline — `( set +o pipefail; producer | grep -q pat )` — which keeps the pipeline's shape, so grep still decides and the exit status is unchanged (0 matched / 1 not / 2 error) while the producer's status (141 from SIGPIPE, or 1 from a failing tool) can no longer decide anything. **The 15 sites MEASURED dangerous** (large producer + early-match pattern) are wrapped and pinned **by name**: `check_b237_22.sh` (build, vet, staticcheck), `check_b237_24.sh` (build, vet), `check_b_modules_admin.sh` (build, vet), `check_b_tailscale_module.sh` (build, vet), `check_b360_prefix_reservation.sh` (two repo-wide greps), `check_b378_push_gate_and_host_artefacts.sh` (gofmt), `check_b95.sh` (two `git ls-remote`), `verify_backup.sh` (find). **The other ~318 sites are SAFE, and the reason is recorded rather than assumed:** either the producer is bounded (a 3-byte `curl -w '%{http_code}'`, a docker name list, `grep -A<n>` over one file, `head`) or the pattern can only match the **last** line (`go test | grep -q '^ok'`), which forces grep to read everything before it can answer. Two more real sites came out of the widened detector and were fixed properly with a `case` builtin instead of the pipe (`check_b_tag_owners.sh` ×2 — the original B311 shape). **B386's detector was the other half of the story:** it matched only `printf`/`echo` producers, which is why it reported «0 unprotected sites» while B387 failed through that hole — and its heredoc state was **shared across files** (`BEGIN` runs once), so a script that ended with a heredoc open made every **later** file be skipped: a detector that silently stops reading is worse than none, and fixing it raised the honest count from 0 to 8. The predicate is now the property that matters (a producer that can outrun the pipe buffer **and** a pattern that can match early, plus the printf/echo-of-a-variable class), its planted-violation selftest gained those shapes, and a `||` chain is accepted as **not** a pipeline (matching the second bar of `||` was the first version's false positive). **What was deliberately NOT done:** the equivalent H1-only remedy (drop `-q`, redirect the grep's stdout so it reads all input — proved equivalent in the contract) was **not** applied tree-wide, because doing that mechanically needs a real shell parser and the obvious tokenizer corrupts text **inside quoted strings** — `"$( … sed 's/…"[^"]*"…' … )"` is re-parsed by the shell and no naive tokenizer models that; the first attempt here mangled message strings and B386's own contract C-section (a scenario it had already paid for once) before the damage was caught by diffing against HEAD. The wrapper needs no parsing at all. The `-q`-drop transform is still available and is proved equivalent; the wrapper is what shipped. 17 contracts in `scripts/check_b388_grep_q_pipeline.sh`, whose behavioural half builds a **deliberately broken Go package** and shows the old shape answering «nothing was printed» while the wrapped shape reports the failure, and reproduces the SIGPIPE case with a >64 KB producer.
- **B387** — (2026-10-15, found by verifying the applied release) **"the database did not answer" is not "your database is corrupt".** Measured minutes after the operator applied v1.5.127 to a docker-compose host: the post-deploy catalog printed `FAIL R30 skygate.db integrity_check FAILED: psql:error:couldnottranslatehostname skygate-pg-local toaddress:Temporaryfailureinnameresolution` followed by *«This is the corruption that triggered the R9 SKIP on 2026-07-30. Run `scripts/recover_db_corruption.sh` … to extract the data and rebuild a clean DB»* — about a database that was **provably healthy in the same session**: `pg_isready` answered *accepting connections*, `acl_snapshots` held **2071** rows, `/readyz` said `healthy:true (db+headscale ok)`, `/admin/exit-rules` served a **611 KB** page, `/admin/exit-nodes` read «политика headscale совпадает с текущей таблицей владельцев», «владелец не объявляет: 0», «3/3 здоровых», and the **same R30 query answered `ok`** when run from a container on the docker network. **The mechanism was two lines:** `INTEGRITY=$(psql_vm "SELECT …" 2>/dev/null | tr -d '[:space:]')` + `INTEGRITY="${INTEGRITY:-fail}"`. `psql_vm` preferred the host's `psql` (this host has `/usr/bin/psql`) and handed it the DSN hostname **verbatim** — and on a docker-compose install that hostname is a **compose service name** (`skygate-pg-local`) that resolves only **inside** the docker network, so the host's resolver failed, psql's error text *became* the answer, and every non-`ok` value meant "corrupt". **R9, R10 and R-B351 inherited it** — R10 printed the psql error as the *name of a missing grant* («missing per-user grants for: psql:error:couldnottranslatehostname…»). Two of this repo's own rules broke at once: **rule 1** (a check whose live dependency is unavailable must SKIP, never FAIL) and **trap #15** (a host transient must never masquerade as a product regression) — at the exact moment the operator runs the catalog. **Shipped:** (1) `psql_vm` is a **ladder** — the DSN host as a **local container** first (`docker exec … psql`, its own socket: no resolver, no bridge address, no image pull), else the host's `psql` **only when the host really resolves the DSN host**, else a throwaway container attached to **skygate's own network** (the pre-B387 fallback used `--network host`, i.e. the host's resolver again — the same bug one rung lower), every skipped rung naming its reason on stderr; (2) new `scripts/lib/post_deploy_truth.sh` **classifies** each read (`vpd_query_reached_db` / `vpd_no_answer_reason` / `vpd_ok_answer` / `vpd_is_tailnet_ipv4` / `vpd_pick_self_ipv4` / `vpd_headscale_health_url`) so **no answer ⇒ SKIP naming the reason** and only a real answer that is not `ok` can reach the corruption verdict; (3) **R5/R6/R7 stop asserting the reference deployment's topology** — they pinned the literal `100.64.100.10` and the bridge IP `172.18.0.3`, so on the operator's host (container at `100.64.0.10`, headscale reachable as the compose name `headscale` answering `{"status":"pass"}`) all three FAILed on a healthy tailnet; R5 now **discovers** the live address and checks the `100.64.0.0/10` range, R6 keys on the discovered address and SKIPs when there is none, R7 probes **the address the APP is configured with** (`SKYGATE_HEADSCALE_URL`, else the compose name); (4) `RESULTS_SKIP` is **initialised and printed** — it was incremented at the R26 skip without ever being set, so under `set -u` that path reported `RESULTS_SKIP: unbound variable` and the summary never reported SKIPs at all. **The contract is behavioural, because a source grep cannot tell "prints SKIP" from "prints SKIP and still exits 0":** the real classifier runs on planted inputs, and the **REAL catalog** is driven against a **stubbed host** (stub `ssh`/`scp`, the measured error text, the measured container address `100.64.0.10`) in three states — an unreachable database must **SKIP** and must **not** mention `recover_db_corruption`, a healthy one must **PASS**, and one that really answers `fail` must still **FAIL with the recovery advice** (so the fix cannot pass by silencing the verdict). **The block's own first CI run then failed the same catalog on the same commit that was green locally (435/0):** A4–A8 plus `grep: write error: Broken pipe`, because those assertions piped a ~110 KB producer into `grep -q` under `pipefail`, whose early exit killed the producer with SIGPIPE and turned a pattern that **is** present into "absent" — **trap #9, inside the block written to stop a checker reporting a plumbing failure as a verdict** (MSYS buffered differently and accepted all five; reproduced locally as `pipeline_rc=141` for a first-line match against `rc=0` for a last-line match on the same input). The producer is now captured once and every match is a `case` or a herestring, with contract **A14** pinning that about the file — which also proved **B386's guard was too narrow to have caught it**: that detector matched only `printf`/`echo` producers, while one widened to any producer finds **304 sites in ~120 check scripts**, now tracked as **TD-32** in `docs/ROADMAP.md` with the measured predicate (it bites only when the consumer leaves while the producer still writes, so a last-line match on a small producer is safe by accident) and B386's per-site method. 51 contracts in `scripts/check_b387_post_deploy_truth.sh`.
- **B386** — (2026-10-15) **no check may turn a host transient into a product verdict.** The bill was paid once already: `B311 A2` FAILED a full gate run on a tree that had passed the same gate twice an hour earlier, claiming «`internal/headscale/preauth.go` does not put tags in `acl_tags`» about a file whose line 241 is literally `body["acl_tags"] = tags`; it reproduced **0 times in 300 rounds** (0 in 400 under 8 CPU burners) and the two sibling assertions reading the same variable through the same form **passed** in that run. The assertion was `printf '%s' "$CODE" | grep -q '…'`: **`grep -q` exits at the FIRST match and closes the pipe**, so under `set -o pipefail` the pipeline's status is the **producer's**, not grep's — anything the host does to that pipe (EPIPE/SIGPIPE, a failed fork) is reported as *"the pattern is not there"*, i.e. a fact about the MACHINE turned into a claim about the PRODUCT, and the check had no way to fail honestly. **Shipped:** every `producer | grep -q` site in `scripts/check_*.sh` — **303 sites in 86 files** — rewritten, with each replacement **proved equivalent before it was applied** by a behavioural selftest that evaluated the OLD pipeline and the NEW expression on ten haystacks per site (empty, case variants, regex metacharacters, multi-line, quotes, backslashes) and compared exit statuses: **303 checked, 0 mismatches**. Four kinds by pattern shape: **R1/R2** literal (case-folded under `-i`) → `[[ $V == *'pat'* ]]` — no fork at all; **R3** alternation of literals → `[[ $V == *'a'* || … ]]` (also `-i` aware); **R4** any other regex → the same grep with its flags and pattern **verbatim** reading a herestring (`<<< "$V"`), so no producer is in the pipeline; **R5** trailing-newline-sensitive patterns, whole-line `-x`, or a **pattern variable** → `( set +o pipefail; printf … | grep … )` — the pipeline stays, its status cannot decide. **Audited and deliberately out of scope:** the `-o`/`-c`/`-n` sites, where grep reads all of its input (no early exit, no producer status to lose); comments and here-document bodies are never rewritten (a comment mentioning the pattern is documentation, heredoc text is data). **Two traps the block had to survive, both recorded in the contract and the release notes:** (1) the first rewriter matched `['"]…['"]` greedily, so the pattern `"href=\"/admin/$p\""` matched only `href=\"` and the rebuilt line became `grep -q "href=\" <<< "$deploy_block"/admin/$p\""` — **`bash -n` ACCEPTED it** (valid syntax, destroyed meaning), caught only by reviewing the diff; the rewriter is now a hand-rolled quote/escape-aware parser that replaces the matched span **whole or not at all**; (2) the second version rewrote COMMENT lines that merely mentioned the pipeline (a "pattern" of `` X` ``) and its alternation branch ignored `-i`, silently turning `grep -qi 'unknown\|stopped'` into a **case-sensitive** test — both caught by the selftest, which is why the transform is proved rather than eyeballed. Also fixed in passing: `check_b311_preauth_key_request_truth.sh` no longer greps a variable through a pipeline at all (its own incident), `TD-19` in `docs/ROADMAP.md` is now genuinely closed with the measurement, and `AGENTS.md` §2 trap 15 carries the lesson. Contract: `scripts/check_b386_no_variable_grep_q.sh` — 0 unprotected sites, the detector **exercised on planted violations** (a real pipeline, a commented-out line, a heredoc body, a status-neutralized line) so it cannot rot into a green no-op, and the five transforms re-proved on fresh haystacks.
- **B385** — (2026-10-14, found by the release itself) **a refusal must name the reason and the interpreter, not a plausible guess.** While releasing v1.5.124, `scripts/ci_gate.sh` answered `CANNOT VERIFY` **three times in a row** and named the wrong cause every time: (1) `the GitHub API query failed (repo=… workflow=… sha=…)` — no cause at all, because `query_runs` discarded the API call's stderr with `2>/dev/null`, and the line that explained everything was `gh` failing to READ the Windows credential store (`unable to get credential storage lock in 1000 ms: File exists`); (2) `'gh' is not authenticated (run: gh auth login, or set GH_TOKEN)` — the token was valid, the store was merely LOCKED at that instant, so the advice sent the operator to re-authenticate a working account and never named the real remedy (`GH_TOKEN="$(gh auth token)"`); (3) `'gh' CLI not on PATH` — true but incomplete, because it did not say WHICH interpreter was running, and on this workstation a bare `bash` is `C:\Windows\System32\bash`, the **WSL launcher**, whose PATH has no `gh` and whose `/c/Projects/skygate` is an **empty** directory (the real checkout is `/mnt/c/Projects/skygate`) — so a command that worked a minute earlier fails with nothing visibly changed. This is the misleading-verdict class B378 closed on the push gate and B381 in the sha lookup, in the one path that had not been converted. **Shipped:** every refusal names the **class** (`classify_auth`/`classify_api`: credential store locked vs genuinely not authenticated vs rate limit vs network/DNS), the **raw text** it was classified from, and the **interpreter/gh** it was observed with (`interpreter=… gh=… PATH head: …`); the locked-store case says explicitly that it is NOT "not logged in" and does not offer `gh auth login`; the API stderr is captured into a `mktemp` file instead of `/dev/null`; and a `say` line prints `interpreter=… gh=…` at the start of every run. Exit codes are unchanged (`0` green / `1` not green / `2` cannot verify), **no bypass switch was added** (rule 14), and the three sanctioned callers plus `release.yml`'s `preflight` still go through the single gate. **19 contracts** in `scripts/check_b385_ci_gate_reasons.sh`, whose behavioural half drives the REAL gate against a stubbed `gh` in seven modes (missing CLI, locked store, unauthenticated, rate limit, network, green, failed) using `$BASH` — never a bare `bash`, because a bare bash is the very trap the block documents. Also recorded in `AGENTS.md` §2 traps 14 (the WSL launcher) and 15 (a check must not let a host transient masquerade as a product regression) and in `docs/operations.md` §1.2.
- **B384.1** — (2026-10-14, found by verifying the applied release) **the `set` rung must not pass a flag this CLIENT does not have, and it is accepted only when the daemon confirms.** One tick after v1.5.123 was applied on the reference host, the same journal carried what B384 had just made visible: `tailscale-apply: 'tailscale set' failed (unclassified): flag provided but not defined: -advertise-tags`. Measured with `tailscale set --help` on **Tailscale 1.102.3** (the version the image ships): it offers `--hostname`, `--accept-routes`, `--accept-dns`, `--exit-node` … and **no `--advertise-tags`**, while `tailscale up` does have it — so the FIRST rung of B384's ladder could never succeed there: every boot and every 5-minute tick burned a failed invocation and printed a red "unclassified" line (the exact noise class B384 exists to remove), and the tags landed *only* because the ladder fell through to `up`. **The verification half earned its keep in the same breath:** it proved B384 works (`tailscale-apply: OK hostname=skygate-host tags=tag:dev-infra-skygate-host`, **0** occurrences of the old `requires mentioning all non-default flags` text) and it is what surfaced this defect — plus it names the original cause precisely: that refusal appears whenever the call does not mention the persisted `AdvertiseTags` pref, and B384's `up` invocation does mention it. **Shipped:** the client is ASKED once — `tailscaleSetSupportsAdvertiseTags()` (cached, with the pure `parseSetSupportsAdvertiseTags` split out so it is unit-testable without a `tailscale` binary) and `skygate_tc_set_supports_advertise_tags()` in the entrypoint — and the tag flag reaches `set` only when this client has it (`skygate_tc_set_tag_arg`, `tagCarriedBySet := tagArg != "" && tailscaleSetSupportsAdvertiseTags()`); the `set` rung is accepted **only when the daemon's own view then matches the intent** (`tailscaleSettingsMatch`, a QUIET single read — an intermediate verdict must not print a false `SETTINGS NOT APPLIED` right before `up` succeeds; `skygate_tc_try_set` returns 0 verified / 2 applied-but-differs / 1 failed), otherwise the ladder continues with `up`, which carries the tags, so a client WITH the flag still gets everything from `set` alone; and `flag provided but not defined` is its own classifier kind (`bad-flag`) in both halves instead of `(unclassified)`. **Verified live in the operator's container against the real daemon before the tag** (idempotent values, nothing changed): probe says no `--advertise-tags`; tag arg `[]` with support 0 and `[--advertise-tags=tag:dev-infra-skygate-host]` with support 1; `set rung rc=0` (applied AND verified); `verify: OK hostname=skygate-host tags=tag:dev-infra-skygate-host`. **Shell trap recorded in the code:** the rung's verdict must be read as `TC_SET_RC=$?` on the FIRST line of the `else` branch — a `$?` read after an `elif` test is the test's status, not the command's. **Contract:** `scripts/check_b384_tailscale_settings_apply.sh` grew 32 → **46** contracts; section G pins the probe in both halves, the conditional tag argument, the verify-gated `set` (all three rc paths), the `bad-flag` classification, and drives the probe and the rung against a **stubbed** `tailscale` whose help has no `--advertise-tags` (the live case) and one that has it. Docs: `docs/UPDATE.md` §4.1.2 (+RU) now says which command carries the tag on which client version. Operator note: the reference host's `.env` still carried the stale `SKYGATE_TS_HOSTNAME=skygate-host-1`; it was changed to `skygate-host` (backup `.env.bak-<ts>`, the container already received the canonical value from compose, so nothing restarted).
- **B384** — (2026-10-14, operator request) **the container's Tailscale settings must actually apply, and the node must say whether they did.** Operator request (verbatim): «добавить проверку "мой узел реально применил свои настройки"», following the recurring boot failure. **The evidence:** the journal carried, on **every boot and every 5-minute tick**, `tailscale-autostart: boot|tick: start FAILED: tailscale up: exit status 1 (out=Error: changing settings via 'tailscale up' requires mentioning all non-default flags. To proceed, either re-run your command with --reset or use the command below to explicitly mention the current va…)`, while the node itself looked perfect — `Self.HostName=skygate-host`, `Self.Tags=['tag:dev-infra-skygate-host']`, `BackendState=Running`, headscale node 87 online with that tag — so nothing appeared broken. **The truth was the opposite: nothing the invocation asked for was ever applied.** `tailscale up` refuses to change *any* setting unless the call mentions *every* persisted non-default pref, and this container's state carries `NoStatefulFiltering=true` and `NetfilterMode=2`, which no caller ever named — so an operator editing the hostname or the tags in the panel would wait forever, and the permanent failure line hid real errors. The entrypoint had the same bug **plus the retired name**: its advertise-tags fallback was the literal `tag:dev-infra-skygate-host-1,tag:private` — the `skygate-host-<n>` shape B320/B321 exist to remove — naming a node that does not exist while the real node stayed untagged. **Shipped (identical ladder in `entrypoint.sh` and `internal/feature/admin/tailscale_apply_b384.go`):** (1) `tailscale set …` **first** whenever the daemon answers — upstream's own help says *"Unlike 'tailscale up', this command does not require the complete set of desired settings"*; (2) `tailscale up …` kept for the **first join** (no state to drift from) and as the fallback; (3) `tailscale up --reset …` retried **once**, and only when the failure text is the full-flag refusal (`tailscalePrefDriftKind`), which is safe here because this invocation genuinely names every pref the container owns; (4) the tag is **derived, not hardcoded** — `tag:dev-infra-<canonical hostname>` through the one normaliser B320/B321 own, so `…-1` can never come back out, an existing tag set is preserved (the derived tag is **added**, because `--advertise-tags` **replaces** the set) and an explicit `SKYGATE_TS_ADVERTISE_TAGS` still wins verbatim (`desiredAdvertiseTags`); (5) **every** path ends in `verifyTailscaleSettings`, which reads `tailscale status --json` and logs one named line — `tailscale-apply: OK hostname=… tags=…` or `tailscale-apply: SETTINGS NOT APPLIED …` naming **both** sides — so the 5-minute re-assert reports the truth instead of a permanent failure. **The trap both halves had to learn (recorded in the files and the docs):** the live message is **wrapped across lines**, so `grep -q 'requires mentioning all non-default flags'` never matches it — the Go classifier returned `""` for the real text, caught by the unit test, and both classifiers now collapse whitespace (`tr '\n\t' '  ' | tr -s ' '` / `strings.Fields`) with the wrapped form pinned by `TestTailscalePrefDriftKind_B384` and contract **D4**. **A second real bug the tests caught:** `AdvertiseTagForHostname("skygate-host-1")` returned the retired `-1` tag until the helper canonicalised first. **Verified:** `bash -n` **and** `sh -n` clean; 5 unit tests (`tailscale_apply_b384_test.go`); the whole `internal/feature/admin` suite green (36.3 s); **32 contracts** in `scripts/check_b384_tailscale_settings_apply.sh`, including a behavioural half that extracts the same shell helpers the container runs (normalisation `skygate-host-1`/`skygate-host-1-1` → `skygate-host`, derivation, the WRAPPED drift text, and `verify()` against a **stubbed** `tailscale` in both directions — a matching node says OK, one extra tag fails loudly). Contract **F** asserts the file is git-tracked (trap #11), registered in `scripts/verify_pre_deploy.sh`, and documented in `RELEASE-NOTES.md`, `docs/ROADMAP.md` (+RU) and `docs/UPDATE.md` §4.1.2 (+RU) with the `SKYGATE_TS_ADVERTISE_TAGS` knob. *Contract trap recorded in the script:* a stub written as `${VAR:-{"Self":…}}` leaks a `}` through bash brace counting and produces invalid JSON — use `if [ -n "${VAR:-}" ]`. **Note for the operator:** the host `.env` still carries `SKYGATE_TS_HOSTNAME=skygate-host-1`; it is normalised and logged, but the canonical value silences the warning (the compose/container env already supplies `skygate-host`).
- **B383** — (2026-10-14, operator report) **a container start must not spend 18 minutes re-fetching the world, and it must never look dead while it does.** Operator report: «после обновления skygate не отображает вебпанель». Measured on the host: the container started **06:32:47** and the process only listened at **06:50:07** — an **18-minute window with nothing bound to `:8080`**, so the browser saw a dead port while the app itself was healthy (`/healthz` = v1.5.121, `/login` = 200, LAN reachable, no restarts). The image **deliberately does not prebuild skygate** (`Dockerfile`: *“Build happens at container start via entrypoint”*), so every recreate recompiles from the bind-mounted source — and four things turned that into an outage: (1) both Go caches lived **inside** the container (`/go/pkg/mod` 570 MB + `/root/.cache/go-build` 261 MB) and died with it; (2) `go mod download` then went to `proxy.golang.org`, the connection **timed out**, and the retry loop is what burned the 18 minutes (no message, no bound); (3) `go mod tidy` ran on **every** start against the operator's bind-mounted checkout — network, and it **rewrites `go.mod`/`go.sum`**, the files rule 7 protects; (4) `apk add openssh-client git` ran every start although the Dockerfile already installs both (an alpine-CDN round trip in the boot path). Measured on the same host: `go mod download` **0.03 s** warm, full rebuild **3.1 s** warm / **48 s** with a cold build cache, unchanged tree **0.4 s**. **Shipped:** the caches move to `SKYGATE_GO_CACHE_ROOT` (default **`/data/go-caches`**, the bind mount that already survives a recreate) with `SKYGATE_GOMODCACHE`/`SKYGATE_GOCACHE` overrides and a **real fallback** to the container-local defaults when the root is unusable, so a read-only `/data` behaves as before; the download is bounded by `SKYGATE_GO_MOD_TIMEOUT` (900 s) and its failure names the incomplete cache, the unreachable proxy and the three ways out; `go mod tidy` is **opt-in** (`SKYGATE_GO_MOD_TIDY=1`); `apk` runs only when git/ssh are missing; an unchanged tree **skips the build** via a stamp holding the source fingerprint **and** the binary's size+mtime (so a replaced binary is never reused) with `SKYGATE_FORCE_BUILD=1` to override; while a build runs, a provisional server answers `/healthz` with `{"status":"booting","phase":"building","ready":false}` and **deliberately no build label**, so no poller can mistake “compiling” for “up” (B269's “bind the socket first”, one level lower — at the entrypoint, where it was missing); `SKYGATE_BUILD_PLAN=1` prints the decision (“why is it rebuilding?”). **The in-app update path is untouched by construction:** the fingerprint is `git rev-parse HEAD` + `git status --porcelain`, and the panel's updater does exactly `git checkout <target>` in that tree, so it always changes → the rebuild always runs and the build label still comes from `git describe`. 33 contracts in `scripts/check_b383_entrypoint_build_budget.sh`, whose **behavioural half runs the same functions the container runs** (extracted between the `B383 helpers` markers): the cache fallback, the fingerprint on a real git repo (stable / uncommitted edit / new commit) and seven build-decision cases; section E pins the strings `B237.15` C.2 and `B91` depend on, so this rewrite cannot silently break the other deployment variants.
- **B382** — (2026-10-13, closes RR-2 + RR-3, half of TD-30) **the update path must not name things that do not exist, and it must not call a crashed unit a broken install.** Three operator-facing truths, each wrong in a way that costs real time. **(RR-2) the in-app manual steps downloaded files the release never published.** `GenerateBareSteps` (`internal/update/manual.go`) told a bare-install operator to fetch `…/releases/download/<tag>/skygate-linux-amd64` and `…/skygate-linux-amd64.sha256`; **neither has ever been a release asset.** The pipeline publishes `skygate-<tag>-<os>-<arch>.tar.gz` (with the `skygate` binary at the **archive root**) plus ONE `SHA256SUMS`, and that file lists the **ARCHIVES**, not the binaries inside them — so step 3 of a documented procedure answered 404, and even a corrected `curl` would have failed the documented `sha256sum -c`, because four of the five listed archives are not on the operator's disk. The steps now build the published tarball name from the tag, fetch `SHA256SUMS`, verify **only** the archive they have (`grep 'skygate-<tag>-linux-amd64.tar.gz' SHA256SUMS | sha256sum -c -`, portable to GNU *and* busybox), and extract the binary under the versioned name the rest of the steps use, so the running binary is never overwritten. `TestGenerateBareSteps` no longer merely checks that the owner/repo appears in the URL — it **requires** the published tarball + `SHA256SUMS` and rejects the dead names — and contract A6 cross-checks the asset shape against `.github/workflows/release.yml`, the workflow that **builds** them, so `manual.go` and the pipeline cannot drift apart silently. **(RR-3) the applier turned our own crash into "your install is broken".** `restart_service` in `deploy/skygate-apply-update.sh` ran `systemctl restart` directly, and a unit that crashed is **latched in `failed`**, where systemd refuses to act on it (trap #6, seen live in the native-update rollback drill) — so the applier stopped a healthy install, failed to bring it back, and reported only *"the service did not come up"*, sending the operator to the journal for a product problem that does not exist. It now clears the latch with `systemctl reset-failed` **before** the restart, **inside** the `MODE = systemd` branch, **only when** `systemctl is-failed --quiet` says so (a healthy unit is untouched, which makes the log line evidence rather than noise); a non-zero reset is a **warning** that still lets the restart try, and OpenRC keeps its `start` fallback. **(TD-30, the cheap half) a hang looked like a failed assertion.** B132 killed its `go test` at Go's own **implicit** 10-minute-per-package default (`601.705s`, `FlushFileBuffers` blocked inside a SQLite migration) and named neither a budget nor a test; B378 fixed the MESSAGE, and this block makes the BUDGET explicit — `-timeout` from `SKYGATE_GO_TEST_TIMEOUT` (default `10m` per package, the smallest safe floor given the measurement, overridable without editing a gate script), printed in **both** verdicts, with the reader saying the budget is the caller's own. The measured COST is unchanged and still recorded: `exit_rules` 214 s + `admin` 68 s + `i18n` 0.6 s, fsync-heavy because every test migrates a fresh SQLite DB (the remaining TD-30 work is that cost, not the message). 30 contracts in `scripts/check_b382_update_path_truth.sh`, two behavioural: the Go test that RENDERS the real steps, and the shape-exact hang dump fed to the verdict reader (rc=2, the test name, the new budget line). **Its own first CI run then refused the commit on a RACE, and the race is fixed here too:** `--- FAIL: TestB375_YesterdaysRunDoesNotBlockToday … testing.go:1369: TempDir RemoveAll cleanup: unlinkat /tmp/…/002: directory not empty` — `tick` is synchronous but `runScheduled` hands the upgrade to a goroutine, and the B375 fixture returned at the terminal **PHASE** while that goroutine was still persisting state transitions, so `testing.TempDir`'s `RemoveAll` deleted directories under a live writer (the same commit passed on re-run — what a race looks like). `scheduler.go` now tracks the goroutine with `scheduledRunWG` (incremented **before** the `go` statement, so a join cannot miss it) and `b375AwaitTerminal` **joins** it before returning; nothing in production calls `Wait` — the counter exists so no fixture can leak a writer into a directory it is about to delete. Verified with 15 repetitions of the three goroutine-spawning fixtures, 5 repetitions of the package and `-race` clean.
- **B381** — (2026-10-13, closes TD-31) **the CI gate must look up the sha it was asked about.** Measured while releasing v1.5.119: the push was green (`428 passed, 0 failed`) and `ci.yml` was **running** for `124f54ef`, yet `bash scripts/ci_gate.sh --sha 124f54ef` answered `NOT GREEN — no 'ci.yml' run found for 124f54ef (was the commit pushed with --no-verify, or is CI disabled for it?)`. `scripts/ci_gate.sh` puts the value it was given straight into GitHub's query `repos/<repo>/actions/workflows/ci.yml/runs?head_sha=<sha>`, and that filter compares the **full 40-character** value: a prefix matches NOTHING, the API answers with an empty list, and the `total -eq 0` branch reported that empty answer as *"the commit was never tested"* — with a diagnosis naming a completely different situation. The verdict **fails safe** (it refuses; it never published), but it is wrong and it sends the operator hunting for a bypass that never happened: the misleading-verdict class B378 closed on the push gate. **Measured impact, stated rather than assumed:** both sanctioned callers already pass a **full** sha — `.githooks/pre-tag` receives `$2` from git, `tag-release.yml` runs `git rev-parse HEAD` — so **tagging was never at risk**; the defect is confined to a hand-invoked `--sha <prefix>`, which is exactly how an operator asks *"was the commit I just pushed tested?"*. **Shipped:** a short sha is canonicalised through the local object database (`git rev-parse --verify --quiet "<sha>^{commit}"`) **before** the ancestry check and **before** the API query; a sha that does not resolve is reported as a limitation of the **query** (`the head_sha filter needs the full 40-character sha`) and never as a fact about CI; and the same principle one step earlier — the ancestry check ran `git merge-base --is-ancestor` without first asking whether the commit was even present, so a missing object became the claim `<sha> is not an ancestor of origin/main`, and an unresolvable commit is now **exit 2 (cannot verify)**, the status both callers must refuse on. Exit codes are otherwise untouched (`0` green / `1` not green / `2` cannot verify), so the whole **B280** D-matrix keeps its verdicts — that check is re-run green. 24 contracts in `scripts/check_b381_ci_gate_sha_resolution.sh`, including the mutation tooth: a stubbed `gh` records the URL it was handed, so deleting the canonicalisation turns B1/B2/B3 red, and D4/D5 pin the missing-object case. **G1–G3 guard the catalog's OWN registration**, and they exist because B381 paid a full 55-minute gate run for one character: its first push was refused with `CATALOG ERROR: run_check "B381" has no command argument (a missing line-continuation backslash?)` — the description line ended at its closing quote instead of `" \`, so bash called `run_check` with two arguments and the check counted as a FAIL. The safety net worked (the gate refused the push — the whole point of B378), but the defect is visible in the file in three seconds, so G1 flags any `run_check` line whose unescaped double quotes number exactly four and which ends at the closing quote — that is `"id"` + `"description"` and nothing else (`"$name" "$desc" "$@"` and a complete one-liner both carry six) — G2 proves the detector on a planted set, and G3 keeps `bash -n` honest. G1 was verified by mutation against the REAL catalog, not only a synthetic sample. The behaviour half runs in a scratch git repository with a real commit, uses no network, and keeps every scratch file under `mktemp -d` (trap #13).
- **B380** — (2026-10-13, operator request) **the operator must be able to group the rules they create by THEIR OWN NAME, not only by domain.** Operator request (verbatim): «возможность добавить в exit rules для пользователей группировать те правила что они задают так им будет проще ориентироваться не только по домену но и по пользовательскому названию». Three things about it are contracts rather than taste. **(1) The label must not reach routing.** The page already carries two DERIVED groupings — CDN ranges (B237.22) and the typed destination (B376) — and this one is **user data**, so the rename action is asserted to contain **no ACL write** and `internal/acl` + `internal/prefixowner` are asserted **not to read the column at all**: a rename that could re-route traffic is a trap. **(2) The label must survive the derivation:** a domain rule becomes a domain row **plus** its resolved `/32` rows, and the `/32` rows are what the page lists — the label is written to both (and to the B276.1 all-devices fan-out copies) from one shared variable, so inheritance is a property rather than a hope. **(3) « Игры» and «Игры » must be ONE group:** whitespace-only and case-only differences are how a free-text grouping feature loses trust, so `NormaliseGroupLabel` (trim, collapse inner runs, control characters become **spaces rather than being deleted** — deleting a separator silently glues words together, cap 40 runes and rune-safe) is enforced at the **writers**, not only at the form, and the view and the rename fold case the same way (`groupLabelKey` / `LOWER()`). **Shipped:** v0.79 `device_rules.group_label` in **both** chains (additive `DEFAULT ''` + a `(user_id, group_label)` index), the column in every `/my` and `/admin` SELECT, a third view (`?view=group`) rendering named groups alphabetically with the **ungrouped bucket LAST**, a rename/clear action (`POST /my/exit-rules/group`, owner-scoped, refuses an empty source, audit `exit_rule_group_rename`), group fields on the single form, the batch form (batch-level: a positional CSV column of free text would be ambiguous, so per-rule groups come from JSON `group`) and the admin form (the TARGET user's label), the field in the API response and request, and a `datalist` of the user's own existing names so the same group cannot drift into two by a typo. 6 tests on a real migrated SQLite DB (incl. a **decoy row of a second user** the rename must not move, and the derived-`/32` inheritance) + contracts in `scripts/check_b380_rule_groups.sh`.
- **B379** — (2026-10-13, closes TD-29) **the admin per-rule delete must exist, and must delete what the owner's own page deletes.** Measured 2026-10-11 while B376 was being written: both delete controls on `/admin/exit-rules` post to `/admin/exit-rules/delete` and **no `mux.Handle` registered that path** — a grep over the whole tree found the two forms and zero server-side matches, so every admin delete, of any rule, of any user, answered **404**, and there was no way at all to remove a rule from another user's device (`PostDeleteExitRule` is registered only under `/my/exit-rules/delete` and deletes by the row's own `user_id`, so it can only ever delete the CALLER's rules). **Shipped:** the route is registered behind `authMW` and the handler re-checks `IsAdmin`; the risk was never the route but the **cascade**, so the handler copies the owner's own decision tree instead of inventing one — an `all_devices` row cascades the whole fan-out group (B277.4), a domain row takes its derived `/32` children (B123/B184 — they are what the ACL pins and the auto-updater would re-add the domain on its next tick), anything else deletes one row — and all three helpers are scoped by `user_id`, a scope that is load-bearing ACROSS users. New `db.GetRuleAdminContext` is the one helper that reads a rule **without** a user filter, precisely so the handler learns the owner first and then calls the same three owner-scoped deletes the owner's page calls (parity by construction, not by review); the single-row delete is re-scoped through `DeleteRuleForUser`, so a row that changed hands between the SELECT and the DELETE cannot be removed by accident. The operator is told **which** cascade fired (`deleted` / `deleted_cascade` / `deleted_fanout`, three new keys in BOTH catalogues), a PARTIAL delete is warned about instead of passing as a complete one, and a missing rule produces a message instead of the `/my` path's silent skip. 6 handler tests against a real migrated SQLite DB pin it behaviourally — including a **decoy row owned by a second user** that the cascade must not touch, and a legacy domain row with an EMPTY `parent_domain` that deletes as one row exactly as it does on `/my` (the boundary of the parity, pinned rather than guessed). 20 contracts in `scripts/check_b379_admin_rule_delete.sh`.
- **B378** — (2026-10-12, operator instruction) **a push is ALWAYS checked, its failure is diagnosed, and it is fixed immediately — never bypassed.** Operator instruction (verbatim): «Пуш должен всегда проверяться и выявлять причины не работы и правится сразу». Written down because v1.5.116 was pushed with `git push --no-verify`, and the honest reason was **four host artefacts, none of them a product defect**: (1) `check_b352_ownership_convergence.sh` D2 asked `command -v python3`, and on Windows that is the Microsoft Store alias **stub** — it resolves, runs NOTHING and exits 49, so the parsed value was empty, the `case` fell through to `*)`, and the check announced «the button's body still reaches the effective-target resolver» about **correct** code (a host artefact printed as a product fact — the class B327/B336 forbid); the interpreter is now **probed for function** and a missing/broken one SKIPs that half with a named reason while the two greps and the test pin keep enforcing the contract. (2) `check_b_prod_health.sh` read `${USER}` bare under `set -u`, so a PowerShell-launched Git Bash (which exports `PATH` but not `USER`) died with «USER: unbound variable» **after** its four HTTP contracts had PASSed, and B327's behavioural half — which drives that very script — went red with it; it now falls back through `${LOGNAME}` to `id -un`. (3) `check_b337_gofmt_ratchet.sh` C1 asked `gofmt` about a Windows working tree carrying CRLF while the **index** is LF (`gitattributes eol=lf`) and named **22 correctly formatted files** as drifted; B375 had already recorded this as «the KNOWN local C1 CRLF false failure», i.e. it was *tolerated* for a release instead of fixed — the ratchet now re-checks a CR-stripped copy, so only REAL drift fails and the checkout artefact is printed as a note. (4) B144 needs `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8` on MSYS (trap #10) and the hook left the locale to the host. **Shipped:** the hook captures the catalog's output, reads the verdict through the new `scripts/push_gate_verdict.sh` (a RED log → names the failing checks; a log with **no** verdict block → FAILURE, not a pass; a TIMEOUT → failure; and the catalog's status comes from `PIPESTATUS[0]`, never from the `tee` in the pipeline), keeps an explicit `LC_ALL`, and still refuses the push on RED — 15 contracts in `scripts/check_b378_push_gate_and_host_artefacts.sh` (26 after the second half below), three of them behavioural (the verdict reader against planted green/red/timeout/crashed logs; B352 with a `python3` that runs nothing; `check_b_prod_health.sh` with `USER` and `LOGNAME` unset). The check deliberately never executes the hook — that would run the catalog from inside the catalog. **THE SECOND HALF OF THE BLOCK WAS FOUND BY PUSHING THE RELEASE ITSELF:** the first checked push ran the whole catalog and failed `B132`, whose message was «go test FAILED: 2026/10/13 07:27:44 all-devices: propagated 1 rule row(s) …» plus «FAIL skygate/internal/feature/exit_rules 601.705s» — nothing saying the run **TIMED OUT**, nothing naming the test, and `601.705s` recognisable only by hand as Go's own 10-minute-per-package default. Re-running the tree with `-timeout 120s` produced the dump that named the cause: `panic: test timed out after 2m0s` / `running tests: TestB2761_MarkGroupKeepsTheIntent` / … `skygate/internal/db.addColumnIfMissingSQLite` → `ExecContext` → `modernc.org/sqlite` → `_winSync` → `syscall.FlushFileBuffers` — a **host filesystem stall inside a test's SQLite migration** (the same test passes in 2.6 s on the same host minutes later; the three packages take ~283 s on a quiet machine and the suite is fsync-heavy because every test migrates a fresh SQLite DB). The FAIL was real — a gate must not go green on a hang — but the MESSAGE was a product-shaped lie that sends the operator hunting a regression that does not exist. New `scripts/go_test_verdict.sh` reads `go test` output and answers what the old message could not — **OK** / **FAILED** (naming the first failing test) / **HUNG** (naming the test running at the alarm **and** the blocking frame, deepest in our code when the stack reaches it) / **NO VERDICT** (a build error is neither a pass nor a test failure) — and `check_b132.sh` contract G routes its output through it. 26 contracts, eleven of them behavioural.
- **B377** — (2026-10-11, operator request) **an AI assistant must be able to help with rules from ONE LINK.** Operator request (verbatim): «ранее был функционал по формированию в skygate инструмента для управлением правилами через ИИ помощника, передавался токен и адрес для ИИ чтобы тот мог помочь настроить правила … пользователь копирует в чат ссылку с токеном, а агент уже пройдя по ссылке получает пакет с командами и помогает сформировать правила для таблиц». Measured before the change: a personal API token and `Authorization: Bearer` existed (`/my/tokens` + `handlers.currentUser`) but the credential could only travel in a **header**, so "paste this link into a chat" could not work at all; the help page's AI section was a prompt the **operator** assembled by hand; and that page was the only one that never printed its base URL — `{{.BaseURL}}` was used in six places and **nothing injected it**, while the catalogue string printed the literal `{{.BaseURL}}` because the `t` helper does not re-expand templates. **Shipped:** (1) `GET /my/exit-rules/agent` renders the package built by `agent_brief.go` — role, credentials (and *where* they came from), the API contract with a ready-to-edit request body, the constraints that are not guessable (**one relay per prefix** — headscale's own rule, an existing rule is a duplicate and **not** an error, a device with no exit-node preference has **inert** rules until the reconciler assigns a relay, domains are re-resolved while a **versioned** hostname is not, a CDN domain expands into ranges shared with unrelated sites, `deny` is informational) — plus the **live state**: every device with its id/hostname/counts/relay, the relays, the caps and the current usage, and the existing rules grouped by destination with the devices as tags; Markdown by default, the same data as JSON with `?format=json`; (2) authentication is cookie **or** Bearer **or** `?token=`, and the `?token=` allowance is pinned to this ONE path inside `handlers.currentUser` next to the bcrypt walk (extracted as `claimsForAPIToken`, so there is one implementation); the route is deliberately **not** behind the session-shaped `authMW` (it would redirect an assistant's fetch to `/login`) and the handler's own **401** is the gate; (3) secret hygiene: `no-store` + `no-referrer`, the token is never echoed into the body, revoking the token disarms the link; (4) the operator's side: `/my/tokens` gains a real token-created page (`my_token_created.html`, i18n RU+EN, replacing the pre-refactor bare fragment with hardcoded Russian) that builds the agent link **in the browser**, and `/my/exit-rules/help` gains a connect-by-link section with a browser-side builder plus a `<details>` fallback for assistants that cannot open links; (5) `GetExitRulesAPIHelp` finally passes `BaseURL` and `ai_base_url` became a `%s` key, so the six curl examples stop printing an empty origin. 25 contracts in `scripts/check_b377_agent_brief.sh` (A–H) + 6 unit tests in `internal/feature/exit_rules/agent_brief_test.go`. **The same block fixed a documented-but-absent field:** `/my/exit-rules/help` and `docs/api.md` had documented `parent_domain` since the API shipped while `apiRule` never carried it, so an assistant reading the contract could not tell a hand-typed `/32` from one the auto-updater derived — `apiRule` now returns it (`omitempty`, so a manual rule gains no key) and `docs/api.md`'s stale GET example (a bare array with `exit_node_id`/`enabled`/`created_at`, none of which the code ever returned) was corrected to the real `{"rules":[…]}` shape. **Verified end-to-end on a local stand** (SQLite + a stub headscale): the link returned a package naming `cyborg` (id=9) and `skyworker` (id=29), and `POST /my/exit-rules/api` with the same token added **18 game rules** (6 subnets + 3 backend domains on each of the two devices) in one batch.
- **B376** — (2026-10-11, operator request) **exit rules must be installable as a BATCH and readable BY TARGET.** Operator request (verbatim): «помоги корректно сделать рефактор отображения правил на странице exit rules и рефактор их установки у пользователя и админа — сейчас не удобно отображение по группам у пользователя и нет возможности набрасывать правила группой». Measured before the change: `/my/exit-rules` rendered ONE tree (`device → exit node → CDN group → CIDR rows`), so *"which of my machines route this destination, and through which relay?"* meant opening a section per device and comparing by eye — the same destination on two devices appeared twice, in two branches, with no visual link; adding N destinations was N form submits per device (the REST API accepted a batch since `api.go`, but it is documented on a help page and speaks JSON); the admin page added ONE destination at a time for a device id typed by hand and could not drive the tree's `<details>` together. **Shipped:** (1) `target_view.go` — `GroupRulesByTarget` projects the same rule slice into **one row per destination** with the devices as tags (a CDN rule groups by the domain the operator typed, accept and deny never merge, one device appears once with a ×N row count); the by-target list is the **default** (`?view=device` keeps the tree) and both views read the **same slice** so they cannot disagree about scope; (2) `bulk_values.go` — `ParseBulkValues` / `ParseBulkPayload` accept a pasted list (one value per line, the type derived per entry, a CIDR normalised to its network address), CSV/TSV columns (`value` | `value,action` | `type,value` | `type,value,action` | `device,type,value,action`, header row skipped) and JSON (`{rules:[…]}`, a bare array, or `{values:[…]}`) — per-line problems are reported while the good rows are kept, and `CanonicalBulkValue` refuses a CIDR in the ip column with a message naming the right one; (3) `bulk_add.go` — the single-rule installation core moved **out of** `PostMyExitRule` into `addRuleTarget`, shared by the single form and the new `POST /my/exit-rules/bulk` + `POST /admin/exit-rules/bulk`, so DNS resolution, `/32` derivation, `parent_domain` bookkeeping and duplicate classification cannot drift (the surface carries **exactly one** `net.LookupHost(targetValue)`); **one** ACL apply, **one** audit row, **one** notification and **one** route sync per batch; the caps are enforced per ROW against a **live counter**, so a paste larger than the budget stops at the budget and the banner says so; the admin picker is built from `node_owner_map` with exit nodes excluded (B265); (4) **no schema change and no new SQL** — the batch goes through `insertRuleUnique` + `db.AppendDeviceRule`, and the admin batch deliberately does **not** write the live policy (the existing admin single path leaves that to the reconciler); (5) both pages gain **collapse/expand-all** for every level of their trees; (6) 41 new keys in **BOTH** catalogues (RU+EN). 32 contracts in `scripts/check_b376_exit_rules_batch_and_target_view.sh` + 4 render tests in `internal/handlers/exit_rules_render_b376_test.go` (the real templates execute with a synthetic context, so a dropped context field fails a test instead of the page). The by-target view exists on **both** pages — on `/admin/exit-rules` every device tag carries its OWNER (`michail/basic`), and its per-rule expander deliberately has **no delete control**: the tree's delete button posts to `/admin/exit-rules/delete`, a path no `mux.Handle` registers (found here, filed as **TD-29** in `docs/ROADMAP.md`).
- **B360** — (2026-10-07, live PostgreSQL deployment) **a failover must be a RESERVATION, and a recovered exit node must get its prefixes back.** Live: during the karolina outage the operator set the panel override (`global_settings.prefix_owner_force_relay = emilia`, audit `prefix_owner_force`, 2026-10-06 19:49:23Z), `prefix_owner` held 139 rows all `emilia`/`source='global'` while `LoadClaims` saw 194 ip/subnet claims naming karolina — the data plane disagreed with the rules for ~19 hours; karolina came back (all three relays `online`, `healthy=1`, routes approved) and **nothing returned**, because the move had never been recorded as a failover and the override is a latch with no expiry and no surface; clearing it by hand made the very next pass return all 139 prefixes (`changed=139`, ACL regenerated) — the engine could return, it had no memory and no obligation to re-ask. **V078** adds `prefix_owner.failover_from` / `failover_at` / `failover_flaps` in BOTH chains (additive, SQLite through `execSQLiteDDL`): a prefix taken from a relay outside the healthy set records where it came from, a prefix whose previous owner was HEALTHY and which moves anyway CLEARS the reservation (L-60: a reservation must not outlive its reason), and `source='manual'` is never reserved and never returned. The pure planner `PlanReturns` hands a prefix back only when the reserved relay is healthy (B273 `online`/`untagged`, never `degraded`), PROVEN (B309 `relay_apply_state` = `ok`), still claims the prefix, and has been continuously healthy for the hysteresis (10 min; `hysteresis * 2^flaps`, capped 6 h) — otherwise it stays reserved and is reported with a NAMED reason (`return-not-healthy|not-proven|no-longer-claimed|too-soon|quarantined|overridden|manual-pin`), never silently dropped (L-54). Anti-flap: a return followed by a failover inside the 30-minute quarantine increments a streak that PERSISTS in the table, so a flapping relay cannot yank its prefixes back every 10 minutes. The override stays an operator tool: never auto-cleared, with its age and the assignment it displaces readable (`DescribeOverride`, `WouldBeAssignmentsWithoutForce`), and a warning once per 6-hour window while it overrides relays that are healthy again. The pass runs inside `ReconcileWithPreference`, which the existing maintenance tick already calls (L-60 convergence), and is idempotent. **Measured deviation:** `exit_node_health.last_state_change_at` is written on EVERY tick (`computeSnapshot` sets it to `now`), so it cannot serve as a "healthy since" value — `prefixowner.HealthySince` reads the durable `exit_node_state_changes` transition log instead, with the snapshot stamp only as a fallback; the one-line monitor fix (`if prev.State == snapshot.State { snapshot.LastStateChangeAt = prev.LastStateChangeAt }`) is a follow-up. 14 contracts in `scripts/check_b360_prefix_reservation.sh` + the scripted health timelines in `internal/prefixowner/reservation_b360_test.go`; V078 also paid one B337 entry (`internal/db/migrations_sqlite_schema_test.go` left the gofmt allowlist, budget 260 → 259)
