- **B375** — (2026-10-10, live defect) **the scheduled auto-update must actually run — the goroutine is armed unconditionally, the DATABASE decides, and the page can finally say whether the scheduler is running.** Measured on the reference deployment (PostgreSQL): `global_settings` held `update_schedule_enabled = 1` and `update_schedule_time = 08:00` (the operator had set exactly that in the panel), the container env held `SKYGATE_UPDATE_SCHEDULE_ENABLED=false` and `/home/skyadmin/skygate/.env` did not carry the variable at all, and the container journal held **ZERO** `update-scheduler:` lines since the container started — the scheduler goroutine had **never been created**. `cmd/skygate/main.go` gated the whole block on `if cfg.UpdateScheduleEnabled` (the ENV value) while `internal/update/scheduler.go`'s own header documents the DB-persisted toggle as the authority, so the panel wrote keys nothing read; the else-branch then claimed `/admin/update page can enable` it — **false**: that page writes `global_settings`, it cannot start a goroutine. **Shipped:** (1) the scheduler is **ARMED UNCONDITIONALLY** in `main.go` (the goroutine always exists; `cfg` stays the fallback for an install that never opened the panel, so untouched installs are unchanged) and the arming is recorded in process-level race-safe state (`update.SetSchedulerArmed` / `SchedulerArmed()` / `SchedulerArmedReason()` / `SchedulerTrap()`), which the admin service reads because it must not import `cmd/`; (2) the boot log is a pure, unit-tested function (`update.SchedulerBootLog`) that says armed-or-not, what the DB says, that the env default is in use when no row exists, names `/admin/update` as the place the schedule is switched, and for the not-armed case says plainly that no page can fix it; (3) the pre-B375 exact-minute predicate is replaced by a **bounded catch-up window** (`CatchUpWindow = 10m`: due at-or-after the slot and strictly before `slot+window`, deduplicated per slot per **DAY** via `ranTodayAt`, with `late by Nm` logged) and `Start` runs one tick immediately so a recreate at the scheduled minute no longer loses the day; (4) the non-Docker skip in `runScheduled` is **VISIBLE** (`logScheduledSkip` names the detected install kind, and `tick` asks before the GitHub check so an unreachable GitHub cannot hide the reason); (5) `/admin/update` renders «планировщик запущен / не запущен» with its reason, plus a red banner for the trap «расписание включено, но планировщик не запущен», in **both** catalogues (RU+EN). Tests: `internal/update/scheduler_b375_test.go` against a real SQLite DB (`db.OpenWithDialect` + `db.ApplyMigrations`) — DB toggle off ⇒ no tick action even when the env default is true; toggle on + exact minute ⇒ runs (state store reaches a terminal phase); late by 5m ⇒ runs; late by 20m ⇒ does not; already ran today for that slot ⇒ does not; yesterday's stamp does not block today; a DB schedule enabled while the env default is false ⇒ armed and active; the boot sentence and the armed state each have their own decision tables. `scripts/check_b130.sh` contract C was **renegotiated** — it now requires the env guard to be ABSENT (the guard WAS the defect) and requires `SetSchedulerArmed` — and `scripts/gofmt_legacy_allowlist.txt` paid two entries down (`internal/update/scheduler_test.go` from the renegotiated test, `internal/update/state.go` from the `StateStore.Get()` snapshot fix that makes the B375 suite `-race`-clean), `FROZEN=249 → 247`.
- **B374** — (2026-10-09, live outage) **the advertised routes must have a PERIODIC owner, and a stale advertisement that could not be revoked must be a named fact.** Measured on the reference deployment: the container was recreated at 14:08 UTC, the first route-apply pass could not reach karolina (SSH `100.64.0.2:18022` timed out), so the B352 machinery excluded her and moved **197 prefixes to emilia** in `prefix_owner` and re-pinned the device preferences to `tag:dev-infra-emilia` — and **emilia's `--advertise-routes` was never pushed**. `headscale` showed `emilia available=2 approved=2` (only `0.0.0.0/0` + `::/0`) while karolina still held `199/199`, `global_settings[relay_apply_state:emilia]` kept its **13:01:07** timestamp for 75+ minutes, and the container log held **ZERO** lines containing `advertis` or `staggeredSync`. Every device whose ACL grant pins `via=[tag:dev-infra-emilia]` (`skyworker`) lost YouTube/Telegram and every other pinned prefix; a manual "Re-sync all" fixed it in seconds (emilia 205/205, karolina 199→2, both `relay_apply_state = ok`). **Root cause:** the ONLY periodic caller of `StaggeredSync()` was the domain auto-updater loop (`internal/handlers/handlers.go`, `RunDomainAutoUpdater`), gated on `added > 0 || removed > 0`, and that tick legitimately logged `auto-updater: 18 domain(s) skipped (re-resolve interval 6h0m0s…)` every five minutes — so no route sync ran at all; **domain churn must not be the only trigger of the data plane**. **Secondary defect:** the ACL drift path deferred its re-apply with `periodic drift check (the assignment table did not move)` — a false statement, the table HAD moved minutes earlier — on the 30-minute derived-row churn budget instead of the 60s ownership one. **Shipped:** (1) `internal/feature/exit_rules/sync_routes_converge_b374.go` — `ConvergeAdvertisedRoutes(reason) (applied, skipped int)` computes each relay's owned set from `prefix_owner` plus the always-present base routes, compares it with what the relay **actually advertises** from the SAME headscale node view the rest of the code reads (`ListAllNodes`, **no new API call**) through the pure predicate `RoutesNeedConvergence`, and calls the SHARED apply path (`syncOneExitNode`, aliased as `syncOneExitNodeFn`) **only** for a relay whose normalised sets differ — order-insensitive, duplicates ignored, both sides carrying `0.0.0.0/0`+`::/0`, an empty advertisement treated as the base pair on the wire, so **a converged relay opens NO ssh at all**; an unreadable advertised plane applies nothing (absence of evidence is not evidence of a difference); a relay that could not be applied is recorded as `relay_advertise_stale:<relay>` = `"<unix>|<relay> still advertises N prefix(es) that prefix_owner no longer assigns to it: <detail>"` and logged `DECLARED STALE` instead of being retried every tick (B309 already proved the transport fails); (2) the pass runs on the **~5-minute maintenance tick** (`RunPreferredExitReconciler` in `internal/handlers/handlers.go`), **not** on the churn-gated auto-updater, with its own `routeConvergenceInterval` (5m) and its own `route-converge:` journal prefix; (3) `sync_acl.go` gains `aclDriftBudget`/`aclDriftReason` and `periodicDriftCheckAfterOwnershipMove()` — when the planes disagree the drift check spends the **60s operator** budget and names the true reason, while the derived-DNS path keeps `churnACLThrottle` (30m, **B298 untouched**); (4) `/admin/exit-nodes` renders the stale records next to the existing B309 transport banner (RU+EN i18n). Contracts in `scripts/check_b374_route_convergence.sh` + `internal/feature/exit_rules/sync_routes_converge_b374_test.go` (real SQLite: the predicate, the converged-no-apply case, the excluded/stale case, the budget).
- **B373** — (2026-10-09, operator request) **hints with buttons that apply the defaults — and ONE meaning for `%s`.** The request: «добавь подсказки с активными кнопками чтобы пользователь мог как прочитать что надо сделать и для чего так и применить параметры по умолчанию». Two defects had to close first. **(1) `cluster_database.dsn_template` had TWO placeholder conventions and the panel taught the WRONG one.** The panel composer (`internal/feature/admin/database.go`) and `buildDSNTemplate` (`internal/dbmigrate/steps/flip.go`) put `%s` in the **PASSWORD** position, with a comment claiming *"the watchdog (Phase 3.1) substitutes the password at read time"* — a substitution that exists **nowhere in the tree** — while the only real substitution, `internal/cluster.substituteDSNTemplate(tpl, primaryHost)` (B212, exercised by `scripts/b212_join_verify.sh`), puts the primary's **HOSTNAME** there. Following the panel's own instruction made the primary answer `skygate join` with `postgres://user:<primary-hostname>@host/db` — the hostname in the password field, and an authentication failure no surface explained. The CLI hint and both i18n texts repeated the same wrong sentence. **Now one convention everywhere** (composer + `dsnTemplateFromParts`, `buildDSNTemplate`, the join hint, RU+EN): `%s` is the **HOST**, the password is **never** stored in `cluster_database` (that row is rendered on `/admin/database`, written to `audit_log` and copied by every database backup), and the standby adds it to its own DSN. **(2) Tailscale had a Start button but no "make this the default" action**, and the reason the client dies at every container recreate is not in the panel at all: the environment is **frozen at container creation** and compose pins `SKYGATE_TS_AUTHKEY_FILE=/dev/null`, so the entrypoint skips tailscaled (B321; measured again 2026-10-09 — recreate at 11:18:51, `TS_AUTHKEY_FILE not set — Tailscale skipped`, client up at 11:19:59, which is the ~70 s window B370 made harmless). Shipped: `/admin/database` gets a DSN-template **hint card** + the button «Заполнить из моего DSN» (composes the template from the DSN *this* process runs on); `/admin/cluster`'s onboarding banner offers the same action (`next=/admin/cluster`) with the inline SQL corrected to the **upsert** (the row can be absent — measured 0 rows on this primary); `/admin/tailscale` gets a hint card + «Применить значения по умолчанию», which persists the usable auth-key path + `desired_state=on` + the canonical hostname and brings the client up through the B321 path — it **refuses by name** when no usable key file exists (never mints one) and names the compose value that removes the window. **Also fixed here:** `GetClusterDatabase` scanned `created_at`/`updated_at` straight into `time.Time`; on SQLite the writer binds `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` (TEXT), so the read died with `unsupported Scan, storing driver.Value type string into type *time.Time` and `/admin/database` could not read back the row the new button had just written on a native install (the same helper backs the dbmigrate watchdog's row loader) — it now decodes through `db.ParseDBTime` (B291). gofmt ratchet 252 → 249 (`certsync.go`, `cluster.go`, `flip.go` left the allowlist). 33 contracts in `scripts/check_b373_operator_hints_apply_defaults.sh`.
- **B372** — (2026-10-09, refactor) **the boot sequence stops being ONE 2500-line function: the tail moves out first, and the middle is pinned as unmovable-until-B373.** `cmd/skygate/main.go` is not "many long functions" — it is **one** function: 2568 lines, of which 128..2567 are the body of `main()`; the only other top-level declarations are a `var` block and three small helpers. Every refactor since v0.30 has **added** to it, so it grows by construction. B372 extracts the **tail** — the five opt-in background schedulers (B143 smoke-mesh cleanup, B223 Tailscale discovery, B154 token auto-rotate, B156 preauth-key notify, B145 HA chain/elector) — into `cmd/skygate/main_schedulers.go` as `wireOptionalSchedulers(ctx, d, app, adminSvc, cfg)`: **2568 → 2398 lines**, the call at the **same** point of the boot sequence (after the certsync wiring, before the shutdown wait), the graceful-shutdown path still in `main()`, every scheduler keeping its own `cfg.*` gate and its own enabled/disabled log line. **Why the tail and not the middle:** the middle **cannot** be moved as text — it holds bare `defer`s in `main()` (`defer wd.Stop()` dbmigrate watchdog, `defer el.Stop()` HA elector, `defer stop()` the headscale-version ask), and a `defer` inside an `if` block still defers to the enclosing **function**, so lifting those lines into a helper would stop the watchdog and the elector the moment the helper returned — a silent behaviour change no grep would catch. Those need a lifecycle-owned type with an explicit `Stop()` called from the shutdown path (B373+), and the contract records the boundary so the next editor does not "finish the refactor" by moving them. 26 contracts in `scripts/check_b372_main_is_not_one_giant_function.sh` (including a line-count ceiling of 2400 that may only fall, and `go build`/`go test ./cmd/skygate/` as the behavioural half).
- **B371** — (2026-10-09, live) **certsync must read the S3 the operator configured — the env-only builder handed it a host that cannot exist.** The operator configured S3 on `/admin/backup` (endpoint `https://minio.skynas.ru`, bucket `skygate-backups`), the panel's own backup worked, and the journal carried **every 30 seconds, forever**: `certsync: get .version: Get "http://s3..amazonaws.com/skygate-backups/?location=": no such host`. The cause is a **stale premise**, not a typo: B147 built the config with `buildBackupConfigForCertSync`, which read `SKYGATE_S3_ENDPOINT`/`_REGION` from the **environment only**, documented as "same source the backup subsystem uses, so operators only configure one place" — untrue since the backup subsystem moved to `global_settings` (`backup.Load`, written by `/admin/backup`). With those variables unset **both** the endpoint and the region were empty and `internal/backup/s3.go` derived `fmt.Sprintf("https://s3.%s.amazonaws.com", c.S3Region)` → the double-dotted host (minio-go normalised it to `http`). Nothing at boot could notice (the failure is inside the first tick) and no surface said which S3 certsync read from. Fixes: (1) the DB is the **first** source of the four transport settings, env vars only fill the gaps, while the certsync **bucket** stays its own setting (`cfg.CertSyncBucket` — a standby's cert bucket need not be the primary's backup destination bucket); (2) new `certSyncS3ConfigProblem` **refuses by name** at boot (no endpoint AND no region would be handed to minio-go; empty credentials/bucket are named too) and `main.go` logs `certsync: disabled — <reason>` instead of starting the doomed loop; (3) the enabled line reports **endpoint + region**, and an identical tick failure is reported at most **once an hour** (`fetchFailureIsNew`, the B318 `discoveryErrorIsNew` pattern). B147's contract C was **renegotiated in place** (it asserted the env-only helper by name). Behavioural: `cert_sync_s3_b371_test.go` (real SQLite — panel first, env fallback, three refusals by name) + `certsync_b371_test.go` (first occurrence always reported, a different failure never suppressed, the same one back after the window). `internal/certsync/certsync.go` left the gofmt allowlist (252 → 251; rule 3 requires `gofmt -w` on every file a block touches). 26 contracts in `scripts/check_b371_certsync_uses_the_configured_s3.sh`.
- **B370** — (2026-10-09, live right after an update) **the portal's own missing tailnet is not a fact about a relay, and an IPv6 jump rung must bracket its target once.** `/admin/update` recreated the container at 11:18:51; the entrypoint logged `[init] TS_AUTHKEY_FILE not set — Tailscale skipped`; the sync pass started 11:19:47 and **every** rung of karolina's ladder failed 11:19:52–11:20:07 — the direct tailnet dial timed out, the node name needed the container's DNS, and **both** peer hops (`100.64.0.3:22`, `100.64.0.4:22`) timed out too because they are tailnet addresses as well; one rung died differently: `Bad stdio forwarding specification '[[fd7a:115c:a1e0::2]]:18022'`. `tailscaled` came up 11:19:59 (`tailscale-autostart: boot: up`), and **11:20:38 — 90 s later, same pass — emilia applied over the tailnet**; a live TCP probe of `100.64.0.2:18022` then answered 5/5 in 135 ms and a manual Re-sync succeeded on the **first** rung. Nothing was wrong with karolina, yet `relay_apply_state:karolina` had already been written as an error, so B309/B352 dropped her from the healthy set, moved her **196** prefixes to another relay and painted «Ретранслятор не удалось настроить — его префиксы переданы другому»; only B361's safety net kept the two devices pinned to her tag online. Two defects: (1) `relayApplyOutcome` gains `NotEvidence`/`NotEvidenceReason`, set at the ladder's failure point when **`!SkygateTailnetState().Ready && AllCandidatesNeedThePortalTailnet(cands)`** (every candidate **and every jump hop** is a tailnet address — an **empty** candidate list stays evidence: that is a misconfiguration and it *is* about the relay), and `recordRelayApply` then writes neither the state nor the `via` key and logs the named reason, so the previous state stands and no prefix migrates (the B362 lesson, applied to the transport ladder); a **real** failure is still recorded and still demotes. (2) `headscale.ForwardSpec(host, port)` strips `user@` and existing brackets, re-brackets only a literal IPv6, defaults port 22, and the jump rung passes it to `-W` — ssh's `%h` expands to the **already-bracketed** form, so `-W '[%h]:%p'` rendered `[[addr]]:port` and ssh refused the rung (B353's C3 contract asserted exactly that broken string and was renegotiated). 14 contracts in `scripts/check_b370_off_tailnet_is_not_a_relay_failure.sh` + `relay_apply_not_evidence_b370_test.go` + `routes_b370_test.go`.
- **B369** — (2026-10-09, live desktop) **`/admin/cluster`'s tables must scroll, not be clipped.** Operator screenshot (1088 px desktop): the row action buttons «Снять / Слить и удалить / Обновить» hang **past the card's right border**. The cause is systemic: `body{overflow-x:hidden}` (`themes.css`) means there is **no page-level horizontal scrollbar**, so a table wider than its card is silently **clipped**; `.table-wrap{overflow-x:auto}` is the project's only scroller and it is **opt-in** (27 template files use it) — `cluster.html` was the one wide-table page with **none**; and the B357 systemic fix (`.card{overflow-x:auto}`, `th{white-space:normal}`) sits inside `@media (max-width:768px)`, i.e. **mobile only**. The nodes table has **nine** columns, one of them the long B359 reason sentence (which defined the table's preferred width) and one the action buttons. Fix: every table on the page is wrapped in `.table-wrap` and the reason cell is bounded by a block-level `max-width` — deliberately **not** `overflow-wrap:anywhere`, which B357 removed because it collapses a cell's min-content to one character. 10 contracts in `scripts/check_b369_cluster_table_not_clipped.sh`, incl. the behavioural half (`TestLoadTemplates` panics on any parse error).
- **B368** — (2026-10-09, live) **a Test button that cannot go red is worse than no button.** The reference deployment's in-app S3 backup had been failing for weeks (`backup.last_error: s3 bucket check: Head "http://172.18.0.5:9000/skygate-backups/": connect: connection refused`) while `/admin/backup`'s «Test» answered `S3 доступен: http://172.18.0.5:9000 · корзина: skygate-backups` — the S3 branch of `backup.TestConnection` checked only that the fields were **non-empty** and echoed the endpoint back, with a comment claiming the probe happens later in `uploadToS3` (true and useless). That green tick is why a MinIO that had **moved to another host** stayed unnoticed; a real AWS-SigV4 probe from the same host then proved the stored credentials are rejected by the MinIO that IS running, and the correct endpoint answers 200 on both `https://minio.skynas.ru` and the LAN address. The branch now performs the same `BucketExists` round trip the upload path makes, bounded by `s3TestTimeout`, and names WHY it failed (unreachable / credentials rejected / no such bucket) while a half-filled form is still reported as missing fields. 10 contracts in `scripts/check_b368_backup_test_tests.sh` + `internal/backup/mount_b368_test.go`.
- **B367** — (2026-10-09, live PostgreSQL) **an ARRAY column is not a string: `COALESCE(roles, '')` made the operator's button and the failover transaction dead on PostgreSQL.** The operator pressed «Слить и удалить» on one of the relay rows the pre-B359 discovery pass had adopted, and got `drain+remove: lookup node: ERROR: malformed array literal: "" (SQLSTATE 22P02)`. `cluster_node.roles` is `TEXT[]`, so the untyped `''` is coerced to `text[]` and PostgreSQL **rejects the statement at parse time** — reproduced directly against the live DB, no data can make it work. **Five** statements carried it: the lookup of `RemoveNode` / `DrainNode` / `DrainAndRemoveNode` (`internal/cluster/node.go`) and — the severe pair — `FindClusterPrimary` + `NodeRoles` (`internal/db/cluster_sql_b291.go`), which the **failover** transaction and the failover **drill** call, so a real failover could not even find the current primary. On SQLite the same column is the `{a,b}` TEXT literal, so `'{}'` parses identically and a native install cannot see it (LESSONS L-16, in the path used during an incident). Fix: `db.EmptyTextArrayLiteral` (`'{}'`), defined once with the SQLSTATE and the affected paths, used by all five sites. Contracts are behavioural — the real functions on SQLite (always) and on PostgreSQL (CI's PG job, the job whose absence let this ship) — plus a source contract that derives every `TEXT[]` column from the PostgreSQL chain and fails on `COALESCE(<array column>, '')`, proved to fire on a planted violation. 13 contracts in `scripts/check_b367_array_coalesce.sh`.
- **B366** — (2026-10-09, live second host, whole flow end to end) **the onboarding must not cut the operator off, and a join must register the BUILD it joined with.** (1) Step 1 passed `--accept-routes`, which **locked the operator out of the host**: the flag installs every prefix the relays advertise into routing table 52, policy rule 5270 consults that table *before* `main`, and with 220 accepted entries (206 from one relay — exit rules resolve domains to single IPs) **the operator's own address was among them** (`95.165.170.190/32`), so the host answered SSH over `tailscale0` instead of its default gateway. Evidence: `ip route get 95.165.170.190` → `dev tailscale0 table 52` with the flag on vs `via 10.0.0.1 dev net0` with it off, and `tcpdump -ni net0 'tcp port 22'` showing the operator's SYNs **arriving with no SYN-ACK ever leaving** while another client completed a full handshake in the same capture (sshd healthy, only that path broken). Reproduced twice in each direction; `tailscale set --accept-routes=false` restored the session in seconds. The block no longer passes it in **either** branch, keeps `hostnamectl` / the idempotent client install / the login server / `nodivert` / `--accept-dns=false`, and records the measurement where the flag would be re-added — a standby is a **server** and needs the tailnet, not the relays' subnets (the pre-B365 runbook said this; the generated block had lost it). (2) The join registered `skygate_version=unknown` (live row + `cluster_audit` `node_join`), because `skygate join`/`skygate init` read `SKYGATE_VERSION` — which nothing in the CLI sets — with a literal `unknown` fallback, while the build identity is injected into `package main` by ldflags: B365's promise was one placeholder swapped for another. New `selfVersion()` owns the rule (env override → build version + commit in the `/healthz` shape → `dev`; never `unknown` for a real build), both callers use it, `main.go` uses `buildVersionString()` so the displayed and registered versions cannot drift. 22 contracts in `scripts/check_b366_onboard_keeps_the_operator_connected.sh` + `cmd/skygate/self_version_b366_test.go` + `internal/feature/admin/cluster_onboard_b366_test.go`.
- **B365** — (2026-10-08, live second host) **the panel-only cluster onboarding loses its last three dead steps and silent gaps.** (1) Step 4 rendered `systemctl enable --now skygate` + a `/healthz` curl, but a standby receives a single BINARY — no unit exists — so the operator was told to run something that does not, and started the daemon by hand with `setsid`; the CLI's own hint even pointed at `deploy/heartbeat-daemon.service`, a file this repo does not contain. The block now RENDERS `/etc/systemd/system/skygate-heartbeat.service` inline (`ExecStart=/usr/local/bin/skygate cluster heartbeat-daemon --state-file=/etc/skygate/cluster-state.json`), `daemon-reload`s, `enable --now`s idempotently and verifies THE DAEMON (`systemctl is-active --quiet` + `journalctl -u … -n 5`) — no curl, because a standby has no web service and a check that can only fail is not a check. (2) `cluster_database.dsn_template` was empty, so the standby got no DSN and could never mirror without a hand edit. The absence is now loud on BOTH surfaces — `missingDSNBootstrapHint()` in the CLI and `ClusterOnboardView.DSNReady` on the page — naming the setting, `/admin/database`, the equivalent `UPDATE cluster_database … WHERE id='skygate-staging'` and the one fact a wrong guess gets wrong (`%s` is the PASSWORD placeholder). NO CREDENTIAL IS EVER INVENTED: an existing template keeps today's behaviour. (3) The row kept the discovery placeholder because `cluster_node` has TWO unique keys — the `id` PRIMARY KEY and B211's `UNIQUE (cluster_id, hostname)` — while the join looked the row up BY HOSTNAME and returned it UNTOUCHED (`skygate_version=(discovered via Tailscale)` on a host that had really joined, and no `node_join` audit row). The join now ADOPTS it (`adoptNodeOnJoin`, id preserved because an invite and a live heartbeat daemon name it) and the INSERT's target is corrected to `(cluster_id, hostname) DO UPDATE … RETURNING id`, with `markInviteUsed`/`auditNodeJoin`/`joinResponse` shared so the branches cannot drift. Behaviour pinned on a migrated SQLite DB by `internal/cluster/join_b365_test.go` + two more test files; 28 contracts in `scripts/check_b365_cluster_onboard_gaps.sh`. **Contract renegotiated:** the step-4 i18n keys were RENAMED to `cluster.onboard_step_heartbeat` / `_note_heartbeat` in both catalogues (a standby has no service). **Gap 2 completed by live measurement:** the reference primary's `cluster_database` table held **0 rows** — nothing creates one until `/admin/database` is opened — so the pre-fix recipe (`UPDATE … WHERE id='skygate-staging'`) changed 0 rows and reported success; both the CLI hint and the page warning now carry an **upsert** (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE`), and the emitted step was RUN on the live standby (systemd accepted the rendered unit, `enable --now` took, and `last_seen_at` advanced on the primary from the unit-managed daemon).
- **B364** — (2026-10-08, live standby) **a real join must revive the cluster row the elector had settled in `failed`.** The B223 discovery pass created the row `pending`, the B204 elector settled it in `failed` because skygate was not running on that host yet, and the join's `ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE` refreshed only `tailscale_ip`, `skygate_version` and `last_seen_at` — never `state`. A `failed` row is terminal for the elector (it only walks `pending ↔ ready`), so the operator could never Approve the node the join had just registered. The conflict branch now re-asserts `state = 'pending'`; B365 superseded that branch with **adoption** (`adoptNodeOnJoin`, which sets `pending` on the same path and also writes the `node_join` audit row). Behaviour pinned by `internal/cluster/join_b365_test.go`; live sequence re-measured on 2026-10-09 (`node_health to=failed` at 04:39 → `node_join` at 04:44:55). **This index line was missing until B366's index-shape contract caught it.**
- **B363** — (2026-10-08, live second host) **the cluster join had never worked on PostgreSQL, plus four more reasons the panel's onboarding block could not succeed.** (1) `internal/cluster/join.go` built its INSERT by string concatenation and three Go comment lines landed **inside** the SQL literal, so PostgreSQL answered `syntax error at or near ":" (SQLSTATE 42601)` — the join never worked on the production dialect; (2) `--netfilter-mode=nodir` is not a value the client accepts (`invalid value "--netfilter-mode=\"nodir\""`; the valid set is `on|nodivert|off`) and it rejects the WHOLE `tailscale up`, so the host never joined the tailnet — 21 occurrences fixed across installers, docs and contracts; (3) the OS hostname must match the invite's target or the server answers `403 hostname does not match token target`, so `hostnamectl set-hostname` was prepended to step 1; (4) the join line put the token BEFORE the flags and the CLI refuses exactly that order; (5) the CLI's own next-steps hint pointed at `deploy/heartbeat-daemon.service`, a file this repo does not contain. Flow pinned by `scripts/check_b212.sh`, `scripts/check_b342_cluster_onboard.sh` and the B365 contract set. **This index line was missing until B366's index-shape contract caught it.**
- **B362** — (2026-10-07, live v1.5.103 deployment) **a deadline that expired on our own clock is not evidence about a relay.** Minutes after the operator applied v1.5.103 the log showed the B356.1 ladder running and its relay fallback **never carrying a single request**: three `setMyCommands` calls failed with `context deadline exceeded … no relay could carry it (tried emilia, karolina, sharlotta)`, then two minutes of `getUpdates error: … dial tcp 149.154.166.110:443: i/o timeout and the relay fallback is unavailable: every relay is cooling down after a failure`. **Cause:** `setMyCommands` carries its own **5-second** context while the bounded direct dial alone is allowed **6**, so the first call spent its whole budget and the relay attempts came back deadline-exceeded — and `noteRelayFailure` cooled **every** relay down for `RelayCooldown` (2 min), locking the 30-second `getUpdates` loop out of the fallback entirely. Measured in the same container at the same moment: the identical argv (`ssh -i /ssh-sync/skygate_sync … -W api.telegram.org:443 -- <relay>`) completed in **2.1 s** against karolina and **2.0 s** against sharlotta — the relays were healthy, only our clock was not. The new pure `relayFailureIsEvidence(candidate, callerContext, err)` answers false when the caller context is done or the error is `context.Canceled`; the relay loop then records through `noteNoRelayFailure` (**no cooldown, reason kept**) and stops trying the remaining relays on a dead context, while a tunnel error raised while the caller **still has budget** remains evidence and cools that relay down exactly as before — so a genuinely broken relay is still taken out of the pool. Pinned in both directions by `TestB362_ExpiredCallerBudgetDoesNotCoolTheRelayDown` (an expired caller budget leaves both candidates available; a refused tunnel leaves none) plus the B356.1 ladder tests. 10 contracts in `scripts/check_b362_expired_budget_is_not_a_relay_failure.sh`
- **B361** — (2026-10-07, live PostgreSQL deployment) **a stored exit-node pin must not outlive the routing it names, and a device with no rules must not be invisible.** Live: device `a71` (skyadmin's Android) had **ZERO** rows in `device_rules` while its only internet permission was ONE `device_exit_node_prefs` row (`tag:dev-skyadmin-a71` → `tag:dev-infra-emilia`, **`set_by_user_id = 1`** — a HUMAN choice recorded ~46 days earlier); `prefix_owner` held **120 rows, every one of them `karolina`**, which advertised 122 routes while `emilia` advertised 2 (`0.0.0.0/0`, `::/0`) — so the pinned relay served **none** of the per-destination routing. Since B265 that preference is emitted as `via=[the preferred tag]` on the per-device `autogroup:internet` grant, which headscale applies as a permission **FILTER**, so `a71` could exit ONLY through a relay carrying none of its destinations; and the existing safety net could not fire, because B356 drops a `via` pin only when the relay is UNUSABLE (B273 `offline`/`degraded`) and `emilia` was `online`, while the reconciler skipped the device with `stale-pref-no-owner` — a device with no rules has no owner to derive, a **named skip in a log nobody reads**. The new pure `internal/acl/acl_relay_ownership_b361.go` `servesNothing` asks whether the preferred relay serves anything **the device can use**: with rules, at least one prefix they claim must be owned by that relay in `prefix_owner`; with **no rules** the relay must own at least one prefix **at all**, because such a device's egress depends entirely on what the relay advertises (an EMPTY `prefix_owner` keeps every pin — absence of evidence must not rewrite a policy, the rule B356 already applies to an unmeasured relay). The per-device emit is gated on health **AND** ownership; a withheld pin falls through to the **UNPINNED** grant so the device keeps egress through any working relay, logged per device and counted per generation. **The operator's stored row is never deleted or rewritten — only the PIN is withheld**, so the human's choice revives by itself the moment that relay serves something again. The reconciler gains the named state `stale-pref-no-rules` (audited + notified, throttled per device+reason) carrying the relay and its prefix count. BOTH `/admin/exit-nodes` and `/my/exit-nodes` render the state and a one-click fix through the **existing** `/admin/devices/preferred-exit` and `/my/devices/preferred-exit` endpoints, with 13 new keys in BOTH catalogues and the sentence built handler-side (B325). **Propagation:** the four dependent artefacts had converged at four unrelated moments over ~65 minutes (ownership 15:03, preferences 15:43, the relay's routes since 12:44, the ACL at 16:07) — a preference change and the reconciler's own write now call `exit_rules.ReapplyACLAfterPreferenceChange`, wired in `main.go` to `exitRulesSvc.ReapplyACLIfDrifted`, the **same** shared 60 s apply slot (`takeACLApplySlot`), so a burst of five changes is ONE apply. 47 contracts in `scripts/check_b361_pin_without_routing.sh` (incl. one that FAILS if a pin can ever be emitted for a relay that owns nothing, and one that FAILS if the human's row is deleted or rewritten by this path) + timelines in `internal/acl/acl_b361_test.go` and `internal/feature/exit_rules/pref_reapply_b361_test.go`. **Contract renegotiated:** `check_b298_cdn_rule_churn.sh` B7 accepts the shared-slot accessor beside the old inline expression (property unchanged: the deferral log names the budget it actually spent), and `reconciler_b356_collector_test.go`'s subject-set test expects `stale-pref-no-rules` (property unchanged: a preference row with no rules is SEEN and NAMED)
- **B359** — (2026-10-07, live PostgreSQL deployment) **the cluster is creatable from the panel alone, and the cluster list holds only skygate hosts.** Live: `cluster_node` carried three exit-node RELAYS (`emilia`, `karolina`, `sharlotta`) as permanently-`failed` `skygate-standby` rows, because the B223 discovery predicate adopted every online peer that was not already in `cluster_node` and had no positive notion of a skygate host. `internal/cluster/discovery.go` now has ONE pure adoption rule, `ClassifyPeerForAdoption`, and its ORDER is the contract: **refuse a relay FIRST** (it carries `tag:exit-node`, or it has a row in `exit_servers` — the relay check must come first because B111 gives every relay its own `tag:dev-infra-<its hostname>`, so a bare prefix test would accept `emilia`), otherwise adopt only positive evidence in the shape the panel's **own** onboarding mints — `tag:dev-infra-<lowercase hostname>` — and RETURN every examined peer that is not adopted with a **named** `DiscoverySkipReason` (`relay-exit-tag`, `relay-exit-server`, `not-skygate-host`, `already-cluster-member`, `ipv6-only`, `no-address`, `no-hostname`), because "we cannot decide" must not look like "there is nothing there" (L-54). The ticker reports the candidate-shaped refusals behind the same once-an-hour throttle as B318/B354 (`cluster.discovery.skip`), the Discover button names them in its flash and reports an unlinked `infra` portal user as a precondition to fix **before** a VM is provisioned (the preauth key would otherwise be refused by headscale and ownership could never be recorded), and `/admin/cluster` renders a per-row reason plus the next action in RU+EN (a relay row is called out as NOT a skygate host, with the `node_health` sentence the B204 elector wrote). 34 contracts in `scripts/check_b359_cluster_panel_admission.sh` + 16 tests in `internal/cluster/discovery_b359_test.go` and `internal/feature/admin/cluster_reason_b359_test.go`; the panel-only procedure (and why a `failed` row is terminal — the elector only transitions `pending`/`ready`) is `docs/operations.md` §17. **Contract renegotiated:** `check_b318_tailscale_state_truth.sh` D3 matches `:= cluster.DiscoverNewNodes(` (the function now returns a `Report`), and a new D4 pins the third stage's noise floor
- **B356.1** — (2026-10-07, live deployment) **the Telegram Bot API falls back to an SSH relay tunnel when the portal's own egress is blocked (the B353 class).** Measured: `api.telegram.org:443` is BLOCKED from the VM host AND from inside the container (TCP connect times out while DNS resolves fine to `149.154.166.110`) while `karolina`/`emilia`/`sharlotta` reach it and the container holds an SSH key they accept — `telegram: getUpdates error: … context deadline exceeded` every ~47 s forever, **every notification lost**, and nothing on any page said so. The existing `ok_relay` vocabulary never applied because it expects the kernel to route via `tailscale0` through a relay SUBNET route, while the container runs `RouteAll=true` with no exit node and the kernel sends the Telegram IPs out of `eth0` through the docker bridge. **Direct first, always** (a recent direct failure is re-probed once a minute and cleared the moment it succeeds); the last rung is an `http.Transport` whose `DialContext` runs `ssh -W api.telegram.org:443 -- <relay>` and uses the child's stdio as the socket, so TLS terminates **in this process** and the bot token can never reach a relay's argv, process list or logs (never `curl`-on-the-relay). The argv comes from ONE builder, `headscale.SSHTunnelArgv`, which reuses `IsSafeSSHTarget` (B266) and `jumpProxyCommandAllowed` (B353) instead of copying a security predicate, with `ProxyCommand=none`, `IdentitiesOnly=yes`, `--` before the host and **`-W` placed BEFORE `--`** (after `--` ssh would run `-W` as a remote command). Relays come from `exit_servers`, tailnet-address-first (B310), proven-first from the B309 `relay_apply_state` record (B353), at most 3 candidates with one attempt each and a cooldown for the one that just failed. `/admin/telegram` gains the honest state `ok_relay_tunnel`; `SKYGATE_TELEGRAM_RELAY_FALLBACK=0` is the off switch and `SKYGATE_TELEGRAM_PREFERRED_RELAY` names the first relay; every decision is logged once per five-minute window, not per attempt. **The recorded token leak is fixed**: `RedactToken`/`redactError` strip the token and the `bot-id:secret` pattern from `getUpdates`, POST, `sendMessage`, `editMessageText`, `answerCallbackQuery`, `getMe`, `sendRichMessage`, `setMyCommands` and the admin probe. No schema change, no migration. 24 unit tests + `scripts/check_b356_1_telegram_relay_fallback.sh`; runbook `docs/operations.md` §8.5
- **B356** — (2026-10-07, live PostgreSQL deployment) **a device's exit-node preference must follow the relay that is ACTUALLY serving, and must not survive its own relay's death.** Live (item 1 of the operator's seven): `device_exit_node_prefs` named `tag:dev-infra-karolina` for `basic` (user 6) and `skyworker` (user 1), both `set_by_user_id = 0` (derived by the engine), while `prefix_owner` held **139 rows and every one of them was `emilia`** — and since B265 that preference is emitted as `via=[the preferred tag]` on the per-device `autogroup:internet` grant, which headscale applies as a permission **FILTER**, so their egress was filtered into a black hole; and nothing re-evaluated an **existing** row, because the reconciliation subject set was built from `device_rules` while the row lives in `device_exit_node_prefs` (L-54: look for the filter that defines the SUBJECT SET, not the logic inside it). `ReconcileDeviceExitNodePrefs` now walks the **union** of rule pairs and preference rows and re-points a stale DERIVED row at the relay `prefix_owner` actually chose, with **named** reasons for every move and every undecidable state (`stale-pref-relay-unusable`, `stale-pref-no-owner`, `stale-pref-owner-split`, `stale-pref-owner-untagged`, `stale-pref-owner-is-pref-relay`) — never a silent `return nil, false`. A row a **person** set (`set_by_user_id != 0`) is never rewritten: it is surfaced as `stale-pref-human-pinned` / `stale-pref-human-non-owner` with an audit row and a rate-limited Telegram alert. The ACL safety net: `monitoring.ExitNodeUsable` (the B273 predicate, **exported** rather than re-derived) now gates the per-device pin, so an unusable preferred relay emits the **unpinned** `autogroup:internet` grant instead of filtering the device out, logged per device and counted once per generation — a **deliberate B265 renegotiation** that leaves `check_b188_2.sh` contract B (exactly one conditional emit, guarded by the per-device preference lookup) intact and renegotiates its live contract W in place (the expected pin count is now the preferences whose relay is usable or unmeasured). Convergence rides the existing hourly maintenance tick (L-60) with one audit row and one notification per (device, reason) per hour. `/admin/exit-nodes` shows the stale or ignored preference, the relay's measured state, the data plane's choice and the consequence, backed by 17 new keys in BOTH catalogues. 48 contracts in `scripts/check_b356_exit_pref_failover.sh`
- **B358** — (2026-10-06, live PostgreSQL deployment) **the admin exit-rules page paginated by RULE ROW, so the operator's three devices and two users became eight pages with the users and the groups smeared across them.** Operator report (verbatim): «веб-форма до сих пор странно отображает правила exit rules для администратора теперь восемь страниц при том что в группе всего три устройства и два пользователя … пользователи и группы размазаны по этим восьми страницам». Measured live: **393 enabled `device_rules` rows, 2 users, 3 devices** — `skyadmin`/9 `skyworker` 219, `skyadmin`/56 `cyborg` 12, `michail`/29 `basic` 162 — while `AdminExitRules` called `db.GetAllRulesForAdminPaged`, whose SQL was `ORDER BY r.user_id, r.device_id, r.id LIMIT $1 OFFSET $2` (defaults page 1 / page_size 50): the unit of a page was a **row**, so no single page could answer «one user's / one device's rules together». B358 makes the window unit the **GROUP** — a distinct `(user_id, device_id)` pair. `qSelectAdminRuleGroups` windows the groups themselves (`GROUP BY r.user_id, r.device_id`, `enabled = 1`, `LEFT JOIN portal_users` so an orphaned row still forms a group named `?`), `qCountAdminRuleGroups` returns the group and distinct-user totals unpaginated, and `qSelectAllRulesForAdminPaged` — which no longer carries a row limit — fetches the rows of **exactly that page's groups in ONE query** through an OR-chain of `(r.user_id = $n AND r.device_id = $n+1)` pairs built with `db.PlaceholderAt` (the OR-chain, not a row-value `IN`, is the form both dialects bind), so **a group is never split across pages** however many rows it holds. The default window is `db.AdminGroupsPerPage = 20` groups — the measured live shape renders as **ONE page** — with the requested size still clamped to 1..500. `realTotal` now comes from the group/count companion (`AdminRulePage.TotalRules`) and **not** from the row window, `ShownRules` stays `len(rr)`, and the rule `COUNT` gained the missing `enabled = 1` (it counted disabled rows too, so it disagreed with every other counter the moment a rule was disabled). A group larger than `db.AdminOversizedGroupRows = 150` rows renders as a **collapsed `<details>`** whose rows are already fetched (presentation only, no second query, no new JS state) with a hint, so one device with hundreds of rules cannot turn the page into a wall of rows. The `?device=` drill-down is untouched — it keeps its own unpaged path on `qSelectAllRulesForAdminByDevice`, because one device is already one whole group (B348/B349 preserved). RU + EN keys `exit_rules_admin.pagination_page_of_groups`, `exit_rules_admin.pagination_total_groups`, `exit_rules_admin.group_oversized_hint`. **Contract renegotiation:** `scripts/check_b351_rule_owner_and_counts.sh` contract C3 pinned the row-based `ORDER BY` as the desired state and now asserts the GROUP window in place, with the reason recorded where the old assertion was — the property is unchanged («the window follows the grouping the page renders»); grouping the `ORDER BY` alone still let a 219-row device straddle a page boundary. Contracts in `scripts/check_b358_group_pagination.sh` + `internal/db/device_rules_b358_test.go` (the live shape = one page; whole groups; the PG dialect) + `internal/feature/exit_rules/form_admin_b358_test.go`; touching `internal/db/device_rules.go` also paid one entry off the B337 gofmt ratchet (262 → 261)
- **B357** — (2026-10-06, live v1.5.101 panel) **on a phone every table printed ONE GLYPH PER LINE, and twelve pages shared one cause.** Operator report: «при мобильном отображении таблиц слова переносятся на следующую строку побуквенно». Measured at 390×844 with a headless browser against the live pages: `internal/staticfs/static/css/themes.css` (mobile block, `@media (max-width:768px)`) set `overflow-wrap:anywhere` on `td code, td .mono, td pre, code, .mono, pre` — and `anywhere`, unlike `break-word`, **reduces the intrinsic min-content width to one character**, so with `table{width:100%}` and auto layout the column collapsed to **29 px** and `/admin/devices` printed `100.64.0.3` as ten lines at 1.00 chars/line. The scroller (`.card{overflow-x:auto}`, `.table-wrap`) already existed and could not help: scrolling cannot widen a 29 px column. Four more pages broke tokens through an inline `word-break:break-all`. Fixes: `break-word` instead of `anywhere`; addresses/timestamps pinned with `white-space:nowrap` inside data cells; the 18 audited `break-all` sites rewritten; and because `body{overflow-x:hidden}` (themes.css:209) **clipped** the overflow of the 62 tables that have no `.table-wrap`, the mobile shell now scrolls (`.shell{overflow-x:auto}`) so a bare wide table keeps its right-hand columns. The remaining audited gaps stay open in the ROADMAP: the dashboard's `.metric-grid` (403 px in a 362 px column, page 27 px wider than the screen) and the stacked-card layout that only `/admin/users` and `/my/devices` actually need. Contracts in `scripts/check_b357_mobile_table_legibility.sh`
- **B355** — (2026-10-06, live PostgreSQL deployment) **the attribution pass recorded the strategy's scope tag in the ownership row, so an OIDC device never joined the mesh.** Operator report: «устройство добавилось во вкладке Мои Устройства пометилось тегом ожидания … но ничего так и не произошло и пришлось вручную делать трансфер устройства на пользователя». Live trace (node 150, `s24-fe--ned`, OIDC): `19:39:04 DBG backfill node=150 name=s24-fe--ned matchedTag=tag:private api_tags=[] hasPrivate=false` → `19:39:05 DBG backfill AddTag called for node=150 (ensure tag:private)` → `19:42:56 [devices] s24-fe--ned has no device-to-device ACL entry: no per-device tag is recorded for it, so the mesh has nothing to grant`. Attribution worked and headscale received `tag:dev-skyadmin-s24-fe--ned`, but the `node_owner_map` row kept the STRATEGY's scope tag (`tag:private`) — and the row is what every access decision reads: the mesh grant source (`internal/db/device_owner_b316.go` refuses anything that is not `tag:dev-<user>-<host>`), the per-device ACL and the rule fan-out. The device therefore never joined the mesh until the operator transferred it by hand, and the transfer is what finally wrote a per-device tag into the row. The row now records the tag the device actually carries, the scope tag stays on the node in headscale (AddTag is a union) and the ACL keeps its tagOwners entry; the write is **scoped to the attributed portal user** (`db.UpdateNodeOwnerTagForUser`, `WHERE node_id AND username`) so a background pass can never rewrite another user's ownership (admin pin / device attributed elsewhere), while the owner-agnostic `UpdateNodeOwnerTag` stays for the admin tag action. Also: `UpdateNodeOwnerTag` hardcoded SQLite's `strftime('%s','now')` and now uses the dialect-aware `NowUnixSQL()`. Tests: `internal/nodeownership/nodeownership_b355_test.go` (the live shape + the rename case, asserting the ROW). Contracts in `scripts/check_b355_device_tag_recorded.sh`
- **B354** — (2026-10-06, live PostgreSQL deployment) **the cluster tree must bootstrap itself, and a broken insert must not fill the journal.** Live, every five minutes: `🔎 discovery-ticker: ensure "karolina" failed: insert discovered node: ERROR: insert or update on table "cluster_node" violates foreign key constraint "cluster_node_cluster_id_fkey" (SQLSTATE 23503)` — **three times per tick** (karolina, sharlotta, emilia). Measured on the live database: `cluster` had **0 rows**, `cluster_node` had **0 rows**, and the FK really is `cluster_node_cluster_id_fkey -> cluster`. The ticker inserts with `cluster_id='skygate-staging'` (`cmd/skygate/main_helpers.go`, `runOneDiscoveryTick`), and that row was created **only** by the admin handlers' first-use paths (`AddNode` / `IssueInvite`) — so an operator who never opened `/admin/cluster` got a permanently empty cluster tree, an empty `/admin/cluster`, and **864 log lines + 864 audit rows a day** in the two places used to find real events. **(1)** Discovery *is* a first use of the cluster tree: `EnsureDiscoveredNode` now bootstraps the row B200 would have created — `LookupCluster` first, then `EnsureCluster` with the same id, whose `ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING` means an operator's own cluster row (its name, its approved nodes) is never touched. **(2)** A genuinely broken insert must not fill the journal: the ensure stage is throttled exactly like the discover stage B318 fixed — keyed on the **joined message for the whole tick** (three peers with one error are ONE event), reported immediately the first time and again after an hour, cleared by any successful tick, and a suppressed repeat says *still failing* instead of going silent. Tests: `internal/cluster/discovery_b354_test.go` starts from the live state (an **empty** `cluster` table — FK enforcement is ON for SQLite in this project, so it reproduced the live error **before** the fix) plus "an existing row is not clobbered" and "all three live relays are discovered in one tick"; `cmd/skygate/discovery_throttle_b354_test.go` pins the noise floor and that the two stages do not share a suppress slot. Contracts in `scripts/check_b354_cluster_bootstrap.sh`
- **B353** — (2026-10-06, live PostgreSQL deployment) **the tailnet path is not automatically a path: a peer relay can carry the connection the portal cannot make.** Operator request: *«проверь еще раз доступ к emilia по tailscale»*. Measured: the container pings **karolina** (129 ms) and **sharlotta** (130 ms) but **emilia** times out; emilia's own view of the portal is `skygate-host online=true relay=waw cur='' lastHS=never` — **no handshake had ever completed**; the container sees emilia `relay="hel"` and all three Helsinki DERP servers (`derp28b/c/d.tailscale.com`, `157.180.28.32` / `95.217.2.165` / `65.109.143.62`) **time out from both the VM host and the container**, while emilia reaches them in 13.4 ms — the site's network blocks those Hetzner addresses. A peer relay reaches the target fine (`karolina`: `nc -z 100.64.0.3 22` → OPEN, `ssh` → `hon-brown.ptr.network`). **A/B with everything else held constant:** emilia homed at `waw` → `pong from emilia via DERP(waw) in 85ms`, TCP22 OPEN, ssh OK; homed at `hel` → ping timeout, TCP22 blocked, ssh timeout (`tailscale debug force-prefer-derp 22` + `break-derp-conns` flips it in 15 s; it is **not** persistent across a tailscaled restart). So B310's *prefer the tailnet address* is necessary but not sufficient: both hosts can be on the tailnet with no path between them, and the portal then silently stops being able to configure a **healthy** relay (`relay_apply_state:emilia` held an `err` record, prefix assignment excluded it). B353 adds a **last rung** to the B310 ladder — candidates that reach a relay THROUGH a peer relay, appended after every direct candidate so a healthy relay never pays for a hop. The hop is an **explicit `ssh -W` ProxyCommand** (not `-J`: measured live, OpenSSH's implicit jump connection inherits neither the identity nor the host-key policy and answers `Permission denied (publickey,password)`, while the explicit form returns the target's hostname) and is another `exit_servers` row, **provably reachable right now** (the HOP is what gets TCP-probed — probing the target is the measurement that already failed), never the target nor one of its addresses, ordered proven-first (a recorded successful apply) then by hostname, bounded at **3** per target with the truncation logged, and refused unless it passes `IsSafeSSHTarget` (B266 class). It needs no key of its own: `ssh -J` tunnels TCP, so the same identity authenticates both legs. The ssh argv now comes from **one pure function**: the direct form keeps every B266 property (`ProxyCommand=none`, `IdentitiesOnly`, `--` before the host) and the jump form carries `-J` instead — OpenSSH refuses both at once (*Cannot specify -J with ProxyCommand*, measured on OpenSSH 10.3p1), recorded where the choice is made. The transport used is logged and stored (`relay_apply_via:<relay>` = `tailnet-jump|<endpoint>`), so the page shows both ends of the path. **Contract renegotiated in place:** B310 B1 now asserts the ladder probes **through `ProbeEndpoint()`** with the bounded timeout — the property (*every candidate is probed before ssh runs*) is unchanged, the probed endpoint is now the hop for a jump candidate. Contracts in `scripts/check_b353_transport_jump_fallback.sh` (18) + `internal/feature/exit_rules/relay_transport_jump_b353_test.go` + `internal/headscale/routes_b353_test.go`. **B353.1 (found by the LIVE verification of B353, one log line above the rung that worked): the ladder was handing its own candidate to a gate that refuses it** — `exit-node sync(emilia): tailnet fd7a:115c:a1e0::3 answered but the routes could not be applied: SetAdvertisedRoutes(emilia): refusing unsafe ssh_target "root@fd7a:115c:a1e0::3" (expected [user@]host[:port])`. `RelayEndpoint.Target()` rendered an IPv6 literal **unbracketed**, so `ssh` would have read it as user `root@fd7a` plus a malformed host and `IsSafeSSHTarget` — correctly — refused it: an IPv6-only relay had one path fewer and the failure named a value the transport had just produced. `bracketHost` now brackets an IPv6 literal (driven by `net.ParseIP`, so IPv4 and names are untouched and an already-bracketed host is not double-wrapped), and `Label()` brackets it too so `[fd7a:115c:a1e0::3]:22` cannot be misread as host+port. Tests: `internal/feature/exit_rules/relay_transport_ipv6_b353_1_test.go` (the live candidate with and without a port, already-bracketed, IPv4/name unchanged, and every target the ladder builds carries a bracketed literal); contracts F1-F3 in `scripts/check_b353_transport_jump_fallback.sh`. **Verified live on the deployed v1.5.101**, with the direct path blocked inside the container and the hop left reachable: `tailnet 100.64.0.3 is not answering` → `routes applied over the tailnet-jump transport (tailnet-jump 100.64.0.3 via root@100.64.0.2:18022; through karolina …)` → `relay_apply_via:emilia = tailnet-jump|tailnet-jump 100.64.0.3 via root@100.64.0.2:18022`, `relay_apply_state:emilia = …|ok`
- **B352** — (2026-10-05, live PostgreSQL deployment) **the ownership decision did not converge, and a relay skygate cannot configure was a legal owner.** Operator report after the B351 restore: *«при попытке применить exit node напрямую не идет доступ у basic может и у остальных устройств windows также… для sharlotta никто не выставлял свои правила но почему то они указаны в админском exit rules и помечены как проблемные»*. Measured sequence: **19:44:10** karolina's route application times out (**one** SSH hiccup) → **19:54:07** the B309 exclusion fires, karolina leaves the healthy set and **251 of its claimed prefixes** move to emilia/shardlotta → **19:54:10** the very next application to karolina **succeeds** (`ssh=ok via tailnet`) and **nothing re-runs the assignment**, so it still owns **zero** prefixes 40 minutes later → `basic`'s 21 prefixes are pinned `via=emilia`/`via=sharlotta` while the device is pinned to karolina: no access, with every server-side fact green. The claims never moved (all 21 were claimed by karolina alone, 81 rules). Four defects: **(1) the ownership pass only ran from a sync** — the auto-updater tick now reconciles before its drift check, so a recovered relay reclaims its prefixes within one tick (5 min); **(2) an INSERT did not regenerate the ACL** (only a CHANGE did), so a rebuilt table (`inserted=191 changed=0`) kept every per-CIDR pin on the previous owner — the pass now re-applies on `ins > 0 || chg > 0`; **(3) a relay skygate had NEVER applied routes to was a candidate** — `sharlotta` owned **95** prefixes while advertising **2**, because the sync lists are built from `device_rules.exit_node_id` and no rule ever named it, so it was elected owner of prefixes whose pin it could not serve; the healthy set is now partitioned into **proven** relays (a recorded `relay_apply_state:<relay>` = `ok`) and the rest — proven-only when any relay is proven, the old behaviour when nothing is (fresh install); **(4) the sync lists are rules-derived**, so a relay that lost its last rule was never visited again and `tailscale set --advertise-routes=` (which REPLACES) kept its stale advertisement forever — `relaysToKeepSynced` adds every relay with a recorded apply and every relay the assignment table names, both `staggeredSync` and `SyncAdvertisedRoutes` visit them, and the per-row **Re-sync** now prunes a relay that has a recorded apply even when no rule targets it (the operator's only tool for a stale relay used to be SSH); rules with an empty `exit_node_id` (B277.3 "engine auto") no longer produce a node named `""` in the sync result (live: skyadmin's 22 youtube CDN rows). **Two live contracts renegotiated:** `check_b188.sh` X and `check_b188_2.sh` T hardcoded `via=[tag:dev-infra-emilia]` for `basic` — the relay that owned those prefixes on 2026-08-26 — so a legitimate ownership move turned them red on a healthy tailnet; they now assert the invariant they were expressing (**every per-CIDR pin names its prefix's owner in the assignment table, and at least one such pin exists**), which is also what catches the B276 class. **Live repair performed and verified:** the 191 auto ownership rows were handed back to the engine (snapshot `~/prefix_owner_snapshot_b352.sql`), `GET /admin/exit-rules/sync` → `karolina: ssh=ok approved=193`, `POST /admin/exit-rules/reapply` → `basic` = **22 grants, all `via=karolina`**, emilia's stale advertisement stripped on the relay (`--advertise-routes=0.0.0.0/0,::/0`) and its 29 stale approvals pruned via `headscale nodes approve-routes -i 3 -r "0.0.0.0/0,::/0"` → **0 prefixes approved on two relays**. **Still open:** the legacy `devices` table is stale (see §3.3), and skyadmin/cyborg still carries **22 enabled rules with an empty `exit_node_id`** (youtube CDN, created by the expander from a domain rule that has no relay) — they are inert, and the sync no longer reports them as a broken relay named `""`. 9 contracts in `scripts/check_b352_ownership_convergence.sh` + `TestB352_NeverAppliedRelayIsNotAnAssignmentCandidate`, `TestB352_RecoveredRelayReclaimsItsClaimedPrefixes`, `TestB352_FirstAssignmentReappliesACL`, `TestB352_RelaysToKeepSynced`, `TestB352_PruneTargetIsTheOwnedSet`.
- **B351** — (2026-10-05, live PostgreSQL deployment) **the admin page measured the PAGE, and the auto-updater wrote rows without their owner.** Operator report with a screenshot of `/admin/exit-rules`: *«Проверь доступность правил для пользователя michail от basic почему то правил 7 страниц у админа, и при этом на каждой по разному, посмотри что не так с отображением и нет ли обновлятора сервиса что переписывает в неправильном ключе»*. Measured on the live deployment (**349 enabled rows**, 2 users with rules): **(1) every counter was page-scoped.** The page is `ORDER BY r.id LIMIT 50 OFFSET n` while the CDN auto-updater inserts new rows for every device on every tick, so one `(user, device)` group is scattered across the table — and the groups *and* their counters were built from that slice: the same user read «19 правил / 3% (19/500)» on page 6 and «49 правил / 9% (49/500)» on page 7, while the heading said «ВСЕ ПРАВИЛА (50)» next to a pagination line on the same page saying «всего 349 правил». **(2) `device_rules.user_name` was empty on 220 of 349 rows — including ALL 68 rows of `basic`.** It is filled by exactly one thing, the one-time V0.44 migration: the runtime backfill that exists for `device_hostname` (`UpdateDeviceRuleHostnameForNode`) has **no twin**, and the auto-updater's two raw INSERTs (`sync.go`: the CDN expansion and the `/32` fallback) listed **neither** column — so the "wrong key" the operator suspected was real, and `DeviceRuleCountsForAdmin` (B348) grouped by it, listing one device **twice** (once under `michail`, once under `""`). **The device's ACCESS was never affected**, which is why nothing else looked broken: all 68 of `basic`'s rules named `karolina`, the `prefix_owner`, which advertises and has **188/188** routes approved, and the ACL carried `src=[tag:dev-michail-basic]` → those destinations with `via=[tag:dev-infra-karolina]`. Three-part fix: both INSERTs now resolve and write the pair from `portal_users` + `node_owner_map` (one lookup per unique pair per pass — never from the column being repaired, which would copy the blank forward); the auto-updater tick **heals** the rows already there via `db.BackfillDeviceRuleUserNames` (the missing twin of the hostname backfill, idempotent, so a quiet tick costs one statement); the admin inventory resolves the owner by `user_id` instead of trusting the column, and every counter (heading, system load, per-user quota badge, per-device, per-relay) is the **unpaginated database number** with an explicit «показано N из M». The quota badge deliberately reuses B328's unit (`enabled AND (target_type != 'subnet' OR parent_domain = '')`) so it quotes the limit the insert guard actually enforces, and the paged query orders by the group key (`r.user_id, r.device_id, r.id`) so a device's rows stay contiguous. **B348's doc comment claimed the blank meant "the row predates the backfill" — measured false, corrected.** 12 contracts in `scripts/check_b351_rule_owner_and_counts.sh` (its live half SKIPs when the rule table is unreachable) + `TestAdminRuleCounters_B351`, `TestBackfillDeviceRuleUserNames_B351` (+ `_PG`), `TestDeviceRuleCountsForAdmin_MergesBlankOwner_B351`, `TestRuleOwnerLookup_B351`.
- **B350** — (2026-10-04, live cyborg) **the client must resolve through the tailnet, and skygate's own instructions said otherwise.** Operator report: *«youtube на cyborg всё ещё недоступен»* while the **same** rule set carried `skyworker`. Every server-side fact was measured and green — 12 enabled rules for the device, its tenant tag applied, **10 per-CIDR grants** carrying `via=[tag:dev-infra-emilia]`, `prefix_owner` **10/10** on that relay, and the relay advertising **73 of 73** approved routes with all ten youtube prefixes among them — while the device answered `curl https://www.youtube.com` → **`Could not resolve host`** and `nslookup www.youtube.com 8.8.4.4` → `142.251.15x.4`. The missing half was a **client preference**: `--accept-dns=false`. skygate grants access by IP **prefix**, so a rule becomes reachable only once the device resolves the domain **into** that prefix; a filtering ISP resolver answers `NXDOMAIN` (or a substituted address), so the destination IP never exists on the device and the rule is unobservable — which is why the search went through rules, ACLs, routes and `prefix_owner`, none of which were wrong. **The cause was the product:** `/my/exit-rules`, `/my/exit-rules/help`, the preauth key page, the device-registration help, the Telegram add-device instructions, both i18n catalogues and `docs/windows-client.md` all printed `tailscale up --accept-routes --accept-dns=false` — and the **B49 gate contract ENFORCED that spelling** with a negative lookahead — while the preauth/bot registration commands carried **no flags at all**, so a device could not accept a route either. B49 is **renegotiated** in the same change (it keeps the historical bare-short-form intent; the negative lookahead is gone) and B350 owns the DNS rule: no user-facing surface may recommend `--accept-dns=false`; every catalogue/template **registration** command carries **both** `--accept-routes` and `--accept-dns=true`; the **server-side** installers keep `--accept-dns=false` (a relay is not a client); new warning surfaces (`exit_rules.client_dns_warn` on `/my/exit-rules`, `help.exit_rules_help.pitfall_client_dns` on the help page — the pitfalls list also gained the `safeHTML` it needed, its `<b>`/`<code>` had been printing literally) explain symptom → check → fix (`tailscale debug prefs` → `CorpDNS`, `tailscale set --accept-dns=true`, and the DNS-free proof `curl --resolve <host>:443:<pinned IP>`), and `docs/windows-client.md` + `docs/networking.md` §6.2 carry them. **What cannot be detected, measured so nobody re-invents it:** headscale carries no client DNS preference and `host_info.Services` is **not** a proxy — `peerapi-dns-proxy` was advertised by **all 12** nodes, the broken one included — so no page can alert on this and a correct default plus a named symptom is the only defence. **Also open (B350.1):** the deployment pins `8.8.8.0/24`/`8.8.4.0/24` to the relay (so tailnet DNS leaves through it), but `dns.nameservers.global` is `[1.1.1.1, 8.8.8.8]` and **nobody advertises `1.1.1.1`** (measured: `/32` and `/24` → no node), and the resolver prefixes are advertised only because a *user rule* happens to claim `8.8.8.8` — delete it and tailnet DNS degrades silently. 14 contracts in `scripts/check_b350_client_dns_truth.sh`; the B337 ratchet was paid in the same commit (`internal/i18n/catalog_bot.go` renormalised, 266 → 265), and that payment **exposed five real localization misses the drift had been hiding** — B325's B1 counter matches `"key"[[:space:]]*:` on one line while this file aligned its maps with a space BEFORE the colon, so it never counted a single value in the file; they are translated now, and `internal/i18n/catalog_user_subnet.go` still carries the same blind spot (ROADMAP §3.3).
- **B349** — (2026-10-04, found while verifying B348 on the live instance) **the admin per-device drill-down was a 500.** `GET /admin/exit-rules?device=cyborg` returned **HTTP 500** with the body **`sql: expected 12 destination arguments in Scan, not 13`** — so clicking any device on `/admin/exit-rules` (or arriving from the new B348 index) produced a raw error page instead of that device's rules, which is the other half of the operator's report *«пропали правила из админ страницы exit rules и по остальным устройствам что не skyworker»*. Root cause: **B277.4** added `COALESCE(r.all_devices, 0) AS all_devices` to `qSelectAllRulesForAdmin` **and** extended the shared scan loop `getAllRulesForAdminQuery` to **13** destinations — but **not** to `qSelectAllRulesForAdminByDevice`, which kept its **12**-column list; both callers share that one scan, so only the **filtered** view broke while the unfiltered one kept working (and nothing in the gate noticed: the SQL is only ever executed against a live DB, where the filtered path had no test). The fix makes the filtered column list match its sibling. The contract is deliberately **behavioural**: `TestGetAllRulesForAdminQueries_B349` runs **both** admin views through the real helpers and asserts that the join-side fields (`user_name`, `enabled`, `all_devices`) land in the positions the scan expects, the case-insensitive `LOWER()` match works, and an unknown device is an empty result rather than an error — because a text assertion cannot prove that a SELECT and its scan agree. **The negative control was measured by hand:** removing the column again reproduces the exact production error; restoring it passes. `scripts/check_b349_admin_device_filter_500.sh` also compares the **tail of the two column lists byte-for-byte** and counts the columns **depth-aware** (`COALESCE(a, b)` contains a comma) against the scan's destination count.
- **B348** — (2026-10-04, immediately after a rule was added for cyborg) **a device must never be invisible because of pagination.** Operator report: *«правила пропали у cyborg у пользователя добавились но админ не видит также пропали правила из админ страницы exit rules и по остальным устройствам что не skyworker»*. Measured live: **nothing was lost** — `device_rules` held **12 enabled rows for cyborg, 215 for skyworker, 89 for basic** — but the pages showed the pagination **window**: `/my/exit-rules` rendered «Страница 1 из 5, Текущие правила (50)» containing **only skyworker's** rows, and `/admin/exit-rules` page 1 of 7 also contained **only skyworker**. Both pages slice **rows** and then build their device groups from that slice, so the device owning the most rows owns the page — and page 1 was all skyworker because the auto-updater's CDN re-resolve pass had just added **~54 new /32 rows** for it. A device with no rows in the window is indistinguishable from a device with no rules. B348 makes the unit of navigation the **device**: `DeviceRuleCountsForUser` / `DeviceRuleCountsForAdmin` return one **unpaginated** inventory (every device with enabled rules and its count, the same "enabled + real hostname" rule as B343/B347), rendered as an index on both pages, and each entry links into a per-device drill-down that shows that device's rules **unpaged** (a device is bounded by the per-device cap). The drill-down filter matches **both** the displayed `DeviceName` and the **resolved device id**, so a device whose headscale name did not resolve (offline) is still reachable; on `/my/exit-rules` the post-save landing derives the same filter from `form_device_id`, so *«I just added a rule»* lands on that device's rules — and the filter is applied **before** the group maps are built, which is asserted by **line order** because a filter applied after them would change the slice and leave the rendered groups untouched. 22 contracts in `scripts/check_b348_device_navigation.sh` + `TestDeviceRuleCounts_B348` / `TestFilterRulesByDevice_B348` / `TestEqualFoldASCII_B348`. **Operator workaround while on an older build:** `?page_size=500` (the «На странице» selector) shows every row, and `/admin/exit-rules?device=<host>` was already an unpaged drill-down.
- **B347** — (2026-10-04, the same cyborg report) **the device → relay assignment must not be paginated away.** Operator report, twice: *«нигде не маркируется что у cyborg доступ теперь появился»* and *«сколько ни пробовал добавить доступ к youtube на cyborg не даёт»*. B343 made the state visible ON the page — a red «нет exit-node» badge for a device with enabled rules and no preference, a green «выход через: `<relay>`» for a pinned one — but those badges live **inside the per-device rule groups**, and the groups are built from the **current page** of rule rows. Measured on the operator's own account: `/my/exit-rules` rendered **«Страница 1 из 5 (всего 228 правил)»** with the default page size of 50, and page 1 contained **only skyworker's rows**, while the picker offered **«cyborg (1/500)»** — no group, no badge, so adding a rule for cyborg changed nothing the operator could see: the answer existed on page 2. `DeviceStatusRows` (`internal/feature/exit_rules/device_status_b347.go`) reads **every** device with enabled rules and the relay it is pinned to in **one query with no LIMIT**, reusing B343's exact "no preference row" `LEFT JOIN` scoped to the same `(user_id, device_hostname)` pair, on **TEXT columns only** so SQLite and PostgreSQL answer identically (the B319 INTEGER-vs-TEXT class is excluded by contract), skipping the pre-rename empty hostnames and the disabled-only devices. The strip is rendered **above** the paginated groups — the position is asserted, because a strip below them would inherit the same page window — one row per device with `id=device-status-active-<host>` / `device-status-inert-<host>`, and the post-save flash now names the relay the saved device ended up on (*«правило сохранено. Это устройство выходит через: `<relay>`»*), which is the half that answers the question actually asked. 26 contracts in `scripts/check_b347_device_status_strip.sh` + the DB-backed `TestDeviceStatusRows_B347` / `TestAssignedRelayFor_B347`.
- **B346** — (2026-10-04) **ONE pinned release every instance orients on.** Operator requirement: «Чтобы версии совпадали необходимо зафиксировать релиз на который ориентируются все экземпляры». Measured: `git ls-remote --tags origin` ended at **v1.5.94**, which points at **4b2186b (B328)**; the authoring tree was **21 commits ahead of `origin/main`** with none of them published; and the running instance answered `/healthz` with **`"build":"v1.5.94+4b2186b"` while its working tree carried those 21 commits**. Every surface therefore said "up to date", the tag described code that was not running, and no two instances could be proven to run the same code — because "follow the latest release" cannot express "every instance on THIS release", and the three paths that choose a target each decided alone: the `/admin/update` page and the **B130** scheduled updater both took GitHub's latest, and the **B342** cluster-onboarding block took `releaseTagFromBuild(primary's own label)`. `global_settings["update.pinned_release"]` now holds **one release tag**, with a single implementation in `internal/update/pinned_release.go`: `NormalizePinnedRelease` canonicalises `vX.Y.Z` and **rejects a branch, a raw commit, a `git describe` label and the updater's own `skygate-pre-update-*` tag** (a pin that cannot be checked out on every install kind is a lie about version equality), `ReleaseOfBuildLabel` extracts the release from `vX.Y.Z+<sha>` and stays **unknown** for a dev label so unknown can never be read as a match, and `PinnedTargetFor` is the pure decision. All three consumers read the **same** key — the page's target and therefore *Update now* (plus *Push update*, which otherwise rebuilds the divergent build it exists to replace), the scheduler **before** its GitHub call so an offline host with a pin still converges, and the onboarding block so a joining host is provisioned with the primary's release. Semantics are pinned by tests, not prose: a pin **older** than the running build **is** applied (that is how a host that drifted forward is pulled back) and "already on the pin" starts no job. The page shows the pin, the release this instance actually runs and a **drift banner**, and the Update button appears on **drift alone** — GitHub may have nothing newer while this host is off the pinned tag. 30 contracts in `scripts/check_b346_release_pin.sh`; `internal/i18n/catalog_update.go` left the B337 gofmt allow-list (267).
- **B345** — (2026-10-03, live cyborg) **the OWNER wins over a rule that names a non-owner relay.** Operator report: *«сколько ни пробовал добавить доступ к youtube на cyborg — не даёт»*. Measured: cyborg had 10 youtube subnets with «авто» plus a **duplicate `youtube.com` domain rule naming `karolina`**, while `prefix_owner` said **emilia** owned all ten prefixes — and emilia was the **only** node advertising them (37 routes, all approved; karolina advertised none). `device_exit_node_prefs` said **karolina**: the reconciler had pinned the DEVICE to the relay the RULE named while every per-CIDR ACL pin — and the actual route — said emilia. `via` is a permission **filter**, so the device's chosen exit node could not serve the destinations its own grants allowed: YouTube did not open however many rules were added. B275 made `prefix_owner` authoritative for the **destination** half and B265/B341.1 for the **device** half; B345 is the missing constraint between them — when the covered prefixes have **exactly one owner** and that owner carries a per-node tag, the preference names the **owner**, for both shapes: creating it (rules named a non-owner) and **repairing** it (an existing preference that names one, which is the state the live host was in), each with `reason=owner-overrides-rule-relay`. The guard stays narrow — one owner AND a resolved tag — so an unknown or split owner never overrides the operator, and the B341 test that asserted *"an existing preference is never overwritten"* is **renegotiated in the same change** into `TestPlan_B345_ExistingPreferenceIsKeptUnlessItNamesANonOwner` (four cases: the owner / a non-owner / no known owner / split owners), because a preference naming a relay that cannot carry the traffic **is** the breakage. Live repair performed: the duplicate rule deleted (natural key already held the «авто» row), preference set back to the owner, audit row written, then verified — rule «авто», pref emilia, `prefix_owner` emilia, ACL 11× `via=[tag:dev-infra-emilia]`. Contracts in `scripts/check_b345_owner_beats_rule_relay.sh` + the database-backed live-shape test `TestCollectDevicePrefState_OwnerBeatsRuleRelay_B345`.
- **B343** — (2026-10-02, operator report) **«правило есть, а доступа нет» must be visible ON THE PAGE, not only in the journal.** Verbatim: *«в интерфейсе всплывает уведомление что правило уже есть но нигде не маркируется что у cyborg доступ теперь появился и соответственно доступа тоже на него не распространяется»*. The notice the operator saw (`exit_rules.duplicate`, *"домен уже покрыт правилом"*) answers *"did I add a second copy?"* — a different question from *"does this device route through a relay NOW?"*. The state that was invisible: a device with **enabled rules and no `device_exit_node_prefs` row**. Its rules are **inert** — nothing pins the device to an exit node, so the client never selects one — while the generated ACL looks **correct**, because since B275 the per-CIDR `via=` pins come from `prefix_owner()` and not from the rule; every check that reads the policy therefore passes. Measured live: `cyborg` had 11 youtube rules, no preference, and no page said anything. B341/B341.1 made the reconciler **derive** the relay from `prefix_owner` on its own tick, so most devices heal automatically; B343 is the half that stays true when the derivation cannot decide (no owner for the prefixes, several owners, or an owner without a per-node tag) and it points at the journal reason (`missing-pref-…`). `DevicesWithoutExitNodePref` is a `LEFT JOIN … IS NULL` — *enabled rules and no preference row* — with disabled rules and hostname-less pre-rename rows excluded, so the banner stays believable; `/my/exit-rules` renders the banner **and** a per-device badge with its own translated label, `/admin/exit-rules` renders the banner for every user that has rules (one query per user, not per rule), and a failed marker query degrades to "nothing marked" instead of breaking the page. 20 contracts in `scripts/check_b343_inert_rules_visible.sh` + `internal/feature/exit_rules/inert_rules_b343_test.go` (the marker is exact in both directions).
