- **B342** — (2026-10-02, RR-15 option (a)) **onboard a second skygate host from the panel alone.** `/admin/cluster` has been a topology and lifecycle control plane since B199 (add a node row, mint an invite, approve, drain, rolling upgrade, HA chain, Patroni failover) but it could never tell the operator what to type **on the new host**: `docs/ha.md` §2.4 took five steps in three places — a preauth-key script on the primary, a token copied by hand, a hand-assembled `skygate join`, a heartbeat unit, then Approve — four of them outside the panel, and §2.4 states the criterion itself: *"If admin must SSH to do X, then X is a gap, not a feature."* RR-15 was **decided 2026-10-01 as option (a)**: the panel mints one-time credentials and renders a ready-to-run artifact, nothing is pushed to the target, and no third-party credential (an SSH key to somebody else's machine — option (b)) is stored anywhere. **One action**, `POST /admin/cluster/onboard` behind `authMW`, validates the hostname with the same `isSafeNodeName` B266 uses (the name is substituted into a shell command), bounds `ttl_hours` to 1..168, validates `api_url`, ensures the `cluster_node` row through the shared helpers, mints the invite with `cluster.IssueInvite` **and** a tailnet preauth key for the **`infra`** user (`CreatePreauthKeyWithTags` — never the synthetic `tagged-devices` user, the B175 Strategy E trap the standalone script exists to avoid), then parks the payload as JSON in `global_settings` under an **opaque 16-byte token**. `GET /admin/cluster?boot=<token>` consumes it **once** and renders the numbered block: tailnet (`--netfilter-mode=nodivert`, never `off` — the iptables trap), install the **exact version this primary runs** from GitHub Releases with the SHA256 verified (a standby ahead of its primary is the documented upgrade-order trap; an unknown/dev build resolves `latest` instead of inventing a tag), `skygate join` with `--write-dsn-to` + the state file, `systemctl enable` + healthz, then **Approve in the panel**. **Secrets:** the invite token and the preauth key appear only inside the rendered commands — never in the redirect URL (which carries the opaque token) — and the audit row records **lengths** (`invite_len`/`ts_key_len`), because a token in an audit detail is a token in every backup; the payload is deleted on consume and swept after 15 minutes if the page is never opened. 25 contracts in `scripts/check_b342_cluster_onboard.sh` (route + guards + one-time parking + credential placement + shared primitives + template + RU/EN parity of every `cluster.onboard_*` key, computed from the sources + the pure unit tests) and 7 pure tests in `internal/feature/admin/cluster_onboard_b342_test.go`.
- **B341** — (2026-10-01, operator report) **a rule that names no relay must still decide the DEVICE, and that decision must come from the same table the ACL pin comes from.** Verbatim report: *«добавил для cyborg правило для youtube, правило уже есть, однако не распространяется на cyborg; на skyworker всё отрабатывает»*. Measured on the reference VM: `cyborg` had **11 youtube rules with an empty `exit_node_id`** (the only device in `device_rules` in that state) and **no `device_exit_node_prefs` row**, while `basic`'s identical rules named `emilia` and `skyworker` had `tag:dev-infra-karolina` with `via_enabled=1`. In the live headscale policy cyborg's youtube CIDRs were **already pinned `via=[tag:dev-infra-emilia]`, exactly like skyworker's** — because since B275 the per-CIDR pin comes from `prefix_owner()`, **not** from the rule. So the policy looked correct while the device had no exit node at all, and the rule had no effect. **Two defects made that invisible:** (1) `ReconcileDeviceExitNodePrefs` listed the devices to consider with `AND exit_node_id <> ''`, so a relay-less device was **not even a candidate** — nothing derived, nothing logged; (2) even if listed, `PlanDevicePrefChange` answered an unresolvable canonical tag with a **silent `return nil, false`**, indistinguishable from "everything is fine". **The fix derives the device's exit node from `prefix_owner()`** when exactly one relay owns the prefixes its rules cover — not a guess: it is the decision the data plane already made, and the ACL pin reads the same table — and otherwise returns a **NAMED skip** (`missing-pref-no-relay`, `missing-pref-owner-split`, `missing-pref-owner-untagged`, `missing-pref-relay-untagged`) that reaches the journal. **Non-blocking by construction** (operator requirement): the new path only runs for a device with **no** preference row, never writes `device_rules`, creates only when the owner is unique *and* tagged, and saving a rule without a relay is still allowed — `skyworker`/`basic`/`a71` keep taking the old branch, pinned by `TestPlan_B341_ExistingPreferenceIsNeverOverwritten`. The new `db.OwnerTagCountsForDeviceRules` (portable `$N` + `CAST(device_id AS TEXT)`, counted over the per-CIDR population only, so a domain row cannot skew it) is tested on **both** backends. 22 contracts in `scripts/check_b341_exit_rule_relay_truth.sh`; its live half prints the actionable list (`cyborg → derivable from prefix_owner: emilia`, 10 pinned-but-relay-less rules) and **SKIPs** without the live DB.
- **B340** — (2026-10-01, measured) **a check must not write a FIXED `/tmp` path — eight of that day's failures were an environment artefact and not one was about the product.** The run reported `B148` (cert.* i18n parity), `B149` (ha.*), `B150` (deploy.*), `B169` (*"PostAdminDeviceDelete is NOT admin-only"*), `B171` (*"devicedelete.Delete does NOT call db.DeleteRulesByDeviceID"*), `B172` (*"GetLogin does NOT read ?next="*), `B-mod-core` and `B-mod-db-retry` — every message naming a **source** contract — while **every log those checks pointed at said `ok`**, and the line that mattered was `scripts/check_b169.sh: line 46: /tmp/_b169_awk.txt: Permission denied`. `sudo` on the reference VM gives uid 0 **without** `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` (`capsh --print`: an **empty** current set), so a `/tmp` file left by the earlier operator-user run **cannot be truncated** by the root run AGENTS trap #8 prescribes; the redirect fails, the check reads a stale or empty artefact and announces a product regression. The failure is **order-dependent** — the same tree was green at 10:40 and red at 11:30. The class had been paid for twice already (`B3`/`B18`/`B90` built to `/tmp/skygate_verify*` and `/tmp/x`) and each time only the files that fired were fixed, because the population was never measured; it is now: **24 check scripts and 24 inline `run_check` commands** wrote fixed paths. All use a per-run directory (`SKY_TMP="$(mktemp -d /tmp/skygate-check.XXXXXX)"` + `EXIT` trap; `f=$(mktemp)` for the inline scripts), the four that owned their own trap or read their scratch file from inside a quoted heredoc were hand-edited (pinned by contract C), and `scripts/check_b340_no_fixed_tmp_scratch.sh` keeps the count at zero — with a **behavioural** contract that plants a fixed-path write and fails if the detector cannot see it, so "no violations" can never mean "the detector broke". 12 contracts; the trap is AGENTS §2 #13 and the write-up is L-51 in `docs/LESSONS.md`.
- **B339** — (2026-10-01, refactor Phase D) **a contract reads a SURFACE, not a FILE.** Every B-check written as `grep <pattern> <one path>` was coupled to the code's *layout*, and that coupling only ever fired in the wrong direction: splitting `internal/telegram/commands_user.go` (1983 lines → nine files) put **B279 contract E2** red with *"commands_user.go no longer uses PickPerNodeTag"* while the telegram layer still used it three files away, and splitting `internal/feature/admin/tailscale.go` (1963 → seven) put **B236, B251, B258, B258.1, B259, B318, B320, B321** plus two source-grep Go tests red in one step, none of them because a behaviour was lost. The form is also **weaker** while green — it cannot see the behaviour re-introduced in a sibling file, which is the regression it exists to catch. The repair is `scripts/lib/gosurface.sh` (`gosurface VAR <file-or-glob>…`): it concatenates the named Go files into one temp file, **skipping `_test.go`** (a marker only a test mentions must not satisfy a production contract) and expanding the globs **inside** itself so a caller running under `set -f` still gets a surface — `check_b258_1` sets `set -f` on purpose, and with the glob left literal the surface silently became the one file that still matched, so contract C announced *"tailscaleAuthKeyMissingForStart helper missing"* while the helper sat two files away. It concatenates rather than handing `grep` a glob because `grep -c` prints **one count per file operand**, which would turn every existing count arithmetic into a `file:number` list. Contracts: the helper exists/tracked/handles `set -f`/excludes `_test.go`; no check script or catalog `run_check` still names a split file as a grep/sed/awk operand outside a `gosurface` argument; all eight admin/tailscale contracts source and call it; and it is exercised **behaviourally** on a synthetic file pair under `set -f` (prod marker present, test marker absent, no-match input must fail rather than pass vacuously) plus on the real surface, where a marker from `tailscale_config.go` proves the call sites concatenate. 15 contracts in `scripts/check_b339_refactor_surface_contracts.sh`; the lesson is L-50 in `docs/LESSONS.md`. **Extended 2026-10-02 (29 contracts)** to the largest surface in the tree: `cmd/skygate/main.go` (4 515 lines) had **655 occurrences of its path across 168 scripts, of which only 196 were operands** — 236 are the "am I in the checkout?" existence test, 100 are `ok`/`bad`/`echo` messages and 48 are comments, so a global replace corrupts the checks' own documentation (it rewrote 47 catalog descriptions before the classifier was fixed to look at the line's COMMAND position). All 196 operands in 111 scripts now go through `gosurface … cmd/skygate/*.go` (or the quoted glob inside a `for … in` list), and the check scans for a new one. Two mechanical traps are pinned by the sweep's own rules: a quoted operand must be consumed WITH its closing quote (`"$SKY_MAIN""` produced 15 syntax errors) and the inserted call must land on a statement boundary, not inside a `\`-continued `if` chain. The sweep also exposed a bug in the helper: its cleanup was spliced from `trap -p` TEXT, so the **second** surface in one shell installed an unparseable EXIT trap (`exit trap: line 1: unexpected EOF while looking for matching quote` after a 13/0 verdict) — temp files are now an array with one handler, and the caller's own trap is chained (contracts A6/A7 + behavioural C5/C6). Lesson: L-55.
- **B338** — (2026-10-01) **three PG-gated tests in `internal/monitoring` had never run anywhere, and all three were STALE rather than product bugs.** They went through `db.OpenForTest`, which SKIPs without `SKYGATE_TEST_PG_DSN`, while CI's `test-pg` job was scoped to `./internal/db/...` and the VM gate ran without a DSN — so they skipped in both places and drifted three blocks behind the code; with the DSN set, `B1` failed on them. **(1)** `TestComputeSnapshot_OnlineAllOK` had drifted **twice** from B273: it did not set `NodeView.IsExitNode` (so `computeSnapshot` returned `ok=false` and produced no snapshot at all) and it set only `AvailableRoutes` while naming itself *AllOK* — but B273's ladder requires the `0.0.0.0/0` route to be **approved** to call a relay online, which is exactly what that block exists for (pre-B273 an unapproved relay counted as healthy). **(2)** `TestTick_DegradedTransition_RecordedButNotAlerted` had the same missing `IsExitNode` in its **second** fixture, so no row was written and the transition it asserted could never happen. **(3)** `TestTick_AutoSyncEnabled_InsertsAndUpdates` had drifted from **B279**: its fixtures carried only the **class** tag `tag:exit-node` and it expected the sync to write that value into `node_owner_map`, but B279 made that a deliberate no-op — the class tag is a role, not an identity, and writing it over the operator's per-node tag is the live `aro` reverter (after which the B272 reconciler compared the row against headscale, found the class tag it had just been given, and reported nothing). The fixtures now carry real per-node tags and the assertions expect them, and a new `TestTick_AutoSync_ClassTagOnlyLeavesRowAlone_B338` pins B279's fix so the reverter cannot return unnoticed. The `run_check` runs the four tests; **without** a DSN they SKIP (honest — rule 1), and the with-DSN half that makes them mandatory is contract **D5 of B334**. Verified on the reference VM with the DSN: all four PASS, whole package `ok` in 22.8 s. 4 Go tests in `internal/monitoring/exit_node_monitor_test.go`.
- **B337** — (2026-10-01) **`gofmt` must be enforceable as a RATCHET, not a rule nobody can satisfy.** Rule 3 has said "every file you *touch* must be `gofmt`-clean" for a while and **nothing enforced it** — `grep -rln gofmt scripts/ .github/ Makefile` found only two comments. Measured on the reference VM 2026-10-01 with `go1.25.4`: **847 tracked `.go` files, 276 not `gofmt`-clean (32.6 %)**. Renormalising all of them at once is a ~30k-line whitespace-only commit that would bury any real change in the same push and poison `git blame` for every line of those files, so the drift is **frozen instead** — exactly like the B325 i18n budgets, where the counts are pinned and may only fall. `scripts/gofmt_legacy_allowlist.txt` carries the 276 paths plus the measurement and the rules in its header, and the contract enforces four things: every tracked `.go` file **not** in the list must be clean (anything new or touched is held to the rule); any listed file that has **become** clean must be **removed** (the list may only shrink — that is what makes it a ratchet rather than a permanent exemption); the list may never grow, with its size asserted against the frozen count; and it is sorted under `LC_ALL=C` and unique, so a change to it reviews as one line per file and the order does not depend on the host locale. It also carries the **file-level** half of rule 3 that a whole-tree ratchet cannot express — every `.go` file changed in *this* tree must be clean even if it is allow-listed, so "I only edited it a little" is not an exemption. That check is what caught three files touched by the `/admin/database` work (`internal/feature/admin/database.go`, `internal/i18n/catalog_admin.go`, `internal/i18n/catalog_common.go`) which were still drifted; they are `gofmt`-clean now. 17 contracts in `scripts/check_b337_gofmt_ratchet.sh`.
- **B336** — (2026-10-01) **a live-state check must not report a read failure as a fact about the database.** Six of the ten FAILs in the baseline gate run came from four checks that had **never reached the database** — the tell is the *empty* count in the message (`FAIL B190 A.1 found  b188_* users in portal_users (expected 0)`, `FAIL B189 B.4 derp_health table NOT in live DB`, `FAIL B118 live policy:  references to svyatoslava-legacy`). The cause is a decision this project made **on purpose**: B278 removed the docker bridge IP from `SKYGATE_DB_DSN` and made the DSN host a docker DNS name (`skygate-pg-local`), because the bridge IP rotates on every container recreate. That name resolves only **inside** the compose network, so a host-side `psql` answers `could not translate host name "skygate-pg-local" to address`; every one of those checks sent stderr to `/dev/null` and then treated the empty result as data. Measured through the new helper on the same deployment: `derp_health` = `derp_health` (**the table IS there**), svyatoslava refs = 0, `tag:dev-infra-*` owners = 4, `node_owner_map` infra rows = 4, `b188_*` debris = 0 — so **every one of those FAILs was fabricated and the contracts all hold**. That is the B294 lesson (a failed read must not render as a fact) applied to the shell checks, and it violated rule 1: an unavailable dependency must SKIP, never FAIL. The fix is a live-DB access layer in `scripts/lib/db_credentials.sh`: `skygate_live_db_probe` runs psql **inside** the container when the DSN host names one — no host networking, immune to the rotation B278 is about, and exactly what `docs/operations.md` §15 tells the operator to do by hand — falls back to a plain DSN for external PostgreSQL (Patroni, `deploy/pg-ha`), and on failure records the **real** error in `SKYGATE_LIVE_DB_ERR`; `skygate_live_db_query` is the only way the checks read the database and it **refuses to run without a successful probe**, so "zero rows" can no longer mean "no connection". B118, B119, B189, B190 and B188.2 all probe first and SKIP with the named reason. Two **vacuous** contracts surfaced in the same pass: B119's predicate ended with an always-true `NOT LIKE '%'`, so its count was always 0 and the contract could never fail; and B118 contract G searched the SQL for a **literal placeholder hostname** (an earlier redaction put it inside the SQL strings — rule 6), a string that cannot exist in any database, so three of its contracts were guaranteed green — G now asserts every `tag:dev-infra-*` is one of the four sanctioned relays, which **can** fail and catches the retired HA mirror coming back. B188.2 contract X also pointed at a hardcoded, long-dead `172.17.0.1:5000`. A **SQL error is a read failure too**, which surfaced while renegotiating B188.2 contract U: the query helper swallowed psql stderr, so a malformed `GROUP BY 1` read as "no rows" (measured) — `skygate_live_db_query` now returns non-zero on a failed statement, records the reason in `SKYGATE_LIVE_DB_QUERY_ERR`, and the docker prefix is decided **once** so a failed command is not silently re-run. **B188.2 contract U was renegotiated** (B275 semantics): it asserted `skyworker` must **never** carry `via=emilia`, written 2026-08-26 — before B265 made the pin conditional, B274 made one relay the advertiser of each prefix, and B275 moved the decision into `prefix_owner`. Measured on the VM: all **200** of skyworker's enabled subnet/ip rules declare `exit_node_id=karolina`, `prefix_owner` splits those same prefixes **77 emilia / 123 karolina** (`source=explicit`), and the live policy carries **exactly** 77 emilia / 123 karolina — the control plane **agrees** with the data plane, and the old assertion was measuring the pre-B275 design. The first version of that renegotiation compared per-owner **counts** with a ±10 tolerance and was itself flaky against the reconciler it watches — two runs minutes apart measured `77/77` (diff 0) then `31/47` (diff 16) while the autoupdater rewrote `device_rules` and B275 re-assigned prefixes. The contract is therefore **set membership**: every relay named by a `via` on skyworker's h-rule grants must be a **current** owner in `prefix_owner` — the invariant B275 guarantees, and the one that catches the **B276** class (a pin naming a relay that owns nothing, which silently filters the client's routes away). Verified on the reference VM: B118 **18 pass / 0 fail**, B119 0 fail, B189 0 fail, B190 0 fail, B188.2 **20 pass / 0 fail** (U green, X `rules=129 via=129 diff=0`). 31 contracts in `scripts/check_b336_live_db_access.sh` **D3 пересмотрен в B437** (2026-10-28): он требовал буквальную переменную `SANCTIONED=`, то есть тот самый замороженный список из четырёх relay-ов, который B437 убрал — CI покраснел на этом (`failing: B336`), потому что контракт закреплял механизм, который сам B437 и заменил. Смысл D3 не изменился: у B118 должен быть контракт разрешённого множества, который МОЖЕТ упасть, — поэтому теперь он требует выведение множества из живого списка узлов headscale и отдельную проверку маркера legacy/retired.
- **B335** — (2026-10-01) **the guarantee catalog must have a verdict, not just a report.** Measured on the reference VM (v1.5.94, 2026-10-01): the catalog ended with **368 PASS, 10 FAIL, 1 SKIP and exit code 0**. `verify_pre_deploy.sh` maintained `RESULTS_PASS`/`RESULTS_FAIL` and never read them, so the script's status was its **last statement's** — `.githooks/pre-push` branches on that status and therefore blocked nothing, and the only enforcement anywhere was CI's grep of the log for the `FAIL` prefix. B322 made the individual checks able to fail; this block makes the catalog's own verdict real: the summary prints `PASS / FAIL / TIMEOUT / SKIP / checks / sub-contract SKIP rows`, a **TIMEOUT counts as a failure** (a check killed at its budget produced no result, which is not a pass), the failing check **names** are listed so the operator need not scroll a 400-line wall of PASS rows, and the file now **ends** on the verdict's own `exit 0` / `exit 1` so no later statement can decide the status again. A SKIP never fails the gate — AGENTS rule 1 says a check whose live dependency is absent must SKIP, so the sub-contract SKIP count is printed as information instead. The contract has a **behavioural half**, because a source grep cannot tell "prints a summary" from "prints a summary and then exits 0": it **extracts** the verdict block from the real catalog and executes it against synthetic counters, keeping the check and the code in lock-step (clean run → 0, the measured baseline of 10 FAIL → non-zero, a lone TIMEOUT → non-zero, SKIPs alone → 0). Writing it also tripped **AGENTS trap #9 inside the check itself** — a producer piped into `grep -q` under `pipefail` dies with SIGPIPE (141) and reports a failed pipeline although the match succeeded — so the extraction is captured once and matched with herestrings. 23 contracts in `scripts/check_b335_gate_verdict.sh`.
- **B334** — (2026-10-01) **the PostgreSQL half of the test suite must actually run, and the gate must say which database it exercised.** `db.OpenTestPG` SKIPs when `SKYGATE_TEST_PG_DSN` is unset, and every PG-gated test in the tree goes through it. Measured 2026-10-01, the packages that call `OpenTestPG(` are `internal/db` (13 call sites), `internal/feature/admin` (18) and `internal/acl` (4). CORRECTED by B338 (measured 2026-10-01): the PG-gated set is EIGHT packages, not three, because tests reach PostgreSQL three ways — db.OpenTestPG(t), db.OpenForTest(t) (which SKIPs without the DSN and delegates to OpenTestPG with it) and a raw os.Getenv("SKYGATE_TEST_PG_DSN") check. The first measurement grepped OpenTestPG( alone and lost internal/monitoring (11 sites) and internal/invite (7); internal/headscale, internal/backup and cmd/skygate use the raw DSN. That gap was not academic: with the DSN set, B1 failed on three tests in internal/monitoring that had been silently skipping since B273/B279 and had never run in CI either (test-pg was scoped to ./internal/db/...). check_b334 contract D1b now derives the set from all three markers and fails when it changes. CI's `test-pg` job ran `go test ./internal/db/...`, and `verify_pre_deploy.sh` never set the variable at all — so the live-PG tests in `internal/feature/admin` and `internal/acl` ran **nowhere**: CI never reached those packages and the gate SKIPped them, while both reported green. That is TD-24, and it is not hypothetical — the 2026-09-25 gate audit found two real PostgreSQL-only regressions (a SQLite-only `INSERT OR IGNORE` on a shared path in `internal/mesh/mesh.go` and `internal/subnet/shares.go`) that no job exercised, precisely because no job ran those paths against PostgreSQL. Fixed on both sides: CI's job now runs `go test ./... -count=1` with the DSN and carries the `timeout-minutes` it lacked, and the gate prints `PostgreSQL test coverage: ENABLED` or `SQLite only — see docs/operations.md` on **every** run, so a SQLite-only verdict can no longer be mistaken for full coverage. The gate deliberately only **reports** the DSN and never assigns one: a DSN guessed by the gate would create `skygate_pgtest_*` schemas in whatever database it names. Measuring the docs also found a defect in the recipe they pointed at — `docs/operations.md` §15 told the operator to prove the DSN can create a schema with `CREATE SCHEMA pg_gate_probe`, but PostgreSQL reserves the `pg_` prefix for system schemas (`unacceptable schema name … The prefix "pg_" is reserved for system schemas`), so the documented sanity check failed on a correctly configured server and looked exactly like the permission problem it was meant to rule out (measured on PostgreSQL 18.4); the recipe now probes with `gate_probe` and records why. The contract also measures live: without the DSN `internal/acl`'s PG test must SKIP (proving the probe can detect missing coverage), and with the DSN it must really PASS in `internal/acl` and `internal/feature/admin`, so "the DSN is set" can never be confused with "the tests ran". **MEASURED with the DSN** (pointed at `skygate_citest`, a database the role owns so `OpenTestPG` can `CREATE SCHEMA`): all three `OpenTestPG` packages pass against real PostgreSQL with **0 SKIP rows** — `internal/db` ok in 210 s (the one CI did cover), `internal/acl` ok in 4.8 s and `internal/feature/admin` ok in 62.8 s (the two that ran **nowhere** before) — and the contract reports 24/0/0 with the DSN set, D3/D4 asserting that those two packages' tests really ran. The gate banner prints `PostgreSQL test coverage: ENABLED`. 23 contracts in `scripts/check_b334_pg_test_coverage.sh`.
- **B333** — (2026-10-01) **the two migration chains must agree, and each registry entry must name the file that really defines it.** Skygate ships two migration registries — `pgMigrations` (`internal/db/driver_postgres.go`) and `sqliteMigrations` (`internal/db/driver_sqlite.go`) — and their whole premise is interchangeability: same version numbers, same `Name` labels, same `SourceFile` authorship, only the DDL inside `migrateVxxxPG` / `migrateVxxxSQLite` differs. TD-17 measured the **result** (both chains reach V77 and produce identical tables and columns) but nothing ever checked the **metadata**, and a 2026-10-01 audit found real drift. **(1)** V070's label read `v0.70 (B238)` in the PostgreSQL registry and `v0.70 (B236)` in the SQLite one — B236 is the Tailscale-ACL block, an entirely different change — so `skygate migrate status` and the `/admin/database` audit page answered a **different question depending on the backend the host runs**, which is precisely the question an operator asks before a cutover. **(2)** V060, V061 and V062 pointed `SourceFile` at `migrations_v0_60_b183_test.go`, `migrations_v0_61_b188_test.go` and `migrations_v0_62_b194.go` while all three functions are actually defined in `migrations_pg.go` (the first two in **both** chains), so the authorship recorded in `applied_migrations.source_file` was wrong — and for two of them named a `_test.go` file that is not even compiled into a release binary. That column is the trail an operator reads after a failed migration. The contract is hermetic (no database) and resolves each entry's `Run` function with `runtime.FuncForPC(...).FileLine(...)`, so it **cannot be satisfied by editing a label** — the registry has to name the truth: same versions/order/`Name`/`SourceFile` across both chains; no version registered twice (`applied_migrations` is keyed on the version, so one duplicate would silently never run); PostgreSQL is the authoritative chain, so its `SourceFile` must be the file that defines `migrateV<NNN>PG`; the SQLite port must live in the generated consolidated `migrations_sqlite.go` or in the authorship file the registry names, never a third file; and `SourceFile` is never a `_test.go` path. A mutation test feeds the detector five synthetic pairs reproducing each real drift class (missing version, label drift, test-file authorship, cross-chain `SourceFile` drift, swapped order) and asserts it reports every one, plus a quarantined identical pair it must stay silent on. **It also closes TD-25:** the B233 shape-drift audit skipped `migrations_sqlite.go` wholesale (mixing the two chains into one walk made every index look created twice), so the SQLite chain had **no** shape-drift coverage at all — a file now belongs to a chain exactly when it defines a `migrateV<NNN>PG` or `migrateV<NNN>SQLite` function (so `migrations_v0_77_exit_location.go`, which defines both, correctly lands in both chains), its decision logic moved into the pure `shapeDriftOffenders` helper, and the mutation test now drives **that** function instead of an inlined copy — an inlined copy proves nothing about the code that actually runs. The V068/B232 repair is pinned on **both** backends by extracting each chain's `migrateV068` body and requiring its `DROP` to precede its `CREATE`, which is the only version pin that can work on the SQLite side, where the legacy ports V025–V070 share the generated file. 8 Go contracts across `internal/db/migrations_registry_parity_test.go` and `internal/db/migrations_audit_b233_test.go`.
- **B331** — (2026-10-01) **the smoke-mesh cleanup must work on both backends — it was silently dead on SQLite.** `internal/mesh/cleanup.go` deleted its candidate rows with `WHERE id = ANY($1::bigint[])`, passing the id list as a PostgreSQL array literal (`{1,2,3}`) in one parameter. That is PostgreSQL-only syntax, and `DeleteSmokeMeshes` runs from `mesh.StartCleanupScheduler` on **every** install kind, so SQLite answered `SQL logic error: near "[]": syntax error (1)` — measured by reverting the fix and re-running the new test. The failure was invisible until it mattered: the `Total == 0` early return means the DELETE is reached **only when there is cruft to remove**, so B143's cleanup looked present and did nothing on every SQLite install, exactly when it had work to do. **No contract could have caught it**, and that is the lesson: `internal/mesh/cleanup_b143_test.go`'s own header said *"the SQL contract (DeleteSmokeMeshes) is covered by `scripts/check_b143.sh` live on the VM"* — and the reference VM is PostgreSQL — while its pure-Go tests pinned `int64ArrayToPGArray`, the helper that **produced** the PostgreSQL-only argument. The test suite was pinning the bug instead of the behaviour. The fix passes the ids as N separate placeholders in an `IN (…)` list built through `db.PlaceholdersList` / `db.PlaceholderAt` (`$N` is the universal form — SQLite accepts it as a named parameter and modernc.org/sqlite binds it by Go argument ordinal), the PG array helper is deleted, and `TestDeleteSmokeMeshes_SQLite` runs the real statement against a real migrated SQLite database (it fails with the exact SQLite error when the old form is restored — verified). The block also carries the **class guard TD-27 asked for**: contract B1 asserts there is no live `ANY($…)` in any tracked non-test Go file (comments may quote it; `internal/db` legitimately builds dialect-specific SQL, but a live `ANY($` in a shared statement is a bug), and contract C4 rejects a `-run` filter that matches nothing, so the test cannot pass vacuously. 11 contracts in `scripts/check_b331_mesh_cleanup_dialect.sh`.
- **B330** — (2026-10-01) **the documentation must agree with the tree, measured rather than assumed.** The 2026-10-01 audit found four defects that no contract could catch, because none of them looked at the docs at all: (1) `docs/ROADMAP.md` still described the **v1.5.9** cycle (`Last updated 2026-09-18`, `Version line v1.5.9`) while the tree was at v1.5.94 — **85 releases of drift**, with `TD-4` (S3 backups, shipped in v1.3.8), `TD-19` (0 remaining sites), `RR-8` and `RR-9` all shown as OPEN for work that had shipped; (2) `docs/ru/ROADMAP.md` had diverged from its English original — its TD table stopped at TD-20 (no TD-21/TD-22 rows and **neither** detail section), RR-10 asserted the opposite of the English text, and it carried operator SQL the English one did not; (3) three relative links were broken (`docs/ru/README.md` → `LICENSE` twice and `docs/ru/ROADMAP.md` → `operations.md`) because `docs/ru/` is one directory deeper than the files it points at; (4) `docs/README.md`, which calls itself *"Catalogue of every document in this directory"*, omitted `i18n-audit.md` and `responsive-audit.md` — and `check_b325_i18n_regressions.sh` cites the former as its evidence. Fixed in that pass (ROADMAP rewritten for v1.5.94 with the v1.5.9 table kept as §3.1 — restoring it was **required**, not cosmetic: `check_b237_23.sh` G.2 and `check_b237_24.sh` C.1 assert this file records those two blocks — the RU mirror re-synced, the links repaired, the catalogue completed, `AGENTS.md` rules 3 and 10 narrowed to what is actually enforced, the B96 index line corrected from "22 pages into 6 sections" to the 10 that exist, and `i18n-audit.md` marked as a frozen snapshot). The contract now measures: no broken relative link under `docs/`; the RU roadmap mirrors the EN section numbering **and** subsection count; **both** roadmaps name the newest `## vX.Y.Z` heading from `RELEASE-NOTES.md` (the drift detector that would have caught all 85 releases); the catalogue names every `docs/*.md`; the four claimed bilingual pairs exist; and the script is tracked + registered. 15 contracts in `scripts/check_b330_docs_consistency.sh`.
- **B329** — (2026-09-28) **the database tab must name the backend in use and be able to change it.** Operator request: «Нету в панели администратора вкладки с тем какая сейчас используется БД SQLite или Postgress а также на этой странице кнопки миграции если администратор решит что хочет перевести все данные на другой тип БД». Measured before this block: `/admin/database` had **no sidebar entry** (`layout.html`'s Data section and `sectionPageSet()` both listed only backup/invites/control-planes; the only in-app link was a button on `/admin/cluster`); the page could not name the backend (`databasePageData` had no dialect field and **no template contained the string `sqlite`**); on a SQLite install the page was **broken rather than unhelpful** — `collectDatabasePageData` parsed `SKYGATE_DB` with `parseLibpqDSN` (accepts `postgres://` only) and probed it with `sql.Open("pgx", …)`, so a healthy SQLite install rendered *"could not parse SKYGATE_DB_DSN"* plus a failed PostgreSQL probe; the **Migrate** card was PostgreSQL→PostgreSQL only (`internal/dbmigrate/handlers.go` hardcoded the `postgres://` DSN; `grep sqlite internal/dbmigrate` = **0**); and the one cross-backend tool, `skygate db-migrate` → `db.Convert`, **refused a PostgreSQL source** (`convert.go`: *"only SQLite source supported in v1.5.4"*), so **PostgreSQL → SQLite — the direction the operator needs — could not run at all**. This block adds (1) the **backend card**: the kind comes from `db.ActiveDialect()` (the process's own answer, so it cannot disagree with reality), the SQLite branch shows the file path + size, both branches read their own version (`sqlite_version()` / `server_version`) and table count **through the live connection**, and a mismatch between the env DSN and the live dialect is surfaced instead of hidden; (2) the **sidebar tab** (`layout.html` + `sectionPageSet` + `pageLabel` + `pageTitle`, B96-checked in both directions); (3) **`POST /admin/database/convert`**, which refuses a same-kind or already-occupied target *before opening anything*, opens the target through the new **`db.OpenIsolated`** — registered for `BackendOf` (the migration chain reads it) but **deliberately not** as the process-wide active dialect, because that value is what every concurrent request's SQL-fragment shims branch on and flipping it would send PostgreSQL SQL to a live SQLite server — and renders the per-table report on the page instead of flashing one line; (4) **`db.Convert` rewritten to work in both directions**: the target schema is created by the **target's own migration chain** (columns, indexes, triggers and partial UNIQUE indexes included, instead of six regex substitutions over the source's `CREATE TABLE` text), the tables are filled **parents-first from the target's own FOREIGN KEY metadata** by a real topological sort that names a cycle instead of failing halfway, every value is coerced to the **target column's declared type** (SQLite `INTEGER` unix seconds vs PostgreSQL `timestamptz`, booleans 0/1 vs `bool`), the copy runs in **one transaction**, per-table row counts are read back and compared (the old doc comment *claimed* this verification; the code did `_ = n`), and the **PostgreSQL identity sequences are advanced** past the copied ids so the first INSERT after a conversion cannot collide; (5) the CLI accepts the **`--from=<dsn>`** form its own `--help` documents (it answered `unknown flag` — measured); (6) the stale **`db.migrate_help` "STUB"** claim (which outlived B202 by ~90 releases) is corrected in both catalogues. 44 contracts in `scripts/check_b329_db_backend_ui.sh` + `internal/db/open_isolated_test.go` (the isolation contract, including a test that proves the *ordinary* opener does write the process-wide value, so the two paths stay distinguishable) and the rewritten `internal/db/convert_test.go`.
- **B328** — (2026-09-25) **the rule caps must count what they name, and the operator must be able to change them.** Operator report: «не получилось добавить правило на другое устройство пользователя… правила что были заданы не распространяются на другие устройства». Measured on the live host: `/my/exit-rules` showed **`cyborg (1/500)`** in the picker and refused the save with **`device limit exceeded: 500/500 user-facing rules on this device`**. Both numbers were real and came from **different queries**: the page counted per `(user, device)` user-facing rows (`1`), while the guard called `countUserFacing(0, devID, false)` — and that closure's `switch` had **no "device without user" case**, so it fell through to `db.CountEnabledRules` = `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM device_rules WHERE enabled = 1` = **500**, compared against `SKYGATE_MAX_RULES_PER_DEVICE=500`. **Every insert for every user was refused**, and the message blamed their device. The correct helper existed (its doc comment claimed this very check used it) and `form_admin.go` already called the right one — only the `/my` path degraded, when the checks were folded into that closure. (1) The ladder is now a **pure decision** (`rule_limits_b328.go`: `ruleLimits` / `ruleLimitCounts` / `ExceedReason`) over counts that each have exactly **one** legitimate producer (`countUserFacingForUserDevice(d, userID, deviceID)`, `countUserFacingForUser`, `countAllEnabledRules`), so "the device count" can never silently be the system count again; the unit is pinned to the **measured** composition (454 subnet rows with a `parent_domain` excluded, the 20 kept per-domain rows count, 17 hand-entered subnets + 9 hand-entered domains count — **no `ip`-typed derived rows exist live**, so the old "/32 IP rules" wording was wrong too). (2) **«Распространить на все мои устройства»** for an **already saved** rule (`POST /my/exit-rules/spread`): it marks the intent and runs the **same** `propagateAllDeviceRules` pass the 5-minute tick runs, so the button cannot produce rows the tick would not; it **refuses to invent** a rule whose natural key the caller does not already own, and it is idempotent. This closes the operator's real gap — **33 user-facing rules on `skyworker`, all 33 absent from `cyborg`** (`all_devices` was `0` on all 500 rows: nobody had ever used the option). (3) The same option on `/admin/exit-rules`, which **never called `MarkDeviceRulesAllDevices` at all**; an empty device field resolves to the target user's first device so the ownership/exit-node checks still run against a real one. (4) The **API** now counts the caller's user-facing rules on the device (same unit as the page): it used `db.CountEnabledRulesForDevice`, which counts all **263** enabled rows on `skyworker` while the page showed `33/500` — it would have refused at 500 where the panel said 467 slots remained. (5) The caps are **adjustable from the panel** (`global_settings` row **>** `.env` **>** default, with the **source** shown per level), because under docker the container environment is frozen at **creation**: changing a number meant editing `.env` plus `--force-recreate` (trap #3) for a value the panel was already displaying. Both pages now **display the caps the guard enforces** through the same resolver call — the pre-fix shape was a page quoting 1/500 next to a refusal quoting 500/500. 46 contracts in `scripts/check_b328_rule_limits.sh` plus `rule_limits_b328{,_handler}_test.go`, `spread_b328_test.go` and `limits_settings_b328_test.go`; the handler-level test **reproduces the operator's exact message against the pre-fix code**, which is the regression proof (nothing in the package mentioned `MaxRulesPerDevice` or drove `PostMyExitRule` before this).
- **B327** — (2026-09-25) **a live-state check must SKIP when its state is unavailable, never FAIL — and `B-prod-health` was reddening CI for the operator's deploy instead of for the commit.** Measured: CI run `36165848857` on a **docs-only** commit failed with `FAIL /healthz returned 502 (want 200)` because the operator was updating skygate to v1.5.92 at that moment (nginx up, container recreating), while re-running the **same commit** gave `catalog clean: 376 PASS, 1 SKIP, 0 FAIL`; a red commit also blocks the **next** tag, because `release.yml`'s `preflight` refuses a commit whose CI is not green. `AGENTS.md` rule 1 already said it. `scripts/check_b_prod_health.sh` now classifies in three states: **PASS** on 200; **SKIP** on `000` (no connection at all) and on `502/503/504` (a proxy up while its upstream is unavailable or restarting — not the application answer, therefore not evidence about the commit); **FAIL** on any other `4xx/5xx` (we reached something that answered wrongly) and on a reachable `build=dev` or unparseable `/healthz` body. **A second defect was found on the way:** `http_get_status` used `|| echo "000"` while `curl -w '%{http_code}'` already prints `000` on a transport failure **and** exits non-zero, so every unreachable host produced the six-character status `000000`, classified as an unknown status and therefore a FAIL — the check could not SKIP even where it tried to. Strict mode is **opt-in** and belongs to the pre-deploy flow, which wants an unreachable production RED: `SKYGATE_PROD_REQUIRE_HEALTHY=1` turns the SKIP rows into FAIL (`docs/operations.md` §1.7 — note that **no** automated path invoked this script before, only the CI catalog, where a FAIL was noise, so this removes a false gate rather than a real one). A tolerant run that SKIPped rows prints **INCONCLUSIVE** rather than a clean bill of health, `All 4 mandatory contracts PASS` can no longer be printed after a FAIL, and the script gained a `--classify <status> [strict]` mode so the decision is inspectable without a network. 31 contracts in `scripts/check_b327_prod_health_skip.sh`, including a behavioural half that drives the real script against a **local HTTP stub** (502 → SKIP exit 0; 502 + strict → FAIL exit 1; 200 healthy → all four PASS; 200 + `build=dev` → FAIL; 500 → FAIL; dead port → SKIP, FAIL under strict) and never touches the operator's production host. **Follow-up:** wire `SKYGATE_PROD_REQUIRE_HEALTHY=1` into whatever pre-deploy path the operator uses (there is none today — `scripts/launch_skigate.sh:43` only names the script in a comment).
- **B325.1** — (2026-09-25) **the localization ratchet is at ZERO, and the two holes a metric sweep opens are closed.** B325 froze two counts and let them fall; this block drove both to **0** — 196 RU values translated, 21 hardcoded English text nodes moved into the catalogues, and **every `<select>` in the panel labelled** (the accessibility ratchet went 31 → 0; the last one was a *false positive in the contract*, which read only the first line of a multi-line `<select>` tag, so it counted `admin/devices.html:133`'s continuation-line `aria-label` as missing — B3 now reads the whole tag and its budget is `0`) — and restored the B76/B77/B78 test bodies the v1.3.0 SQLite→PG purge had left as `t.Skip` stubs (**21 test functions across five files** that had been passing vacuously ever since). Paying a metric down opens two holes the metric cannot see, and both are contracts: **an EMPTY translation is not an ASCII one** (blanket-blanking a value lowers the count and turns the gate green while the page renders a blank label), so no RU value may be empty/whitespace-only and the sweep is held up by a floor on Cyrillic-bearing RU values (2872 of 3068 measured; the other 196 are the deliberately byte-identical command/code/protocol values the B325 metric excludes); and **a restored test that never runs is not coverage** (`go test -run` matching nothing exits 0), so the restored tests are checked for existence, for the absence of any `t.Skip` stub, and for ACTUALLY RUNNING (`-list` verifies the filter; a `[no tests to run]` result fails — the B322 C1 class). It also pins the two findings the sweep surfaced: `internal/nodeownership/auto.go`'s nil-DB guard compared the `DBSource` **interface** with nil, so a typed-nil `DBSource` walked past it and the first tick dereferenced a missing pool inside a goroutine started from main — it now asks the shared `db.DBCurrent` accessor (`TestAutoBackfill_NilCurrentDBSourceIsSafe` is the guard); and `normalizeUpdateTarget`'s comment promised that a ref "looking like a SHA/branch" is left alone while the helper only exempts the `v`/`skygate-`/`main`/`HEAD` prefixes (it answers `vdevelop` and `ve2d0b9e` — now pinned by test and described truthfully). 12 contracts in `scripts/check_b325_1_i18n_sweep.sh`. **Also recorded, not fixed here:** `gofmt -l` is not clean over 302 of 835 tracked Go files (a one-commit renormalisation is 38 737 lines) — TD-21; and `verify_pre_deploy.sh` maintains PASS/FAIL counters it never reads, so it exits 0 with any number of FAIL rows and the pre-push hook therefore always allows a push — TD-22. Both live in `docs/ROADMAP.md` with the measured figures.
- **B325** — (2026-09-25) **localization must not regress silently.** Operator report: «по всему проекту страдает локализация — большинство новых описаний не переведено на русский». The measured audit (`docs/i18n-audit.md`) found **311 hardcoded user-visible English strings across 40 templates**, **621 RU values that are not Russian**, **6 keys USED in templates but defined in NEITHER catalogue** — those pages rendered the raw key (`admin.subnets.total_devices`) or a raw status token (`user.subnet.cell_disabled`, a family typo) — and `derp.help_title` declared twice with different values (the admin copy silently overridden by the derp catalogue). `TestCatalogsParity` cannot see any of it: it compares KEY SETS only, so a Russian map full of English passes happily. This block defines the six missing keys, fixes the family typo, drops the shadowed duplicate, translates the **device-registration result page** (44 strings — every new user sees it), `/admin/system_tests` (37), the exit-rules nodes page (14), the module pages (23) and the destructive `confirm()` texts, and turns **142 RU values into real Russian** (61 `ha.*` + 61 `oidc_sync.*` + the rest): the two measured counts fell from 284→196 and 68→21. The contract is a **ratchet** (`scripts/check_b325_i18n_regressions.sh`, 8 contracts): no key used without being defined, no catalogue key declared more than twice, and both counts may only go down. `TestSystemTestsRendersWithLastResults` was renegotiated — it asserted the hardcoded `run #42`, which is exactly what B325 removes.
- **B326** — (2026-09-25) **a wide table must not be clipped off-screen on a phone.** Operator report: «таблицы становятся широкими и большими … некоторые таблицы начинают ломаться и не поддерживают скролл уходя за экран отображения». Measured on the panel: **84 tables across 58 templates, only 19 wrapped in `.table-wrap`**, and `body{overflow-x:hidden}` means there is **no page-level horizontal scrollbar at all** — an unwrapped wide table is not scrollable off-screen, its right-hand columns are silently **clipped and unreachable**; `table{width:100%}` + `th{white-space:nowrap}` inflated the min-content width (one page had 830px of headers alone). The fix is systemic and markup-free under the mobile breakpoint: the **card** becomes the horizontal scroll container (covers every table inside a card, and the table keeps its normal layout — unlike the `table{display:block}` trick, which also drops `width:100%`), the forced `nowrap` on headers is dropped, long tokens (keys, DSNs, UUIDs) wrap, form controls cannot exceed their card, and the hard 3-column create-user grid collapses; the two tables that render outside a card (`admin/modules.html`, `admin/module_detail.html`) got the explicit `.table-wrap`. 13 contracts in `scripts/check_b326_responsive_panel.sh` (incl. every-unwrapped-table-is-inside-a-card and a brace-balance guard on the stylesheet); the full audit is `docs/responsive-audit.md`.
- **B324** — (2026-09-25) **the OIDC banner must say WHAT to put in the env.** Operator report with a screenshot of `/admin/oidc`: «на скрине указано что нехватает параметра в env для корректной работы однако нигде не пишится пример того что в нем должно быть чтобы открыть данный функционал». The banner named the variable (`SKYGATE_OIDC_ISSUER`), the file (`.env`) and the action (restart the container) — but never the **value shape**, and it sent the operator to a restart that under docker does not apply a changed `.env` at all (the container environment is frozen at creation). Both catalogues now carry a copy-paste example (`SKYGATE_OIDC_ISSUER=https://skygate.example.com/oidc`, `CLIENT_ID`, `CLIENT_SECRET`, `REDIRECT_URIS`), the literal-match rule (`issuer mismatch`), a link to the new `/admin/service` control and the restart-vs-recreate distinction (docker needs `--force-recreate`; systemd/OpenRC re-read `EnvironmentFile` on restart). 15 contracts in `scripts/check_b324_oidc_guidance.sh`.
- **B323** — (2026-09-25) **the SERVICE CONTROL block: restarting skygate to apply new env values is a page, not a hidden button.** Operator request: «вопрос по осуществлению контрольного перезапуска сервиса skygate либо в докере либо нативно под системой для применения новых параметров из env явно нужно вынести подобного рода функционал в настройки и в отдельный блок управления состоянием skygate чтобы не искать где он есть сейчас и скорей всего текущая функция отработает только под докером». Both halves of that report were true: the only control was `handleTailscaleRestart`'s `restart_skgate` button on `/admin/tailscale`, and it branched on the container marker ALONE, so an OpenRC/Alpine, Kubernetes or bare-binary install was pushed through `systemctl restart skygate || service skygate restart` and failed with a message that named neither the install kind nor the reason. New `internal/feature/admin/service_control_b323.go` + `internal/handlers/templates/admin/service.html` + `GET|POST /admin/service` (authMW, the `/admin/tailscale` CSRF cookie + `subtle.ConstantTimeCompare` pattern, flash redirects): a PURE `detectInstallKind(installProbe)` over injectable predicates (`/.dockerenv` or `/run/.containerenv` → docker; `KUBERNETES_SERVICE_HOST` or the service-account dir → kubernetes; a `skygate.service` unit or `systemctl` + unit listed → systemd; `/etc/init.d/skygate` + `rc-service` → openrc; else binary/unknown — docker is checked first because a pod is also a container and the container is what the action can address), a state card (kind + WHY, env file + WHERE the path came from, unit/container/compose file, `/healthz`'s build string, uptime from `app.StartedAt`), the exact command line per kind in a `<pre>`, and the env-vs-recreate hint table. Restart per kind: docker `docker compose -p <project> -f <host-compose-file> restart skygate` (falling back to `docker restart skygate` when no compose file is found, rather than offering nothing), systemd `systemctl restart skygate` (or `service skygate restart`), OpenRC `rc-service skygate restart`; kubernetes and bare-binary are REFUSED with their own reason (a pod restart belongs to the Deployment). Recreate is docker-only (`up -d --force-recreate`, the only way a changed `.env` reaches a container — AGENTS trap #3) and both actions run detached (`setsid` + `applySysProcAttr`) with the response flushed first, writing to `/tmp/skygate-restart.log` (appended, so the page shows the last actions). The env path is honest per kind: systemd parses `EnvironmentFile=` from the unit (all four spellings, `-` prefix and quotes included), docker uses `SKYGATE_HOST_REPO_PATH` (fallback `/home/operator/skygate`) + `/.env`, OpenRC `/etc/conf.d/skygate`, k8s "from the Deployment". Sidebar + breadcrumb group it with `/admin/update` (Settings), the Tailscale page cross-links to it while its legacy `restart_skgate` action keeps working and now delegates to the same kind-aware command. 51 contracts in `scripts/check_b323_service_control.sh` + `service_control_b323_test.go` (detection precedence, all env-path kinds, per-kind argv, refusals) + `service_b323_render_test.go` (template renders for docker/k8s/binary, every `service_ctl.*` key in RU+EN).
- **B322** — (2026-09-25) **the guarantee catalog must be able to fail.** Operator report: «необходимо провести ревизию всех тестов B1-320 … после некоторых правок правила перестали работать но предварительные прогоны не помогли определить что они сломались тоже и с тегами было и политикой ACL». Five read-only audit passes over all ≈351 `run_check` entries found **four mechanical classes** that made contracts decorative, and all four are now contracts in `scripts/check_b322_gate_can_fail.sh`: (1) **24 entries (B59–B81, B86, B89, B90) ended `… && bash "$f"; rm -f "$f"`**, so the pipeline status was `rm`'s — always 0 — and the gate read rc==0 as PASS; arming them (`rc=$?; rm -f "$f"; exit $rc`) exposed **21 failing contracts** and **two real product bugs**; (2) **a check that counts `FAIL` and never exits non-zero** — `check_b251.sh` (the reserved `skygate-host` name B320/B321 depend on) plus **33 more scripts**, all armed with a real exit status; (3) **`go test -run <PATTERN>` filters pointing at deleted tests** (`ok … [no tests to run]` exits 0: B5, B21, `B227`, `TestDBSwap`, `pollOnce`, `TestAutoBackfill_*`, `TestBackfill_StrategyD_*`, …), now a contract that every filter matches ≥1 test; (4) **check scripts referenced by NO catalog** (21 of them — Telegram async, device adoption, the Tailscale enable/disable UI, derper-in-docker, the live OIDC probe, prefix-admin …), 20 registered now. `run_check` also **prints a failing check's own FAIL lines before the 20-line window**, which was hiding exactly the late behavioural sections (B315 G7/H1/I2, B317 I2/I3, B273 F–J, B276 D–F, B288 E–H, B289 F, B290 E–G). **The two real bugs:** `internal/mesh/mesh.go` and `internal/subnet/shares.go` used SQLite-only `INSERT OR IGNORE` on shared paths, so on PostgreSQL (verified on PG 15: `ERROR: syntax error at or near "OR"`) **joining a mesh and granting a subnet share failed outright** — both now build their statement through a dialect-aware helper (`meshMemberInsertSQL`/`subnetShareInsertSQL`) with unit tests, and the B60 sweep is back with a branch allowance. All 24 band entries were renegotiated against the current code and re-verified through the gate's own `run_check` (24/24). Full audit, per-slice numbers, the deliberately-open items (B76/B77/B78 lost their unit tests to `t.Skip` stubs; ~200 further weak assertions; the runtime invariants that still have only a grep behind them) and the method: **`docs/gate-regression-power.md`**.
- **B321** — (2026-09-25) **Tailscale must survive every container recreate, and `skygate-host` must be the name the tailnet actually gets.** Operator report: «при каждом обновлении слетает запуск tailscale приходится прожимать каждый раз старт и активным стал skygate-host-1 как skygate tailscale хотя должен быть строго skygate-host». Live recon **after** the operator's reclaim proved the rename itself had worked — node 87 was `given_name=skygate-host`, the client reported `.Self.HostName=skygate-host`, ghost 57 was gone — and that **two independent** defects were behind the report: (1) `docker-compose.yml:111` pins `SKYGATE_TS_AUTHKEY_FILE=/dev/null` and **Docker freezes the environment at container CREATION**, so every update (`--force-recreate`) produced a container whose entrypoint skipped `tailscaled`; the operator's click was the only thing that brought it back, because the decision lived only in a `global_settings` row the **entrypoint cannot see**; (2) `tailscaleHostname()` returned the same frozen `SKYGATE_TS_HOSTNAME=skygate-host-1` pin, so that click ran `tailscale up --hostname=skygate-host-1` and renamed the node straight back to the **v0.33.1.9 placeholder** that B251 matches by **strict equality** — a suffixed client misses every "is this me?" check. B321: `global_settings[tailscale.desired_state]` (`on`/`off`) records the operator's intent (Start/Stop/Enable/Disable write it) and the **process** re-applies it — a boot pass plus a 5-minute tick call `EnsureTailscaleUp`, which starts `tailscaled`, runs `tailscale up` and then **enforces the name on the RUNNING daemon** (`tailscale up --hostname` does not rename an existing node; `tailscale set --hostname` does), idempotent and **never fatal**; the hostname resolves **DB > env > default** and any `skygate-host-<n>` value is **rewritten to the reserved canonical name** instead of being honoured, while the `return "skygate-host"` literal stays in `tailscale.go` so `check_b251` contract C keeps its meaning. Reclaiming the name also removes the infra tag that carried the **previous** name (guarded: online self node, previous-name tags only — live node 87 had kept both `tag:dev-infra-skygate-host` and `tag:dev-infra-skygate-host-1`). **Also fixed here:** `UntagNode` swallowed its own node-list read error and fell through with an empty list, rewriting the tag set to `[tag:private]` — the same defect the 2026-08-10 `AddTag` fix closed in the other direction, made reachable automatically by B321; and `scripts/check_b251.sh` had **no failure counter and no `exit`**, so all 13 of its contracts were decorative (the gate hides a check's output on PASS) — found by the B1–B320 regression-detection audit, and repaired in this block because that file guards the very name B321 had to stop re-applying. 28 contracts in `scripts/check_b321_tailscale_survives_update.sh` + `internal/feature/admin/tailscale_boot_b321_test.go` + `internal/headscale/untag_guard_b321_test.go`. **B321.1 (v1.5.88):** the stale-tag selection is the pure `staleInfraTags(tags, canonical)` — on a node wearing the canonical name, every `tag:dev-infra-<name>` / `tag:infra-<name>` whose name is not canonical is stale, regardless of which epoch produced it (tags outside the infra family are never touched); the autostart tick clears it, so the operator updates into a clean state without a click; and the reclaim action runs the cleanup BEFORE its nothing-to-delete early return (live: node 87 wore BOTH the canonical and the `-1` tag after a successful reclaim, and the button then refused because the ghost was already gone). 31 contracts + `TestStaleInfraTags_B321`.
- **B320** — (2026-09-24) **the canonical tailnet name `skygate-host` must belong to the RUNNING client, not to a dead registration.** Operator report: «Посмотри не переприменился ли skygate-host — он offline и это tailscale клиент при skygate при этом появился skygate-host-1 … у нас явно конфликт так как при skygate устройство с tailscale всегда базово имеет имя skygate-host и skygate-host-1 появляется только у тех что дублируют текущий в кластере». Read-only live recon: headscale held **id=57** `given_name=skygate-host` (internal name `skygate-host-1-1`) `tag:dev-infra-skygate-host` `100.64.0.9` **OFFLINE since 2026-09-21 21:32** with a **different machine key**, while **id=87** `given_name=skygate-host-1` `tag:dev-infra-skygate-host-1` `100.64.0.10` was **ONLINE** and is this container; the client itself reported `.Self.HostName=skygate-host-1`, and `SKYGATE_TS_HOSTNAME=skygate-host-1` is pinned in **both** `.env` and `docker-compose.yml`. So the canonical name is squatted by a ghost and the live client wears the suffix — which is **not** a re-application of anything: it is the **v0.33.1.9 placeholder** that B251 fixed in the code (the default is `skygate-host`, matched by **strict equality** in `isInfraNode`/`isReservedSelfHostname`) but which nobody removed from the operator's files, so every "is this me?" check (infra ownership attribution, colocation sanity, the SSH-source ACL, the self-probes) was keying off the **ghost**. The suffix cascade the operator described is visible in the data: the ghost had to be given the internal name `skygate-host-1-1`, i.e. `skygate-host` **and** `skygate-host-1` were both taken at its registration. B320 adds the detection and the safe half of the repair: `ReservedSelfHostname` is one constant; `selfNameState` reports the live name, the configured name, the **env pin** and the offline ghost (id/name/tag/ip/last-seen); `/admin/tailscale` renders a red banner with both node ids plus a confirmed action that deletes **only** a node that is (a) offline, (b) wearing the canonical name, (c) in the **infra family** (`tag:dev-infra-*` / `tag:infra-*` / the `infra` user) and (d) **not** the live one, then renames the running client (`tailscale set --hostname=skygate-host`) and **says so** when the daemon is down instead of pretending. It deliberately never edits the operator's `.env`/compose — the banner names the pin that would re-apply the suffix on the next restart. 21 contracts in `scripts/check_b320_tailscale_self_name.sh` + `internal/feature/admin/tailscale_selfname_b320_test.go` (the guard table: an ONLINE holder and a foreign tag are never delete targets; and the infra matcher must understand the **real** tag shape `tag:dev-infra-<host>` — a `:infra-` substring check matches **nothing**, which the test caught).
- **B319** — (2026-09-24) **the rules lookup must run on PostgreSQL: an INTEGER compared with a TEXT column.** Operator report: «и также перестали работать правила что случилось? на agent vm». Read-only live recon: on **every** container start, once per device that has a preference, the journal carried `preferred-reconciler: state for michail/basic: rules for michail/basic: ERROR: operator does not exist: integer = text (SQLSTATE 42883)` (and the same for `skyadmin/skyworker`) — so the preferred-exit reconciler could not compute the state for **any device with an ownership row** and skipped it on every tick, i.e. a preferred exit node was never reconciled for those devices. Reproduced by hand against the live PostgreSQL: `device_rules.device_id` is INTEGER, `node_owner_map.node_id` is TEXT, and the lookup ORed `device_id IN (SELECT node_id FROM node_owner_map WHERE user_id = $1 AND hostname = $2)` — PostgreSQL refuses that comparison outright, while **SQLite compares `9` with `'9'` happily**, which is why the whole suite stayed green (`.dsh/skills/skygate-db-dialect-audit` is the class). The same line hid a second defect: `node_owner_map` has **no `user_id` column** (it links a node via `headscale_user_id`/`username`/`tag` — see `internal/db/device_owner_b316.go`), so that predicate silently bound to the **outer** `device_rules.user_id`: it filtered nothing and correlated nothing. The lookup now compares `CAST(device_id AS TEXT) IN (SELECT node_id FROM node_owner_map WHERE hostname = $2)` — valid on both backends, verified live on PG (the same 45 rules come back) — keeps the pre-rename branch, and drops the bogus owner predicate (the outer query already scopes the user, `node_id` is unique, and re-adding the owner here would be wrong because headscale rewrites a tagged node's user to `tagged-devices`). Contracts in `scripts/check_b319_rules_owner_type.sh` (its live-PG half also proves the server still REFUSES the old comparison) + `internal/feature/exit_rules/reconciler_b319_test.go`.
- **B318** — (2026-09-24) **two pages, one daemon, one story: the Tailscale state must read the same on `/admin/tailscale` and `/admin/exit-nodes`.** Operator report: «теперь на странице admin/exit-nodes показывает что skygate не включает tailscale однако он работает если переходить на соответствующую вкладку посмотри в чем дело». Read-only live recon: `/admin/exit-nodes` said «skygate НЕ в tailnet … tailscaled is not running: failed to connect to local tailscaled; it doesn't appear to be running», while `/admin/tailscale` rendered the **ENABLED** branch (green card, clickable Start) because `global_settings[tailscale.auth_key_path]` pointed at `/data/ts/authkey` and that file exists. Both pages were internally honest and both named the wrong thing: the container env carried **`SKYGATE_TS_AUTHKEY_FILE=/dev/null`** (the entrypoint logged «[init] TS_AUTHKEY_FILE not set — Tailscale skipped (non-RF mode)»), a value **frozen at container creation** which a DB override can never change — it only changes what the page and its Start button do; and `data/ts/tailscaled.log1.txt` (previous container instance) recorded `CreateTUN("tailscale0") failed; /dev/net/tun does not exist`. Neither fact was rendered anywhere. New **`internal/tsstate`** (imported by BOTH features — a cycle otherwise) detects the entrypoint's skip conditions (unset / empty / `/dev/null`), whether `/dev/net/tun` exists in this container, and the last error-looking line of the tailscaled log (JSON-lines via logtail, decoded to its `text` field, with routine lines like iptables noise filtered out), and `Explain()` composes one operator sentence — boot-skip first, then the TUN device with its `docker-compose.yml` fix, then the daemon's own last failure. `/admin/tailscale` carries `EnvDisabled`/`DSentinel`/`TunPresent`/`DaemonError`/`NotRunningReason` and renders a warning banner («настроен, но демон НЕ работает» + the reason + the way out) whenever the daemon is down — on the **error** path *and* on the **stopped** path, so the page can never again look enabled with a dead daemon; `/admin/exit-nodes` appends the same explanation to its tailnet reason. The discovery ticker stops burying the journal: an **unchanged** failure is logged *and* audited at most **once an hour** (the live host wrote 288 identical log lines and 288 `cluster.discovery.error` audit rows a day), a success clears the throttle so the next failure is reported at once, and a different error is never suppressed. 23 contracts in `scripts/check_b318_tailscale_state_truth.sh` + `internal/tsstate/tsstate_b318_test.go`.
- **B317** — (2026-09-24) **one region, one verdict; a derpmap document is not a relay.** Operator report with a screenshot of `/admin/derp/dashboard`: «релей что сейчас рядом со skygate не выбирается и не доступен для выбора как основного DERP сервера хотя при этом он должен иметь минимальную задержку». The screenshot said «Рекомендуемый DERP: region_id `%!s(int=901)` (controlplane.tailscale.com)» and showed two identical 901 rows, while the operator's own relay (region 900, ~20 ms) was absent. Read-only live recon on the reference host: `derp_relays` holds **two enabled rows for region 900** — `https://derp.skynas.ru:443` (sort_order 10) and a stale `https://derp.skynas.ru:8443` (sort_order 11, nothing listens) — and `derp_health` is keyed by `region_id` (`derp_health_pkey`), so the two rows competed for ONE verdict: `FetchAllDERPs` collapsed them in a `map[int]DERPInfo` loop where the **last** row in query order won (the dead one), the cron wrote `dial tcp …:8443: connect: connection refused` every 5 minutes, and the dashboard — which shows only healthy+measured rows — hid the local relay and fell through to the public map, while `skygate derp-probe` built its own un-deduplicated list, persisted per row, wrote 20 ms and made the banner **flip between two views of the same table**. The third row was the other half: the legacy `derp.external_urls` derpmap URL (migrated into `derp_relays` as region 901 by `AutoMigrateDerpRelays`) was **dialled as a relay** — the prober measured Tailscale's control plane, filed it "region 901, public, healthy, 117 ms" and let it be recommended; the public map has 28 regions and none is 901 (verified live). Fix: `FetchAllDERPs` keeps **every** enabled own row of a region and returns a deterministic order (own first, then region_id, then url — no map iteration); `FetchOwnDERPs` skips non-relay URLs via the new `urlIsRelayNode` (the same closed path set the derpmap publisher already applies in `admin.isDerpMapURL`, cross-referenced both ways) and names the skip in the journal; `ProbeAll` still **probes** every row but persists the **best per region** through the new pure `BestPerRegion` (healthy beats failed, then lower latency; all-dead keeps the first error so the shown reason is stable); the CLI prints which verdict a multi-row region kept; the dashboard warns about regions with several ENABLED rows and links to `/admin/derp/relays` where the stale row can be disabled; its healthy filter builds a NEW slice instead of filtering in place (`visible = all[:0]` left the full set truncated for the recommendation that reads it); and the banner formats the region id as `%d` instead of `%s` — that is the `%!s(int=901)` in the screenshot. 26 contracts in `scripts/check_b317_derp_relay_truth.sh` + `internal/derphealth/relay_rows_b317_test.go` and `internal/feature/admin/derp_duplicate_rows_b317_test.go`.
- **B316** — (2026-09-24) **the device-to-device mesh must follow the DEVICE, not a username headscale rewrote.** Operator report on the native host `aro`: `workpc` and `homepc` do not ping each other over their tailnet addresses although both belong to ONE user, and the panel showed the contradiction in his own words — «skygate считает эти устройства как у пользователя daniil как и должно быть но во все устройства отображает такую картину по тегам, из-за чего скорей всего и идет конфликт». Measured read-only on **both** hosts: on `aro` `node_owner_map` held `2 workpc` and `3 laptop` as **`tagged-devices`** (with their real tags `tag:dev-daniil-workpc` / `tag:dev-daniil-laptop`) and only `6 homepc` as `daniil`, while the live policy had **0** grants of the form `tag:dev-* → tag:dev-*`; on the agent VM every `node_owner_map` row carries a **real** portal username and the live policy has **13** such grants (6+3+4, one per device) — the same code, different **data**, so the VM worked only by luck. Root cause: headscale rewrites a tagged node's user to the synthetic `tagged-devices` as soon as it wears any tag (B287), and the mesh grouped devices **by that column** (`db.GetPerUserDeviceTags`, a JOIN on `portal_users`), so two of daniil's three devices did not exist for the mesh, he looked like a single-device user, `writePerDeviceGrants` skipped him at `len(userTags) < 2 → continue`, and the tailnet ended up with **no** device-to-device permission at all — silently, because the skip is a bare `continue` (nothing logged, nothing on any page). **Fix, both halves:** (1) *resolve* — `internal/db/device_owner_b316.go` takes the owner from the ownership row, then from the **device's own tag** (B288's `PerDeviceTagUser`), then from the user its `device_rules` were created under; `DeviceTagsForMesh` feeds **both** ACL generators (`GenerateACLLiveFormat` + the plane one) and `MeshTagsByHost` feeds the panel; devices nobody can attribute are **returned and named** (`acl: N device(s) cannot join the device mesh and get NO device-to-device grant: workpc (its ownership row names tagged-devices …) — adopt them on /admin/devices (or fix their tag) to restore it`), so this can never be silent again; (2) *repair* — `db.RepairSentinelDeviceOwners` rewrites rows whose username is the sentinel (or empty) from the same tag, only when the tag parses **and** names an existing portal user, logs one line per change (`workpc: tagged-devices -> daniil (from tag:dev-daniil-workpc)`), is idempotent and runs from the periodic maintenance tick (`nodeownership` tick), so an existing install self-heals and every consumer (mesh, `tagOwners`, the SSH rule sources, the panel column) sees a real owner again; `/admin/devices` stops rendering «—» for a device that *has* a per-device tag. 27 contracts in `scripts/check_b316_device_mesh_ownership.sh` (A6 pins `$N` placeholders — the repair shipped with `?`, a PG syntax error — and G5 pins the gate's registration path case) + `internal/db/device_owner_b316_test.go` and `internal/acl/acl_b316_test.go`, both built on **`aro`'s own inventory** as the fixture.
- **B315** — (2026-09-24) **derper's metrics reach the container through an operator-set endpoint, and `/admin/derp` stops drawing numbers it did not measure.** Operator report on the agent VM: «по DERP все еще не доступно на VM agent он заявляет публичный адрес - адрес локальной машины а не публичный адрес домена derp.example.com, и также висит предупреждение о падаваемых метриках что никак не управляется и не объясняется для администратора». Two independent defects, both measured live. (1) derper serves `/debug/*` to **loopback and tailnet sources only** (upstream `tsweb.AllowDebugAccess`) and answers everything else `403 debug access denied` — the verdict is made on the request's **source** address, so the same request returned `200` with ~6.3 KB from the host's loopback and `403` from the container, and the B296 probe-host knob (which makes probes **reach** the relay) structurally cannot make them **admitted**. The page then drew `0` in four traffic tiles — numbers that look like measurements — beside one banner naming neither a cause nor a fix. New **metrics endpoint**: `internal/derpcfg` gains `derp.debug_url` / `SKYGATE_DERP_DEBUG_URL` (DB override > `.env` > none, re-resolved on **every render** so saving applies with no restart and no container recreate; refusals carry `spaces`/`scheme`/`port`/`invalid` codes); `collectDerpStatus` reads the four rich-metrics paths from it while the liveness probes stay on the **relay** (a bridge would 404 there), and `MetricsEndpoint/Source/Via/Available/ErrCode(Detail)/HTTPStatus/Bytes` make the verdict explicit. `parseDerperVars` now **requires the `derp` block**: the old tail compared an int against zero with a greater-or-equal, true for **any** JSON object, so an empty `{}` still produced `Running: active` next to twelve zeros. `httpGetViaStatus` keeps the status code, because a `502` from a broken bridge must not be reported as a `403` from the relay. (2) The STUN tile names its **vantage point**: with metrics readable it reports derper's own `stun.counter_requests` (`success` + `not_stun`) — what clients experience — and only without them falls back to the B265 UDP round trip from inside the container, whose egress is demonstrably not the clients' path (measured: the container timed out while clients scored the relay fine). (3) The two addresses are separated: `WhiteIPFallback` flags an `egress` answer (the container's **own** outbound address, normally `172.18.0.x`) so the template renders «не удалось определить» **with the reason** instead of printing it under «Публичный IP», and the address skygate itself dials gets its own row with its layer. (4) The bridge **ships inside the binary**: `skygate derp-metrics-proxy` runs on the host, dials the relay's loopback over TLS so derper sees a loopback source, forwards **only** `/debug/`, `/debug/vars`, `/active-conn`, `/all-recent` (a **closed allow-list**, not a prefix match — the upstream also carries the DERP protocol), refuses client sources outside loopback/private/CGNAT ranges even on a `0.0.0.0` bind, answers `/healthz` without touching the relay, and names `--server-name`/`--insecure` when the upstream TLS handshake fails — no new dependency, image or compose edit. The card (`POST /admin/derp/metrics-endpoint`, admin-only, audited) offers save / clear / **test without saving** and prints the exact host commands for both install kinds. (5) It also fixes skygate's own instrument: the STUN probe used a **connected** UDP socket (`net.DialTimeout("udp", …)`), and Linux only delivers a datagram to a connected UDP socket when its **source** matches the peer it was connected to — where the container's path to the relay rewrites the address (the reference host DNATs `:3478` onto the docker bridge gateway) the reply arrives from the rewritten source and the kernel drops it, so the tile said «UDP-проба не прошла» on a relay that answered in 28 ms (same namespace, same instant: unconnected → `REPLY` source `172.18.0.1`; connected → `read udp 172.18.0.3:…->192.168.13.69:3478: i/o timeout`; connected to `172.18.0.1:3478` → `REPLY` 198 µs). The probe now uses an **unconnected** socket and accepts a reply from ANY source, with the fresh 96-bit transaction id (compared byte for byte) as the proof that the datagram is ours — a foreign transaction id is still rejected (pinned by a test) — and reports the answering address when it differs from the address dialled. 54 contracts in `scripts/check_b315_derp_metrics_endpoint.sh` + `internal/derpcfg/derpcfg_b315_test.go`, `internal/derpmetricsproxy/proxy_b315_test.go`, `internal/feature/admin/derp_metrics_b315_test.go`, `internal/feature/admin/derp_stun_b315_test.go`.
- **B314** — (2026-09-23) **the sidebar grouped the way the operator asked.** Verbatim: «Группы по OIDC - явно две страницы, группа по DERP с релеями и здоровьем derp, вынести сертификаты в настройки так как они относяться к настройке самого skygate, группа по deploy кластер highAvailability - так как имеют один смысл по настройке и связаны между собой, группа tailscale headscale headplane так как непосредственно тоже связаны ну и реальные интеграции - это телеграм а сама страница интеграции больше должна называться Сервисы и иметь больше переходных ссылок на остальные сервисы что появились во время разработки и разнесены по группам». The old sidebar had **one** «Integrations» section carrying twelve unrelated pages, so where a page sat told the operator nothing about it. Now **ten** sections: the five that existed (Devices, Access, Health, Data, Settings) plus **Services** (the Integrations page + Telegram, the one real integration), **Providers** (tailscale + headscale + headplane — one stack), **DERP** (the map, the relays **and** the relay health dashboard), **Deployment** (deploy + cluster + HA — one physical cluster) and **OIDC** (exactly its two pages); **certificates moved into Settings** because they configure skygate ITSELF. The Integrations page is now «Сервисы» and carries **15 cross-links** to every other service page (providers, DERP, relays, DERP health, OIDC and its sync, Telegram, certificates, availability, monitoring, deploy, cluster, HA), while the availability page keeps a name of its own («Доступность сервисов») so two pages cannot both be called «Сервисы». Every new section has a title key in `catalog_common.go` (RU+EN) and an `{{if .InSectionX}}open{{end}}` conditional, and `sectionPageSet()` in `handlers.go` matches `layout.html` in **both** directions. **Contract renegotiated in place:** `B96` now expects ten sections, the ten `InSection` names and the twelve title keys, and the Go test `TestB96_*` carries the new expected-section map. 41 contracts in `scripts/check_b314_nav_grouping.sh`.
- **B313** — (2026-09-23) **OIDC is APPLIED from the panel, not copy-pasted.** Operator report on the native host `aro`: «на aro все еще висит в OIDC предложение по исправлению env и появилось поле со скриптом однако никаких автоматической настройки кнопок нет». The panel already held every value (B290/B304) — what it lacked was the **write**: with `ProtectSystem=strict` the skygate unit cannot touch headscale's config and the restart belongs to root.
