B313 reuses the privilege split the policy applier proved (B272.3/B283): the panel stages a **data-only** request and a root-owned `skygate-oidc.path` unit applies it, restarts headscale, verifies and records the verdict where the page reads it. **One renderer**: the `oidc:` block comes from `internal/oidc.RenderHeadscaleBlock`, which `cmd/skygate oidc-export` now delegates to, so the CLI output and the button cannot drift. The request is published **atomically** (temp + rename — a reader seeing half of each document is what crash-looped headscale on the policy path), created **0600** (it carries the client secret), **never sourced** by the helper, refused when empty or not an `oidc:` block, and consumed **only on success**. The applier gains `--from-request` (needs neither the skygate binary nor `oidc-export`), records `ok`/`failed` on **every** exit path (including `die`, with the reason), and `OIDCHelperArmed` is a stat of the applier **and** the path unit — a request staged where nothing consumes it would look like a successful press; when it is not armed the page names the exact command that installs it. `install-common.sh` writes the `skygate-oidc.service`/`.path` pair beside the policy and routes ones, so a normal install has the button. The handler (`POST /admin/oidc/apply-headscale`, admin-only) refuses an incomplete configuration with a named reason (`client_id` keeps its documented `headscale` default), audits the request **without the secret**, and the panel renders the button plus the last verdict with the documented fallback (RU+EN). 42 contracts in `scripts/check_b313_oidc_apply_button.sh` + `internal/oidc/apply_request_b313_test.go` and `internal/feature/admin/oidc_apply_b313_test.go`.
- **B312** — (2026-09-23) **every exit node shows WHERE it sits, and an unreachable owner's prefixes go to the CLOSEST healthy relay.** Operator request: «если доступ никак нельзя восстановить то необходимо компенсировать правила по другим exit node но предлагаю приоритет расставлять на сходный признак расположения по exit node и как отдельная фича в exit node также отображать локацию где расположен exit node». Live case: `karolina` was blocked and its 75 prefixes moved to whichever relay answered first — right for reachability, arbitrary for latency, because nothing in skygate knew that one relay sits in the same country and the other does not. **V077** adds `exit_servers.location_label/location_country/location_lat/location_lon/location_source/location_checked_at` in **both** chains (SQLite via `execSQLiteDDL`; the schema-parity test now expects V77 and checks all six columns), with «unknown» as a first-class answer — missing coordinates are never stored as the real `(0,0)`, a refused lookup is an **error and not a place**, and a manual value is marked and protected **in SQL** (the automatic write refuses to touch a manual row). New `internal/geoloc`: an **optional, offline-safe** lookup — `SKYGATE_GEO_LOOKUP=off` disables it entirely, `SKYGATE_GEO_LOOKUP_URL` points it at the operator's own service, the request is bounded at 4 s, **LAN and tailnet addresses are never geolocated** (a `100.64.x.x` address says nothing about where the relay is) and the result is cached for 30 days behind a 12 h guard between passes. `internal/feature/exit_rules/location_b312.go` refreshes the due relays from the sync path and answers the one question the assignment needs — `NearestHealthyRelay`: **same city → same country → smaller great-circle distance**, and with no shared fact it returns nothing so the engine's own choice stands. `prefixowner` gains `AssignWithPreference`/`ReconcileWithPreference` (`Assign`/`Reconcile` keep their signatures and delegate with no preference, so every existing caller is bit-for-bit unchanged); the preference is consulted **only** for a prefix whose owner is not healthy and a name outside the healthy set is ignored, so it can never pin a prefix to a down relay, never reshuffle a healthy owner and never override a manual pin or an explicit majority. `/admin/exit-nodes` renders the location per relay with its source and a form that sets it (`POST /admin/exit-nodes/location`, admin-only, audited, coordinates validated as a pair with range checks, refusals as translated flashes — never a raw error page), because the automatic lookup needs a public address and a working network and neither is guaranteed. 53 contracts in `scripts/check_b312_exit_location.sh` + `internal/geoloc/geoloc_test.go`, `internal/prefixowner/prefixowner_b312_test.go`, `internal/feature/exit_rules/location_b312_test.go`, `internal/feature/admin/exit_node_location_b312_test.go`.
- **B310** — (2026-09-23) **SSH to an exit node over the tailnet — a first-class transport, and a named failure when it cannot work.** Operator question after `karolina` was blocked («не пингуется»): can skygate reach it over tailscale, and can that be a feature configured at onboarding so a geo-blocked public IP never removes management access. Measured (agent VM): the relay row pointed at the **tailnet** address `100.64.0.2:18022` — the right idea, because that path survives a provider block of the public IP — and it could never work, with nothing saying so: `ip route get 100.64.0.2` answered `100.64.0.2 via 192.168.13.1 dev ens18` (the **LAN gateway**), because the host has no `tailscaled` at all and the container's Tailscale is configured but **disabled** (`SKYGATE_TS_AUTHKEY_FILE=/dev/null`), so `ssh -i /ssh-sync/skygate_sync -p 18022 root@100.64.0.2` ended in `Operation timed out`; headscale showed karolina `online` with **last seen 2026-09-22 07:18**, i.e. its own tunnel was stale too. B292's “use the live Tailscale IP” fallback therefore *looked* like an automatic repair while being structurally impossible. **Fix** (`internal/feature/exit_rules/relay_transport_tailnet_b310.go`): the transport becomes data — `RelayEndpoint` kinds `tailnet`/`public`/`name`, each carrying where it came from — and a **ladder**: `relaySSHEndpoints` orders the candidates (tailnet first when skygate can use it, the operator's `ssh_target` next, the bare node name last; the tailnet candidate is **always kept with a note** when skygate is not on the tailnet, so the failure names it), `ProbeRelayEndpoint` TCP-probes each (3 s) and then the real `ssh` runs, the first that works wins, and the transport is logged and recorded per relay under `relay_apply_via:<relay>` (its own KV key, so B309's error text is never re-parsed) and rendered on `/admin/exit-nodes`. `SkygateTailnetState` answers whether skygate has a usable **kernel** path (an interface carrying a `100.64.0.0/10` address — a userspace-mode tailscaled cannot carry a plain `ssh`) and names every “why not” (`tailscaled is not running`, `NeedsLogin`, `Stopped`, `NoState`, no binary), cached 15 s; the page warns with a link to `/admin/tailscale`. Onboarding hands the new relay skygate's **own public key** (idempotent `authorized_keys` step, only a well-formed OpenSSH key is rendered into a root shell, and the page says which file it came from or that it could not be read), so a relay registered from the panel is manageable over the tailnet from its first sync. The operator's target, the local transport, B292's persistence and the prefix assignment are all unchanged. Renegotiated in place: B300 A2 counts the single `SetAdvertisedRoutes` call site across the tail **plus** the ladder, B300 B5 pins the new outcome shape (`ssh=ok via <transport>` / `ssh=err=<every candidate>`), B309 D1 follows the loader rename to `fillTransportState`. 38 contracts in `scripts/check_b310_tailnet_exit_transport.sh` + `internal/feature/exit_rules/relay_transport_tailnet_b310_test.go` and `internal/feature/admin/exit_node_register_b266_test.go` (the onboarding key step).
- **B309** — (2026-09-23) **a relay that headscale calls online but skygate cannot CONFIGURE must not keep owning prefixes — the second cause of the permanent «нет маршрута» rows.** Operator report (same message as B308): `/admin/exit-nodes` carried «владелец не объявляет: 29» and a table full of «нет маршрута» on both hosts, and «Пересобрать и применить ACL» never helped. Measured (agent VM): the owner relay `karolina` **was** online in headscale — so `exit_node_health` called it healthy and the B275 healthy set kept it — while every route application to it died: `staggeredSync(aggregated): karolina advertising 114 unique routes` → `exit-node sync(karolina): not a local relay (local daemon unreadable) — using the SSH transport` → `staggeredSync(aggregated): karolina SSH err: ssh root@100.64.0.2:18022 … Operation timed out`, next to `emilia advertised: ssh=ok approved=1`. So `prefix_owner` kept 75 prefixes on a relay that could never advertise them and the ACL pinned `via=karolina` — and no ACL write can repair that, because re-applying a policy does not configure an unreachable host. **Fix** (`internal/feature/exit_rules/relay_transport_b309.go`): every route application records its outcome per relay in `global_settings` under `relay_apply_state:<relay>` (`<unix>|ok` / `<unix>|err|<reason>` — no migration, both dialects), **both** transports count (`ssh=err=`, `local=err=`) and an approval failure counts too (routes headscale refuses to approve are not served either); a relay whose LAST application failed within `RelayApplyFailureWindow` (15m = three ticks) is removed by `healthyExitRelaysForAssignment` from the set `prefixowner.Assign` receives (B275 already implements "an unhealthy owner loses the prefix"), so the prefixes move to a relay that answers and B276's ACL pin follows in the same pass; the exclusion is logged once per pass with the reason and the age (so the journal explains the move). One successful apply clears the record — a recovered relay returns by itself, nothing to un-block by hand — and an unreadable record is read as never-recorded, never as a failure. The exit-node **health** table is deliberately untouched (a relay can be a healthy tailnet node and still be unconfigurable; writing it would re-create B273's lie in the other direction). `/admin/exit-nodes` renders a banner naming each excluded relay, its error, the age and how many prefixes it held, and says outright that an ACL re-apply cannot fix it (RU+EN). 21 contracts in `scripts/check_b309_relay_transport_demotion.sh` + `internal/feature/exit_rules/relay_transport_b309_test.go` (the live sequence against a migrated SQLite: the unreachable relay loses the prefix to the reachable one while the health table stays green, one success brings it back, an expired failure does not demote).
- **B308** — (2026-09-23) **a domain rule is re-resolved once per interval — the permanent «политика УСТАРЕЛА» banner and the endless policy rewrites were our own DNS churn.** Operator report: `/admin/exit-nodes` kept showing the red stale-policy banner on `aro` **and** the agent VM although nothing had been changed by hand, the policy was rewritten over and over, and the agent VM's prefix table stayed «problematic». Measured (agent VM): **39 acl-drift lines in two hours**,每 five minutes `auto-updater tick changed 34..54 rule(s) (added=17..32 removed=17..32) — deferring (throttle 30m0s)`, then `acl-drift: ACL re-applied (snapshot v1655, generated=50420 bytes)` and the drift immediately returned. The rules were not the operator's: `DomainAutoUpdater` re-resolved **every** domain rule on **every** tick, and a domain whose A records rotate (`ghcr.io` 29 rows, `quay.io` 17, `minimax.io` 33) returns a different IP set each time, so the derived `/32` rows were deleted and re-inserted forever — the generated ACL never stopped changing, the banner was effectively permanent, and on a `policy.mode: file` host every throttled re-apply restarted headscale (B298's 30-minute throttle deferred the *apply*, not the churn). **Fix** (`internal/feature/exit_rules/domain_interval_b308.go`): a per-domain minimum re-resolve interval — `global_settings.dns_domain_resolve_interval_sec`, default **6h**, floor 5m, ceiling 7d, `0` = every tick (the pre-B308 behaviour); the last successful resolve is recorded per domain under `domain_resolve_at:<domain>`; a never-resolved domain is **always due** (a fresh rule is never starved); a failed lookup is deliberately **not** marked (an unreachable resolver is retried next tick); a backwards clock resolves rather than parking the domain; both paths (CDN + per-IP) mark only after success and the skip is logged (`auto-updater: N domain(s) skipped (re-resolve interval …)`). The interval is editable from `/admin/system_tests` (`POST /admin/system_tests/dns-interval`, next to the autoupdater toggle) through the **same key** the updater reads, and the stored value is the **clamped** one so the page can never show a number the runtime ignores. 21 contracts in `scripts/check_b308_domain_resolve_interval.sh` + `internal/feature/exit_rules/domain_interval_b308_test.go` + `internal/feature/admin/settings_dns_interval_b308_test.go`. **Still open from the same report:** `karolina` unreachable over SSH (`ssh root@100.64.0.2:18022 … Operation timed out` every tick) leaves 29 prefixes unadvertised — the plan is to demote an unreachable prefix owner to a healthy relay; plus the OIDC auto-configure buttons and the navigation/«Сервисы» regrouping.
- **B307** — (2026-09-23) **the STUN probe must send FINGERPRINT — the red tile was OUR false negative, not a derper defect.** Corrects B302's conclusion. `/admin/derp` showed "STUN UDP :3478 closed" while the relay's STUN answered real clients (`tailscale netcheck` scored every public region through it), and rebuilding derper from upstream changed nothing (fresh `v1.70.0` and `1.102.4`, and a non-standard `--stun-port 23456`, all behaved identically) — which pointed the investigation back at the probe. The relay's OWN counters (read from `/debug/vars` over loopback, which `tsweb.AllowDebugAccess` admits) settled it: `{"not_stun":20,"success":0}` → four bare RFC 5389 Binding Requests → `{"not_stun":24,"success":0}` → **one request carrying SOFTWARE+FINGERPRINT → `{"not_stun":25,"success":1}`**, and a python probe with those bytes got `REPLY 44 bytes (txid match=True)` from `127.0.0.1:3478` while the bare form stayed silent. The source says why: `tailscale.com/net/stunserver` parses with `stun.ParseBindingRequest`, which returns `ErrNoFingerprint` ("STUN request didn't end in fingerprint") / `ErrWrongFingerprint` and counts both as `not_stun` — it never answers a header-only request, and Tailscale's own client (`net/stun.Request`) always sends SOFTWARE (`"tailnode"`) + FINGERPRINT (CRC32 of the message so far, XOR `0x5354554e`, with the header length already counting the fingerprint). **Fix:** `buildSTUNBindingRequest` now emits exactly that shape, `buildSTUNBareBindingRequest` keeps the old header-only form as a documented **fallback** for a generic RFC 5389 server, `probeSTUNUDP` tries the fingerprint shape first and retries with the bare one, and a failure names BOTH attempts (`stun host:port: fingerprint form: …; bare form: …`) so a red tile can never hide which packet was sent. The B265 wire-format test is renegotiated in place (it pinned the 20-byte shape). 14 contracts in `scripts/check_b307_stun_fingerprint.sh` + `internal/feature/admin/derp_stun_b307_test.go` — including a local UDP server that implements derper's exact rule (reject without a valid fingerprint) and must answer our probe, a pedantic generic server that pins the fallback, and the both-shapes error.
- **B306** — (2026-09-23) **the project's modules are first-class system tests.** Second half of the operator's request («расширить [тесты системы] давая возможность полностью контролировать модули проекта и получать уведомления по неисправности или некорректном поведении»); B305 is the notification sink. Before: the catalogue was a static list of in-process checks and the modules lived on their own page — nothing asked "is this module actually working?", and `StateError` was a badge elsewhere. Now `Service.ModuleTests()` generates one `SystemTestDef` per registered module from the live `Manager` (`module.<name>`, category `modules`), `AllTests()` = `TestRegistry` + those, and the page, `RunAllTests` (one-line change to `for _, t := range s.AllTests()`) and the per-module button all use it. `runModuleCheck` is the pure ladder: not installed → **SKIP** (a fresh install must not be red), `StateError` → FAIL naming the state, running-but-unhealthy → FAIL, stopped → SKIP, nil module → SKIP (no panic); the output always carries state, started time, sorted `Health().Checks`, sorted `Info` keys and `LastError`. Per-module control is `case "test"` in the existing `/admin/modules` dispatcher (`RunModuleTestAction` → `RunModuleTests` → `PersistRun` → flash), offered via `actionsForState` in EVERY state and mirrored by a module section on `/admin/system_tests` (`ModuleTestRows`); i18n `modules.test` / `modules.test_help` / `modules.test_section` RU+EN. Notifications: `ReportRunToMonitor` routes a `module.<name>` test to source `module:<name>` with fingerprint `module:<name>:health` and link `/admin/modules/<name>`, and a PASS resolves it — one notification per module fault, not per run; in-process checks keep the `system_test` source. 23 contracts in `scripts/check_b306_module_tests.sh` + `internal/feature/admin/system_tests_modules_b306_test.go`.
- **B305** — (2026-09-23) **one monitoring inbox for every skygate signal.** Operator ask: «поле с уведомлениями куда будут приходить все сообщения разной важности с мониторинга skygate» + notifications about faults/incorrect behaviour. Before this block every signal lived somewhere else and most were fire-and-forget (exit-node health → Telegram and gone; tag-reconcile failure → metric + audit row; a failed system test → rendered once; DB health → one page's badge), so nothing answered "what is wrong right now, and is it new?". **v0.76 `monitor_events`** in BOTH chains (SQLite via `execSQLiteDDL`, both drivers registered) with a **UNIQUE fingerprint** as the dedup key: `ReportMonitorEvent` upserts (`ON CONFLICT(fingerprint) DO UPDATE`, `repeats = repeats + 1`, `last_seen` refreshed, `state` back to `open`, `resolved_at = 0`) and returns whether the row is NEW/reopened; `ResolveMonitorEvent` closes a condition without deleting history; `AckMonitorEvent`/`AckAllOpenMonitorEvents` set `state='acked'` (so a known problem leaves the unhandled count but stays visible); `ListMonitorEvents`/`CountMonitorEventsByState`/`CountOpenMonitorEventsNotLowerThan` serve the page. **`internal/monitorinbox`** holds the policy: four normalised severities (`Normalize` maps warn/fatal/panic spellings, `SeverityRank`, `LevelsAtLeast`), a dedup-aware `Report` that only pages when `created && rank >= minNotify` (a repeat of a known problem must not re-page), `Resolve`, `FormatAlert`, and the threshold read from `global_settings` key `monitor.notify_min_severity` (default `error`, DB `DBSource` per call so an HA swap is followed) — the operator can move it to `warning` or `critical` with no restart and no env edit. **`/admin/monitor`** (routes `GET /admin/monitor`, `POST /admin/monitor/{id}/ack`, `/ack-all`, `/settings`, all under `authMW`) renders state/min-severity/source filters, per-state counters, per-row Ack + Ack-all, the threshold form and the fingerprint, sits in the sidebar's health section (`InSectionHealth` + `monitor.nav`), is translated RU+EN and answers refusals with a flash (`monitorDBHandle()` makes it survive a Service without a DB). **Producers:** `ReportRunToMonitor` in `PersistRun` — a FAIL opens an `error` event (`system_test:<name>`, category + truncated output), a PASS **resolves** it (so the list shows what is broken NOW), a SKIP is ignored — and `monitorTagAlertSink` wraps the tag reconciler's `AlertSink` so a dev-tag that never reached headscale also becomes an operator-visible event (one folded fingerprint, not one row per failure text). 46 contracts in `scripts/check_b305_monitor_inbox.sh` + `internal/db/monitor_events_b305_test.go`, `internal/monitorinbox/monitorinbox_b305_test.go`, `internal/feature/admin/monitor_b305_test.go`. **Next (B306):** per-module control of the test catalogue (the other half of the same request).
- **B304** — (2026-09-23) **OIDC fully controllable from the panel — no env edit, no restart.** Operator report from the native host `aro`: the OIDC page still warned that the configuration «требуется изменение в env и из вебинтерфейса это никак не изменить», plus the ask for env autofill, absolute key-dir paths with owner/mode checks, and a script that deploys the headscale side automatically. **Two real causes.** (1) **`key_dir` was a form field that applied to nothing**: the live applier (B290) pushed only `issuer`/`client_id`/`client_secret`/`redirect_uris`, so a new directory took effect — if ever — on the next restart. The signing store is now movable at runtime (`oidc.KeyStore.Reload`, one shared `loadOrGenerateKey` path with boot, the new key loaded **before** the swap so a bad path leaves the running key and `/oidc/jwks.json` untouched, `loadPrivateKeyFromDisk` reusing the ≥2048-bit refusal), readers go through the guarded `KeysRef()`/`SetKeys()` accessors (a runtime repair cannot race the request path), a relative path is refused at the store level (B270's live failure was `./data/oidc-keys` resolved against a systemd CWD), and `/admin/oidc` renders the **resolved** path, owner (uid/gid + name, `oidcStatOwner` split per platform), mode, kernel-answered writability, the active `kid`, and a copy-paste fix for each failure mode (`relative_path`/`missing`/`unwritable`/`world_readable`/`unavailable`). (2) **`/admin/oidc/sync` read the RAW env** and refused with «SKYGATE_OIDC_ISSUER is not set on the skygate container» even for an operator who had saved everything on `/admin/oidc`; it resolves the effective configuration now (the saved row wins) and its refusals name the panel field. **Added as asked:** the page renders the exact `.env` block (secret never included — it points at `skygate oidc-export --secret`, a new CLI subcommand that prints the effective values, including the secret, on the host only) and one copy-paste command pinned to this build that runs `deploy/skygate-apply-oidc.sh` — which reads the values from skygate itself, backs headscale's config up under a **unique** name (two runs in the same second used to overwrite the only clean backup and made `--rollback` a silent no-op), writes the `oidc:` block between managed markers, restarts headscale (systemd/docker/none), rolls back on a failed restart, refuses to clobber an `oidc:` section it did not write, and never edits HuJSON by string surgery (`--dry-run` first). 34 contracts in `scripts/check_b304_oidc_panel.sh` — including a real run of that script against a temporary config with a stub binary — plus `internal/oidc/keys_b304_test.go` and `internal/feature/admin/oidc_b304_test.go`.
- **B303** — (2026-09-23) **an ownerless device must have a working admin path, and no action on `/admin/devices` may answer with a raw error page.** Operator report (screenshot): pressing **Transfer** on a device that "turned out to belong to nobody" opened a separate page whose entire body was `node not in node_owner_map: db: node_owner_map: no row`, and a device "not bound by a tag" could not be tagged by the admin at all — «словно проигнорировано для тех скриптов что должны проверять и добавлять устройства». **One deadlock, three pieces, all in `node_owner_map`:** (1) `PostAdminDeviceTransfer` read the CURRENT row first and refused when it was missing — the one button that exists to give a node an owner demanded that it already had one, and it answered with `http.Error` (a text/plain page with no navigation that leaked raw DB text); (2) `PostAdminNodeTag` recorded ownership only when headscale named a user **and** the row already existed (the synthetic `tagged-devices` branch ran `UpdateNodeOwnerTag` — an UPDATE, which matches no row), so the tag landed in headscale while skygate stayed blind: every per-device ACL rule missed the node and the next Transfer click hit (1); (3) `findAdoptionCandidates`/`classifyNodeForAdoption` dropped ownerless nodes entirely (empty `UserName`, no `portal_users` row, synthetic `tagged-devices`), so the adoption card offered nothing for them either. **Fix:** a missing row IS the adoption — `errors.Is(err, db.ErrNodeOwnerNotFound)` becomes an empty current owner, the transfer proceeds, the audit says `device_transfer_adopted_ownerless` (an adoption is now distinguishable from a reassignment) and the new row is stamped with the live hostname via `SetNodeOwnerHostnameIfEmpty` (B272.5); **Tag always persists ownership** (missing row → `InsertIgnoreNodeOwnerWithHostname`, existing row → tag-bump UPDATE so hostname/os/device_type survive, both audited) and its live node read is mandatory, so the per-user-device exit-node guard can never again run against an empty tag list; every operator-facing refusal in both handlers is a **303 flash on `/admin/devices`** carrying a named reason (shared `devicesFlashErr` helper; the 403 for a non-admin caller stays the only `http.Error`), so a real DB error and "no row yet" can no longer be confused for each other — the class the loaded skill `skygate-error-ux-migration` exists for. The adoption card renders an explicit **owner dropdown** for rows no portal user can be inferred from (`NeedsOwnerPick`, new RU+EN labels), and an ownerless node can also simply be transferred. **Renegotiated in place:** `scripts/check_b257_adopt_devices.sh` contract F now pins the two former skip rules as owner-pick candidates (`TestClassifyNodeForAdoption_EmptyUserNameBecomesOwnerPick_B303`, `..._OrphanHeadscaleUserBecomesOwnerPick_B303`). 29 contracts in `scripts/check_b303_ownerless_device.sh` + `internal/feature/admin/devices_b303_test.go` (the real handlers against in-memory SQLite and a fake headscale REST server: ownerless transfer adopts + stamps the hostname, four refusal shapes never leak the raw text, unreachable headscale is named, tag-without-a-row creates the row for both ownerless shapes, an unreadable headscale refuses instead of tagging blind).
- **B302** — (2026-09-23) **every `/admin/derp` probe must dial the reachable ADDRESS and speak the HOSTNAME.** Operator screenshot of the agent VM's `/admin/derp`: `DERPER.SERVICE stopped` (red) beside `DERP SOCKET :443 TCP listening` (green), `STUN UDP :3478 closed` and `VERSION v1.70.0 go` — while `docker ps` showed the relay had been **up 39 hours**. Two independent facts came out of the live host: **(1) the service tile lied.** `/etc/hosts` inside the skygate container maps the relay's own public name to `127.0.0.1` (AGENTS trap #2), and `derperLivenessWebSocketProbe` — the probe that decides `Running` whenever `/debug/vars` answers **403**, i.e. on every hardened deployment — dialled that **name**, so it connected to the container's own loopback and failed; its neighbours succeeded because they already pin the address (`httpGetVia`, B289.1), which is exactly why the same page showed a green socket and the version. Proven from the host with the page's own shape: `derp.skynas.ru:443` **and** `127.0.0.1:443`, both with the proper SNI, answer `HTTP/1.1 101 Switching Protocols`. A liveness probe that dials a different address than its neighbours is not a liveness check, it is a second opinion. Fix: the probe takes a `dialAddr`, pins its TCP dial with the same `net.JoinHostPort(dialAddr, port)` shape `httpGetVia` uses, keeps the hostname for `Host`/TLS SNI, and its call site passes the **same** address the other probes use. **(2) The STUN tile told the truth** — derper **binds `*:3478` and answers no Binding Request at all**: not from the host, not from inside the container, not even on loopback, over IPv4 *or* IPv6, while `derper --help` lists `-stun`/`-stun-port 3478` (both correct in the running argv) and its log says `STUN server listening on [::]:3478`; a UDP loopback echo control proves the probe can receive, and `tailscale netcheck` gets STUN replies from every public region on the same host, so the silence looked derper-side (`skygate-derper:latest`, built 2026-09-17, banner `1.70.0-ERR-BuildInfo`) — reported, not papered over. **That conclusion was WRONG and is corrected by B307 (v1.5.72):** the probe sent a bare RFC 5389 request, which `tailscale.com/net/stunserver` rejects as `not_stun` (`ErrNoFingerprint`) — the relay was healthy all along, and its own counters prove it (4 bare probes → `not_stun` +4, one request with SOFTWARE+FINGERPRINT → `success` +1). The tile now also **names every candidate it probed**, so a UDP filter can be told apart from a relay that never answers. Live map chain checked and correct: headscale's `derp.urls` merges `http://skygate:8080/admin/derp/relays/derpmap.json` and skygate serves region 900 (`derp.skynas.ru:443`, STUN 3478); the stale enabled `:8443` row (`derp_relays` id 3) is dead weight for the operator to clean up on `/admin/derp/relays`. 12 contracts in `scripts/check_b302_derp_probe_dial_truth.sh` + `internal/feature/admin/derp_probe_dial_b302_test.go` (an unresolvable URL host plus a pinned address must still reach the relay; a closed port must still fail; Host/Upgrade headers survive the pinned path).
- **B301** — (2026-09-23) **a sudo refusal that can never succeed must fall through to the privileged helper.** Live on `aro`, with B300 already running — so the relay was correctly recognised as **LOCAL** and the ladder *was* walked: `staggeredSync(aggregated): exit-node-vps applied LOCALLY: local=err=tailscale set (local, sudo): sudo: The "no new privileges" flag is set, which prevents sudo from running as root. … — no SSH involved`. `deploy/install-common.sh` writes **`NoNewPrivileges=yes`** into the skygate systemd unit, so sudo can **never** become root from inside skygate (the kernel flag is checked before any sudoers rule, which is why option «c» in `RoutesFallbackHint` — a NOPASSWD rule — changes nothing on a systemd install). That phrase was missing from `isPrivilegeRefusal`, so the refusal was classified as a **real** `tailscale set` failure and `ApplyRoutesLocally` returned one rung early: the root-owned helper sat **installed and idle**, `routes-apply.status` was never created and every prefix stayed «нет маршрута» — the v1.5.65 journal line is the proof that rungs 1 and 2 ran and rung 3 was skipped. Fix: the systemd refusal (`no new privileges`, `prevents sudo from running as root`) plus the other sudo-only refusals (`not allowed to execute`, `no tty present`, `sorry, user`) are privilege refusals, so the ladder reaches rung 3; a **real** tailscale failure (unknown flag, bad CIDR, daemon down) is still surfaced as-is instead of being masked by a retry on another rung, and that negative case is pinned too. `RoutesFallbackHint` now warns that option (c) cannot work on a systemd install, so the next reader does not chase it. 10 contracts in `scripts/check_b301_sudo_nnp_rung.sh` + `internal/headscale/local_apply_b301_test.go` (the verbatim two-line live refusal drives the ladder to the helper).
- **B300** — (2026-09-23) **one transport decision for every sync path.** Live on `aro`, whose journal showed the per-node fixes were simply not on the path that runs: `staggeredSync(aggregated): exit-node-vps SSH err: ssh exit-node-vps (target from the node name (exit_servers has neither ssh_target nor tailscale_ip for this relay), key /var/lib/skygate/ssh/id_ed25519): ssh: Could not resolve hostname exit-node-vps` — while the host itself proved the relay **is** local: `tailscale status --json` reports `Self exit-node-vps ['100.64.0.1','fd7a:115c:a1e0::1']`, `headscale nodes list` lists node id 1 `exit-node-vps` with exactly those addresses, and the `exit_servers` row is `('exit-node-vps','','')`. **Two paths configure the same relay** — the per-node one (`syncOneExitNode`: `SyncAdvertisedRoutes`, `SyncAdvertisedRoutesForNode`, the per-row Re-sync button) and the aggregated one (`staggeredSync(aggregated)`, which is what the periodic tick and the domain auto-updater actually run) — and the aggregated path carried its own **shorter copy** of the body: it never called `DetectRelayPlacement` (B293) and never ran B292's target repair, so every tick handed `SetAdvertisedRoutes` an **empty** target, ssh fell back to the bare node name, and the local transport the evidence proves would match was never even considered (`routes-apply.status`/`.log` were never created; every prefix stayed «нет маршрута»). Fix: both call sites go through **one shared tail**, `applyRoutesToRelay` (`internal/feature/exit_rules/sync.go`) — B293's evidence chain (live daemon → local interfaces; a negative daemon answer is final) decides locality; **local** → refuse to advertise a subnet this host sits inside (`SelfCoveringRoutes`) and apply through the privilege ladder (direct → `sudo -n` → the root-owned helper); **remote** → resolve the target by B292's chain (operator override → the live Tailscale IP, persisted into the still-empty column → a NAMED warning) and run SSH; then approve through the same single call site, whichever transport ran. There is now **exactly one** `SetAdvertisedRoutes` and **one** `ApproveAllRoutesWithList` call in the sync package, and a readable daemon that answers "not local" **logs its evidence** instead of leaving «why ssh?» unanswered. 17 contracts in `scripts/check_b300_relay_apply_one_path.sh`; no contract needed renegotiating (B132's `syncOneExitNode` + call sites, B274's `syncOneExitNode(… OwnedPrefixes …)` call text and B293's `DetectRelayPlacement`/`ApplyRoutesLocally` greps all still hold, which the new script asserts as D1).
- **B298** — (2026-09-23) **derived-rule churn must not restart the control plane.** Live on the native host `aro`, every five minutes, forever: `acl-drift: ACL re-applied (snapshot v483, generated=7331 bytes) — auto-updater tick changed 18 rule(s) (added=17 removed=1)` immediately followed by `auto-updater: dedup removed 16 redundant derived rule row(s)`. On a `policy.mode: file` host an ACL re-apply **is** `systemctl restart headscale`, so that pair was **288 control-plane restarts a day** — and the operator's own DB dump proved it: 19 subnet rows for 19 distinct CIDRs with `openai.com` carried under **two** `parent_domain` values (`cdn:cloudflare:openai.com` 15 rows + a bare `openai.com` 2 rows). Two independent defects: **(1)** `CollapseDuplicateDerivedRules` (B274) partitioned on the **five** columns that exclude `parent_domain`, while `device_rules_natural_key_uniq` is a **six**-column index (B237.23/V068) that deliberately lets two domains of one CDN keep a row per CIDR each — that per-domain row is exactly what the CDN short-circuit looks for (`parent_domain LIKE 'cdn:%:<domain>'`) and what B184's DOMAIN-status propagation reads, so the collapse deleted the row the losing domain needed, and on the next tick that domain re-resolved, re-inserted its whole published range set (~15 Cloudflare CIDRs) and had it deleted again: an insert→delete ping-pong that changed the generated policy on every tick. The partition now matches the index, so only **EXACT** duplicates are collapsed, a domain's rows survive, its short-circuit fires on the next tick and the cycle is gone. **(2)** The domain auto-updater and the periodic drift check both called `applyACLIfDrifted`, which spent the **60-second** `ownershipACLThrottle` — so a single rotating `/32` from ordinary DNS rotation re-applied the policy; the derived-rule paths now spend a separate `churnACLThrottle` (**30 minutes**) through the new `applyACLIfDriftedChurn`, while operator actions (rule create/delete, explicit resync, an ownership move) keep the 60s budget and still land within a minute, and the deferral log names the budget it actually spent. 17 contracts in `scripts/check_b298_cdn_rule_churn.sh` + `internal/feature/exit_rules/churn_b298_test.go` (real migrated SQLite DB: two domains keep two rows for one CIDR; the churn path defers where an operator action applies; the periodic check spends the same budget) — **contract renegotiations**, each documented in place: B274/D.2 (+D.2b, the five-column partition must be absent), B276/A10, B276.1/C1 and B288/C3. Still open and tracked: the local-relay detection that never matches on `aro` (the sync still tries SSH because `exit_servers` has neither `ssh_target` nor `tailscale_ip`, so the B293 helper ladder is never reached — B300) and the DERP `derp_health`/probe knobs.
- **B297** — (2026-09-23) **the RUNNING headscale's version is read, not declared.** skygate's only answer to «which headscale is this?» was `SKYGATE_HEADSCALE_VERSION_PIN`, an env var the operator types once — `internal/config/config.go` says so out loud («The pin is an env var (not auto-detected) because skygate doesn't shell into the headscale container»). The two live hosts were reported to disagree (`aro` 0.29.0, the agent VM 0.29.3) — and the FIRST live run of this probe (v1.5.63 on `aro`) answered `0.29.2`, i.e. the declaration was wrong in exactly the way the block exists to catch — and 0.29.x is **not** uniform in the surface skygate depends on: `POST /api/v1/node/{id}/approve_routes` left REST at **0.29.1** (`internal/headscale/routes.go`), the REST expire path broke at **0.29.2** (`nodes.go`), `grants[]` replaced `acls[]` at 0.29.0-beta.4 and 0.29.2 is the version that rejects wildcards in `tagOwners` while requiring `ip: ["*"]` (`acl.go`). A stale pin is wrong in three places at once: (1) the update monitor compares the latest GitHub release against it, so «доступна новая версия headscale» fires in whichever direction the pin is wrong; (2) the `headscale_releases.is_breaking` history is computed from it, so patch releases are filed as breaking (or the reverse); (3) nothing said the daemon and the declaration disagree. New `internal/headscale/version_b297.go` probes a **ladder** — authenticated `GET /api/v1/version` → unauthenticated `GET /version` → `headscale version` through the install-kind ladder (`runHeadscaleCLI`: `docker exec` on a container host, the local binary on a native one), the CLI rung bounded from the outside so a hung `docker exec` cannot hold a boot phase or a tick — and records **every failed rung** (`ServerVersion.Tried`/`Reason()`), so an undetected version names what it tried instead of guessing. A **non-2xx answer is a note, never a version** (an error body carries codes and ports a loose parser would happily publish), a named JSON field (`version`/`Version`/`server_version`/…) is validated strictly while free text is searched loosely **only on a short body**, and the CLI parser prefers the **server** line over the client binary's own version. `internal/headscale_version.Monitor` gains `VersionProbe`/`DeclaredPin` + `VersionStatus()`: the detected version wins **everywhere** a version is compared (tick, `IsBreaking`, the alert body, `Snapshot()` for the page **and** the bot), the declaration is kept beside it for display, a **failed** probe keeps the last real detection and records the error — it never silently hands control back to the declaration it exists to distrust — and alerts no longer **require** the pin (a detected host with the pin unset used to sit in observe-only mode forever). The probe runs before the GitHub poll, so an offline host still notices a headscale upgrade. Boot logs `headscale-version: running headscale is X (via …)` plus a named `MISMATCH` line when the declaration disagrees, `/admin/headscale` renders a «Версия запущенного headscale» card — detected, declared, the rung that answered, the timestamp and a semver-aware mismatch banner (`0.29` vs `0.29.0` is **not** a mismatch) — and the old pin-help text was rewritten in RU+EN (`pinned_help` used to say the version is «задаётся через SKYGATE_HEADSCALE_VERSION_PIN»). 43 contracts in `scripts/check_b297_headscale_version_truth.sh` + `internal/headscale/version_b297_test.go`, `internal/headscale_version/monitor_b297_test.go`. The pin stays supported as the **fallback** for a host whose headscale cannot be asked, and the capability ladders are unchanged: skygate still tries rungs rather than branching on a version.
- **B290** — (2026-09-22) **OIDC must be enableable from the UI, without hand-editing `.env`.** Operator report: «нет удобного выставления включения OIDC — пока нет в env строчки нельзя никак настроить, но из описания непонятно что и как добавлять». The `oidc_settings` table + form have existed since v0.75 and the boot path read the row, but three defects made the feature feel absent: (1) saving answered *«OIDC settings saved. Restart skygate (/admin/update) to apply»*, so nothing the operator could observe changed; (2) the page rendered the **env** values, not the effective ones — a saved row was invisible on the page that saved it — and a DB row with `enabled=0` was ignored at boot (only the env off-switch counted); (3) `client_secret` was stored **in the clear**. Fix: `effectiveOIDCSettings` resolves **UI over env** with a per-field `ui`/`env`/`default` source badge and reports the env off-switch; `internal/oidc.Service` gained a lock-guarded `ApplyConfig`/`ConfigSnapshot` (every handler now reads through `Issuer()`/`ClientIDValue()`/…), and `adminSvc.OIDCApplier`/`OIDCStatusFn` (wired in `main.go`) make **Save apply immediately, no restart**; the page shows what the *running* provider holds next to the form; the secret is encrypted at rest with `SKYGATE_SECRET_KEY` behind an `enc:v1:` marker (legacy plaintext rows still read, a wrong key is a **named** error instead of a silently empty secret); `enabled=0` disables the provider at boot **and** live, expressed as an empty issuer so the routes stay mounted and can be switched back on; the form validates the issuer/redirect URIs and refuses to enable without an issuer or a secret (an empty secret field keeps the stored one); and every field carries RU+EN help naming what to put there and which headscale key it must match. 26 contracts in `scripts/check_b290_oidc_ui_enablement.sh` + `internal/feature/admin/oidc_settings_b290_test.go`.
- **B295** — (2026-09-23) **a failed policy READ must not become a blind WRITE (and a headscale restart).** Live on `aro`: the prefix card said «состояние политики неизвестно … `connect: connection refused`» while headscale was listening on exactly that address (`ss -ltnp` showed `headscale pid=117261` on `127.0.0.1:8081`, `systemctl is-active` = active, `listen_addr: 127.0.0.1:8081` and `HEADSCALE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8081` agreed) — the refusal was **transient** — and the code made it permanent: `acl-drift: cannot read the live policy to decide whether a re-apply is needed (…) — applying unconditionally`. On a `policy.mode: file` host a policy write means `systemctl restart headscale` (0.29 re-reads the file only at startup), so **answering a read failure with a write answers a restart with another restart**: the observation fed the outage, the page could never converge, and every prefix stayed «нет маршрута». Fix (three parts): (1) a **transient** read failure (`connection refused` / `actively refused` / `no connection could be made` / `connectex` / i/o timeout / `deadline exceeded` / `timeout` / EOF, in POSIX **and** Windows spellings) is retried with a bounded, injectable delay (`aclReadRetries`/`aclReadRetryDelay`) before any fallback — covering the restart window — while an **HTTP answer** (401/404/500) is deliberately NOT retried, because the daemon is demonstrably up and the policy-file/CLI rungs are the interesting part; (2) when the read still fails, the write decision comes from the **last APPLIED snapshot in `acl_snapshots`** (`db.LastAppliedACLVersion` + `db.GetACLConfig` + `PolicyEquivalent`): generated == snapshot → **no write and no restart**, different → write, absent or unparseable → the pre-B295 blind apply survives but the log says the decision was blind; (3) `/admin/exit-nodes` reports «в синхроне» from that snapshot and **names the source** (`PolicyVia` = "compared with the last APPLIED snapshot vN (headscale did not answer)") instead of «состояние политики неизвестно», while a read failure with no usable snapshot stays a real error. 19 contracts in `scripts/check_b295_no_blind_policy_write.sh` + `internal/headscale/policy_snapshot_b295.go`/`policy_snapshot_b295_test.go`, `internal/feature/admin/exit_nodes_b295_test.go`.
- **B294** — (2026-09-23) **the live-policy read must not need docker, and a failed read must never render as a fact.** Operator report from `/admin/exit-nodes` on `aro`: «состояние политики неизвестно: read live policy: api: Get "http://127.0.0.1:8081/api/v1/policy": dial tcp 127.0.0.1:8081: connect: connection refused; cli: all variants failed» with «владелец не объявляет: 0 · никто не объявляет: 19 · объявляет несколько: 0» and every one of the 19 prefixes showing «АНОНС: нет · нет маршрута» (19 «проблемных»), and the same page on the docker VM «не меняется … таблицы». Three defects behind one screen: (A) the live-policy READ had only two rungs — the API and a **docker-only** `headscale policy get` — so on a native install whose API is unreachable it gave up immediately and answered «cli: all variants failed» about a CLI it never ran, while the policy **FILE** headscale serves in `policy.mode: file` was never read (the WRITE path has used that file since B272); (B) the prefix table's advertisement map is filled from `ListAllNodes()` and the loader **discarded the error**, so an unreachable headscale made every prefix look unadvertised — absence of evidence presented as a negative fact, which no click on that page could change; (C) the unreachable API is a **configuration** class (the address must be reachable from the skygate PROCESS; inside a container `127.0.0.1:<port>` is the container's own loopback, never the host's headscale) and nothing named `HEADSCALE_URL`. Fix: `GetACL` walks **API → policy file (`c.PolicyPath` / `DiscoverPolicyPath`) → headscale CLI through the B267 install-kind ladder** (`docker exec` when docker+container exist, else the local binary, including the legacy `policy show` / `policy` variants) → an error naming **every** rung it tried plus the reachability hint (`HEADSCALE_URL`, the container-loopback trap, a ready `curl` probe), where the hint fires only for a genuine reachability failure (`connection refused` / `actively refused` / `no such host` / timeout, POSIX **and** Windows spellings) and never for a 401/500 from a reachable daemon; the prefix card now carries `LiveReadErr` + `LiveReadHint` so it declares «не удалось прочитать маршруты из headscale — «нет» здесь значит «не удалось спросить»» instead of counting 19 phantom problems (RU+EN). 20 contracts in `scripts/check_b294_headscale_read_truth.sh` + `internal/headscale/acl_read_b294_test.go`, `internal/feature/admin/exit_nodes_b294_test.go`.
- **B293.1** — (2026-09-23) **detecting a local relay must not need daemon privileges.** Live follow-up to B293: right after installing v1.5.57 the operator reported «команда ничего не дала» — the `IS this host` line never appeared and the relay stayed on SSH. Cause: B293's detection asked the live daemon (`tailscale status --json`) and treated "I could not ask" as "fall back to SSH", but the native install runs skygate as an **unprivileged service user** and tailscaled's socket is root-owned unless `--operator` was granted — so the probe failed on permissions and detection never matched. Fix: a second, privilege-free source of evidence — `LocalInterfaceIPs()` reads this machine's own addresses straight from the kernel (`net.InterfaceAddrs`, loopback/link-local excluded), because a tailnet address bound on a local interface is the same proof as the daemon's own answer; `DetectRelayPlacement()` is now the evidence chain **daemon → interfaces → not local**, where a NEGATIVE daemon answer is final (it knows its own address) and the fallback runs only when the daemon could not be asked at all, carrying `Evidence` / `MatchedIP` / `SelfIPs` / `DaemonErr` so the log names what proved it and why the daemon was unreadable; the pages use the same chain (`LocalSelfIPs`) so the «локальный узел» badge and the corrected Telegram egress advice appear for an unprivileged service user too, and the self-covering-route loop guard is fed the interface-derived list. Division of labour: **detection** needs no privileges, **applying** still needs root / `--operator` / `NOPASSWD` / the root-owned helper (B293's ladder). 56 contracts in `scripts/check_b293_local_exit_node.sh` (section I) + `TestDetectRelayPlacement_B293_1`, `TestLocalInterfaceIPs_B293_1`.
- **B293** — (2026-09-23) **an exit node that IS the skygate host is managed LOCALLY, and the transport is chosen from evidence.** Operator clarification of the B292 report: «тот exit-node что существует он расположен на той же машине что и headscale и skygate … в таком случае доступ как таковой не нужен по ssh», confirmed on the host — `tailscale status --json` reports `Self.HostName=exit-node-vps` with `100.64.0.1` + `fd7a:115c:a1e0::1`, i.e. the local tailscaled IS the relay (headscale + skygate + the exit node in one place, and `telegram.egress_node_id` pointing at it can therefore route nothing). Managing it over SSH (B292's path) meant an SSH session from the host to itself, through the very tailnet it configures: pointless (a key + `authorized_keys` on the same box) and fragile (it fails exactly when the local tailscaled is the thing needing repair). The operator also required the feature to work without privilege («бывает что ставят без [root] … чтобы не было ситуации что нет возможности настроить по причине доступа»). Fix: the decision is **address equality** between the live daemon's `Self.TailscaleIPs` and the relay's headscale addresses (a match is proof; a shared hostname is deliberately not enough — the B265.1 `skygate-host` lesson; "I could not ask" keeps SSH); a local relay is configured with a LOCAL `tailscale set` through a **privilege ladder** — `direct` (root, or the daemon's `--operator` user) → `sudo -n` → a data-only `<update_dir>/routes.request.props` consumed by the new root-owned `deploy/skygate-apply-routes.sh` (`skygate-routes.path`/`.service`, written by `install-common.sh`, re-installable via `deploy/install-routes-helper.sh`, the same split as the policy helper) → a NAMED error listing all four ways to grant access (`RoutesFallbackHint`); only a **privilege refusal** falls through (a real `tailscale set` error is surfaced as-is) and a staged-but-unconsumed request is an **error**, not success (the B288.1 lesson); the sync also refuses to advertise a subnet this host sits INSIDE — the documented "advertise your own LAN" loop, base routes exempt — and reports what it skipped; remote relays keep the unchanged SSH path; `/admin/exit-nodes` marks a local relay with a «локальный узел» badge, shows the rung the next sync will use and **suppresses** B292's missing-key warning for it (no permanent false alarm); and `/admin/telegram` stops folding the two co-location shapes together — a relay that IS this host cannot be an egress detour (its way out is skygate's own), while the `api.telegram.org` probe becomes **representative** instead of untrustworthy. 48 contracts in `scripts/check_b293_local_exit_node.sh` + `internal/headscale/local_node_b293.go`/`local_apply_b293.go` and `internal/headscale/local_node_b293_test.go`, `local_apply_b293_test.go`, `internal/feature/admin/exit_nodes_b293_test.go`.
- **B292** — (2026-09-23) **the exit-node SSH sync must name its blocker instead of echoing `ssh`.** Operator report from `/admin/exit-nodes` («он даёт ошибку при пересинхронизации»), in the GREEN flash box: `Sync exit-node-vps: ssh=err=ssh exit-node-vps (key /ssh-sync/id_ed25519): Warning: Identity file /ssh-sync/id_ed25519 not accessible: No such file or directory. ssh: Could not resolve hostname exit-node-vps: Name or service not known approved=21` — on a page whose only relay showed «1/1 здоровых», «2 маршрутов» and «mismatch: have 2, want 19». Three defects in that line: (A) `/ssh-sync/id_ed25519` is the **container** default (docker-compose binds the operator's `~/.ssh` there) and cannot exist on a native install, and the `ssh` warning was the entire explanation; (B) `Could not resolve hostname exit-node-vps` means the SSH target was the bare node NAME — `exit_servers` had neither `ssh_target` nor `tailscale_ip`, the discovery pass writes `INSERT OR IGNORE` (so an empty column stays empty forever), while the page displayed the relay's Tailscale IP (`100.64.0.1`) read from headscale, and the «Use Tailscale IP» button resolves through the same empty chain — there was **no in-UI way out**; (C) the per-row Re-sync handler always redirected with `?ok=`, so a failed SSH sync rendered as success. Fix: new `internal/headscale/ssh_key.go` (`SSHKeyProblem` / `SSHKeyFixHint` / `SSHKeyState` / `SSHKeyStateNeedsOperator`) preflights empty / non-absolute (POSIX-aware, since `filepath.IsAbs` calls the container default relative on Windows) / not-found / directory / unreadable and explains the container default, and `SetAdvertisedRoutes` refuses **before** spawning `ssh`, appending the target's origin (`targetSource`) so a DNS failure is no longer the whole story; the SSH key default is now resolved **per install kind** (`resolveExitSSHKeyPath`: container → `/ssh-sync/id_ed25519` unchanged, native → `<data dir>/ssh/id_ed25519`, the same `nativeDataDir` anchor B270 used for the OIDC keys) and the installers create `<data_dir>/ssh` (`0700`, service-owned); `syncOneExitNode` resolves the relay from the **live headscale view** when the row has no address, persists it through `db.SetExitServerTailscaleIPIfEmpty` (only into an EMPTY column) so the B81 chain and the button work afterwards, and logs the case where headscale has no address either (`db.FirstTailscaleIP` is the one address-picking rule: IPv4 wins); the per-row Re-sync flash is derived from the result (`exitSyncFailed`: `ssh=err=` / `approve=err=` / `error=` → `err=`, while `ssh=ok approved=0` stays success — it is the normal state of an unapproved relay); and `/admin/exit-nodes` shows the effective key path, a per-row badge whose tooltip carries the reason **and** the fix, plus a banner naming both fields to set (RU+EN). 40 contracts in `scripts/check_b292_exit_ssh_truth.sh` + `internal/headscale/ssh_key_b292_test.go`, `internal/feature/exit_rules/sync_b292_test.go`, `internal/db/exit_servers_b292_test.go`, `internal/feature/admin/exit_nodes_b292_test.go`. **Contract renegotiation:** `scripts/check_b266_exit_node_register.sh` E3 (the absoluteness check moved into the shared preflight) and `TestConfigSSHKeyPath_DefaultChangedForDocker` → `TestConfigSSHKeyPath_DefaultIsPerInstallKind`.
- **B291** — (2026-09-23) **the cluster/HA tree must work on SQLite (it was PostgreSQL-only, therefore dead on a native install).** Live on the native `aro` host: `cluster.discovery.error cluster_node:workpc error="insert discovered node: SQL logic error: near \"['skygate-standby']\": syntax error"` **every 5 minutes** (137 rows ≈ 11 h), and `/admin/cluster` + `/admin/ha` rendered empty. Every write path was PostgreSQL-shaped — `ARRAY['skygate-standby']::text[]`, `'[]'::jsonb`, `'node-' || substr(md5(random()::text), 1, 12)`, `NOW()`, `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, `'skygate' = ANY (roles)`, `roles || ARRAY['skygate']::text[]`, `array_remove(roles, 'skygate')`, `array_to_string(roles, ',')`, `$N::jsonb` — so `cluster_node` never received a row, the elector could not transition a node or recommend a failover, and `skygate cluster failover` could not promote anything. New `internal/db/cluster_sql_b291.go` owns the dialect spellings (`NowExpr`, `NowMinusExpr`, `CastJSON`, `JSONField`, `CastTextArray`, `RandomHexExpr`, `ForUpdateExpr` — PostgreSQL only, SQLite would answer `near "FOR": syntax error` — and `TimeValue`, which binds RFC3339 on SQLite instead of Go's `time.Time.String()` form that `sql.NullTime` refuses and `ParseDBTime` could not decode before B291); role membership and role surgery (promote/demote/drill/CLI) happen in **Go** with exact matching (`RolesContain`/`RolesAdd`/`RolesRemove`, never `LIKE '%skygate%'`, which also matches `skygate-standby`); every timestamp is decoded through `db.ParseDBTime` on read **and** bound through `DialectKind.TimeValue` on write, so all three shapes SQLite can hold render instead of dropping the row; the pending-invite expiry filter and the elector's 5-minute `failover_recommend` dedup moved from SQL into Go (no `NOW()`/`INTERVAL`, no `jsonb ->>`); and a bug that was broken on **both** backends is fixed — the `/admin/ha` event union read `unix_timestamp` and `actor` from `audit_log`, columns that exist in **neither** schema, so half that history was silently missing on PostgreSQL too. 50 contracts in `scripts/check_b291_cluster_sqlite.sh` + `internal/cluster/cluster_sqlite_b291_test.go`, `internal/elector/elector_sqlite_b291_test.go`, `internal/feature/admin/cluster_ha_sqlite_b291_test.go` (all running the real entry points against a migrated in-memory SQLite database).

## 5. Named blocks and terms pinned by contracts

These strings are asserted verbatim by existing check scripts, so they stay in this
file even though the surrounding narrative now lives in `docs/LESSONS.md` and
`docs/ROADMAP.md`.

* **`B-derper-cert`** — DERP certificate automation: the `derper certmode` setting
  and the cert-sync path (`derp_cert_sync` bookkeeping, `reloadDerper`). Operator
  view: `docs/derp.md` + `docs/https.md`.
* **`B-derp-fix` / `B-bug-fix`** (2026-09-15 DERP/infra batch) — the DERP status
  collection and relay-probe fixes (hardcoded probe URL, TLS vs plain HTTP probe,
  non-deterministic bundled-port lookup, WebSocket liveness fallback). Symptoms:
  `docs/troubleshooting.md`.
* **Standby provisioning** — `skygate init <standby-host>` uses
  `create-standby-preauth` and `SKYGATE_STANDBY_TS_AUTHKEY` (`netfilter-mode=nodivert`
  for the standby's Tailscale). Procedure: `docs/ha.md`, `docs/operations.md`.
* **Registration methods** — both methods are supported and must stay documented:
  the classical pre-auth key flow and the OIDC flow (OIDC devices are matched to
  portal users by Strategy E; see the `tagged-devices` synthetic-user caveat).
* **`B252`** — DERP cert auto-renewal: `derp_cert_sync` table + `StartCertSyncCron`
  (24 h) + `/admin/derp/cert-sync/run`.
* **`B253`** — Telegram probe async: cached probe with stale-while-revalidate
  (`telegramProbeTTLSuccess` 30 s / error 5 min, background refresh).
* **`B255`** — Telegram background poll and "pin nearest exit node" for the relay
  selector.
* **`Phase 3.4`** — HA "Force cluster node failover"; **`Phase 3.6`** — the
  `failover-drill`. Implementation status: `docs/ha.md`.
* **Technical-debt markers kept for the contract scripts:** `TD-15` (backticks
  inside `run_check` descriptions were executed by bash), `TD-16` and `TD-18`
  (missing i18n keys `common.online`, `common.offline`, `cluster.col_actions`,
  `cluster.node_upgrade_help`), plus their follow-ups `TD-18.1` and `TD-18.2`.
  Status of all technical debt: `docs/ROADMAP.md`.
